# Decisions

Newest first. One entry per decision: what, and why. The spec states the result; this keeps the
reasoning and what it replaced.

Entries before 2026-10-20 are in [DECISIONS-to-2026-10-19.md](DECISIONS-to-2026-10-19.md), searched the same way.
- 2026-11-13 (Anita, T-3332): a station with a platform on one track only pays 0.6x the station upkeep (`UPKEEP_ONE_PLATFORM_SHARE`, either figure, at grade 500 to 300 and underground 2,000 to 1,200 real US$ a day): a one way station on double track or a quad pair, and a stop on single track without a passing loop. A two way station and a single-track passing place keep 100%; bus stops, tram stops and ferry piers keep their own figures; build prices unchanged. Her words: "lets maybe lower single platform station upkeep to 60% of a double platform station".
- 2026-11-12 (agent paths, T-3374): the trip explorer between zones shows the inter-city riders' routes whenever any inter-city trip goes by train, no longer pooled with the commutes and everyday outings between the zones (replaces part of T-880/T-1229's call). Zone lines cutting one built-up area (Trenton / Morrisville between New York and Philadelphia) made 5 km hops across the line the "route between the cities". With no inter-city rider at all the routes are still every kind's.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3367): the new game sheet no longer asks the running side (it was too prominent on the first screen); the game takes it from the start's country, a real network's metro, and for Choose on map (or a location outside every zone) from the player's own country by the browser's time zone then language region (`homeCountry`, no new permission prompt), else right. Settings' Trains run on stays the way to change it. Supersedes the sheet pick of T-3297's line below.

- 2026-11-12 (agent t3329, Anita): Map detail (Low/High) is gone and the map is always High; the lighter map's code (`map/lite.ts`, the basemap's lite layer list) is removed and a stored `lighterMap` is dropped. Her reason is that the extra usage is small; the measurement is T-2840's (notes/T-2840.md, 2026-11-09 line above): Low saved no resident memory, JS heap and WASM the same, committed GPU bytes about 470 MB lower on desktop (15% of Chrome's committed total) and about 100 MB on a phone setup, frame times 0-2.5 ms better. The `t2840-*` checks moved to checks/old. The Settings Memory row reads "3.4 GB / 8 GB" and its hover says the per-1,000-stations figure as 0.5 GB (Anita's wording; the cards' estimate fit says 0.65).
- 2026-11-12 (agent schem2, T-3364, T-3362; the orchestrator's call): the schematic draws water only where the ground has it. Past the carried map's edge a cell takes the nearest cell on the map (water only from water of 3,000 square pitches or more), not sea as before (every network sat on a round island); carrying the ground's own sea out past the edge was tried and dropped, since past the masks that sea is itself a guess (it drew Pennsylvania as sea). Guessed land under 40 km across on the ground's far grid is sea (the strip east of Palm Beach).
- 2026-11-12 (agent schem2, T-3345, Anita): bigger city centres: each station's room in the rubber sheet times (median distance to the 4th nearest / its own)^0.8, within 0.6 to 3 ("ok can do bigger centers, looks nice"); the dial stays 0.5. Raising the sheet's `core` instead grows every station alike.
- 2026-11-12 (agent t3329, T-3329, Anita): the game shows its memory two ways, an estimate on each saved-game card (1.8 GB + 0.65 GB per 1,000 stations + 0.5 GB per extra solving worker, fitted on 13 real-network saves) and a live Memory row in Settings (1.15 GB + 1.76 x the demand workers' WASM and heap); the limit is `memBudget` (8 GB, or 0.75 of a smaller reported device, 6 where unreported), red at 85%, and the warning on Open only where the browser reports memory. The estimate is not `memoryCheck`'s: that one stays cautious (it judges by zones and blocks what no worker can hold).
- 2026-11-12 (agent t3357, T-3357, Anita): the pill is out of the line shapes a player can pick ("its just a long circle ... it feels a bit out of place"); T-3319's four become three (circle, square, diamond). The ferry's default stays the 1.5-wide pill, drawn as before, so the shape type still has "pill" but only `MODE_SHAPES.boat` holds it; a saved line shape or Settings mode shape of "pill" no longer validates and falls back to the mode's default. Lie: the Ferry row in Settings and a ferry line's editor light no button while the default is on, and picking a shape there cannot be undone back to the pill (the editor's mode-default hover does not apply). "Round" for "around" is out of the English text too (Anita: "a pet peeve of mine"), wiki and docs included; DECISIONS and ARCHIVE history is left as written. The running-side switch has no hover now.
- 2026-11-12 (agent t3357, T-3361): following a train moves the map's centre onto it from the overlay's own draw (`afterDraw`, set only while following) with `jumpTo` each draw, over an easing camera or a per-frame animation, so it runs on the existing loop, costs nothing when off, and a wheel zoom still eases (the centre is not taken while the map is moving; scroll and pinch zoom turn about the centre meanwhile). The camera is one draw behind the train (0-3 px at 240x on one core, median under 1). The panel's button and a double click are the only starts; a double click on a train does not zoom with a finger either (`preventDefault`, not tested on a phone).
- 2026-11-12 (agent tt1, T-3346 stage 1): the departures board shows the next 24 hours from the start of this hour laid out as a day from 4:00 (Japanese boards start early morning), so every row is the timetable as it stands now and an edit shows on every row at once, rather than today's calendar day (whose morning rows would keep the trains of before an edit). The mode choice shows Simple alone until Detailed is built (the two later options hidden, not disabled). The panel opens only for a running rail line, as the chart does (stage 3 should open it on a planned line too). A group's chart takes its longest line, by length, as the axis; a group gets no board yet.
- 2026-11-12 (agent onetrack, T-3332): a one-track station stores the way its trains run against a fixed axis (east, or north where the track runs due north-south), not the node's heading (a renumbered edge can turn it round) and not the physical side (the running side picks the track); switching the running side turns the way round so the platform stays on its track, and lists the station. Lie: a node drag that turns the track through due north-south moves the platform to the other track (T-3354). Switched to one track from the panel, it keeps the way that refuses no line (the axis way first) and loses a terminal or passing tracks, nothing refunded; a one-track station takes neither. Drawn as the half of the station box on its track's side, from the centre line out (over the line until double track's two tracks part); a no-passing station on single track is drawn as plain single track. The stop list's half dot now shows in the related-lines view too (it was only in the plain list), for loops as well. Ceiling tests not rerun: with no one-track station the demand graph is the same edge for edge (a pass is only `span` -1).
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3356; replaces T-3333's "found by distance, 4-16 m"): a stop's station on the other pair is any station of the stop's complex ("if they are connected at all in a station complex its fine") whose node lies on a track beside the stop's (4-16 m off it, parallel), the nearest if several; any level. The sim works the complex out itself (`Network::complex_of`, the rule of game/complexes.ts: centres 100 m, platforms 50 m, links, buildings) rather than being sent the app's, since the twin is remade inside every edit, before the app's complexes catch up. The beside test stays so a complex member on a crossing line or on the same pair is never taken.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3365): quad becomes one thing the game remembers, with a second click for one double or one track; "simple" (doubles) and "advanced" (every track with a way) are two ways of thinking about track, not two modes, and there is no mode switch; a player who really wants it can reach a double's single tracks (e.g. delete one side); Double up becomes the Select bar's 4, and more such standardizations are wanted; the crossover tool comes back. Parked until after v0.3.3. notes/T-3365.md.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3348/T-3363): v0.3.3 ships with two pairs of quad (T-3333) hidden; two pairs, the one-click quad station and AA/BB quad come out together later as one package ("itd be mroe confusing to land a half-finished quad station feature that doesn't account for AABB and then do this later on top of it"). Their design should step toward a later station editor with hand-placed and hand-shaped platforms and tracks routed into any station shape. One-track stations (T-3332) ship in v0.3.3.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3346 design): timetables get three modes per line, Simple (today), Detailed (rows by days and whole hours, up to 6 a day, averaged into demand's five fixed periods, so no extra solving; weekends Same as weekdays / Scaled / Custom, holidays as weekends, Saturday and Sunday one weekend) and Exact (single trains nudged 1 min a click, 15 s with Shift, 10 min with Ctrl, added or removed; conflicts first come, first served, worked out by the sim and shown; leaving Exact drops train edits after asking). Weekend riders don't react to weekend service (assumed). The express is timed exactly behind a local it can't pass. notes/T-3346.md.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, on T-3329's figures): `t1156-all-china.save` is no reference for anything: an early save of mostly straight-line lines, not a real or player network ("kinda a scam"). Measurements and checks use the real-network saves, T-007 and player saves instead (checks/t1205-memory.mjs and old/t1373-rush-days.mjs still name it).

- 2026-11-12 (agent gaprun2, T-3287; maps-37's call): the gaprun rule of the same day (move the pair files of rebuilt rows to data/work/retired/ before they register) is withdrawn. Its diagnosis was wrong: `market_zones.Groups` keys pairs by 6 km square and maps each to the nearest zone of the pack as it is now, so a stale pair cannot give an out-of-range zone. The panic was T-2776's race (region 3's unregister of evo, its heal not yet run, against region 1's register of spi bja foc). Rebuilt rows keep their old pairs until the join solves them again, as before.
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaprun2, T-2776): `market_zones.py` rewrites, under the registry lock, every zone whose market pack names a place no registered zone holds, besides the ids it is given; `world_run.unregister`'s heal after the lock stays as it is. Finding them with a regex over the raw files (about 9 s for 3,982 packs) instead of parsing them (46 s), so every register can afford it.
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaps, T-3287; Anita's go on the design): dead units between built land become zones in the `GAP_FILL` countries (a line between two 50,000 towns within 300 km runs 15 km over it, it holds such a town and touches built land, or built land holds 75% of its land border; in rounds), zoned as any free unit; a gap zone under 50,000 joins the built zone it borders longest, which is rebuilt (bja, evo, spi, fie). Ordinary zones, no pass-through kind (Anita). Replaces T-772's question. notes/T-3287.md.
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaps, T-3287): Norway, Sweden and Finland zoned with no density floor (`MIN_DENSITY`), "just to finish north scandinavia" (Anita); Gotland becomes a zone of its own (54,000), Jan Mayen stays dead.
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaps, T-3287): northwestern Ontario as two thin corridor zones along Highway 17 and the CP line (`CORRIDORS`, a 40 km strip; Dryden 27,000 and Marathon 19,000), kept under the 50,000 zone minimum down to 10,000 (Anita: "the south rim of northwest ontario"); the route's towns are typed in, a one-country rule like Alaska's belt.
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaps, T-3287): North Korea planned on Korea's rule with its own realm; KP-KR border factor 0 (the DMZ, closed), China the default 0.05; zones shown by their city's plain romanisation (Hamhung, not Hamhung City).
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaprun, T-3287): the gap zones show their biggest city, the other towns and the units as aliases (one short name, docs/zones.md 2): Dalarna's row with Gavleborg, Vasternorrland and Jamtland is "Gävle" (Sundsvall, Falun, Östersund aliases), Vasterbotten with Norrbotten "Umeå", Nordland with Troms and Finnmark "Tromsø", Pohjois-Savo's five-unit row "Kuopio", Vidzeme with Latgale "Daugavpils", Alentejo Central's "Évora", Alto Alentejo with Beira Baixa "Castelo Branco"; Ávila (with Segovia, Soria), Cuenca (Teruel), Creuse, Lozère and Gotland keep their names.
- 2026-11-12 (agent gaprun, T-3287; maps-cb's call): before a shipped row rebuilt in place (a grown gap row: bja spi fie evo) registers, the joint and everyday pair files whose pairs start from it, of the solve units its join re-solves, go to data/work/retired/ (the join writes them afresh). Their pairs still carry the old pack's zone numbers, and two rebuilt partners registering together (spi with bja) made market_pack panic "invalid commute between zones". A file of a unit the join does not re-solve (crn-me, poi-mk) stays.

- 2026-11-12 (agent notices, T-3314): the figures-stopped notice waits 180 s for both a clock-worker edit or read left unanswered and a demand network waiting with no step, counted in visible time only. Normal play (a new game, the real save imported, a line built every 30 s, one core) peaked at 17 s and 8 s, so 180 s is about ten times the worst; the T-3313 proposal's 60 s for the clock worker was too near a cold real-save import. A solve already in flight is not watched; add it if a report shows one.

- 2026-11-12 (agent onestop, T-3333): a line on both quad pairs stores one pair's counts, headways and rates while its twin runs (counts halved rounding up, so the two pairs' together are always even, as a two-way loop's are); the panel and the train/headway setters work in the whole line's figures. Chosen over storing the whole line's and dividing in the service, which would have touched every reader of a line's counts.
- 2026-11-12 (agent onestop, T-3333): the twin is an ordinary line in every sum the app makes (money, fares, demand, achievements' network totals) and is left out only of what the player sees listed (`listedLines`); dropping it from the world's lines would have stopped its fares being paid. Its station beside a stop is found by distance (4-16 m, same level, parallel), not by the app's complexes, which the sim does not have.

- 2026-11-12 (agent fare2, T-3303): after a fare edit a line keeps its shown riders and fares until the whole solve's final, instead of taking the local result's riders a second or two after the edit (about 30 s later on the real save). The local result holds the line's longer trips as last solved, at the old fares, so taking it priced them at the new fare; pricing the two parts apart was the other option, more code for figures that last seconds. Zone views still update from the local result.

- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3338): the default high-speed fare multiplier is 1.25, down from 1.5: "ok i guess we can drop to 1.25x if you think tha tmakes snense." Beside a parallel local line 1.5 left a high-speed line 26k riders against 82k at 1.0 (notes/T-3310.md). Saves: the multiplier is one global value, stored only once set, so a save with none follows the new default and a stored value (including a hand-set 1.5) is kept; no migration. Track construction and upkeep for high-speed stay 1.5x.

- 2026-11-12 (agent shapes, T-3319): line bullets come in four shapes, circle, square, diamond and pill (the ferry's 1.5-wide pill, kept since a ferry's default must stay today's look); no fifth: a triangle has no room for a symbol at 16 px, and a hexagon is what a wide symbol makes of a diamond. The modes in Settings are the game's four vehicles (train, tram, bus, ferry); high-speed trains share Train.
- 2026-11-12 (agent shapes, T-3319): so the shapes look one size beside a circle, a square's side is 0.94 of the circle's height and a diamond 1.14 times it (judged by eye on the Lines tab), overflowing the bullet's box above and below; bus bullets are 6% smaller than before. A symbol too wide for a diamond stretches it into a hexagon with the same slopes.
- 2026-11-12 (agent shapes, T-3319): a line's own shape lives in the save header (`shapes`, by line id), app only, outside the undo history like the hand order; picking the mode's shape in the editor clears the line's own, so it follows Settings again (no separate Default button). A copy takes its source's shape; a new line reusing a deleted line's id starts with none.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3297): one running side per game, for trains, trams and buses alike; a new game's follows its start country until the player picks; real networks of left-running cities ship left-hand; an existing game can be switched anyway, with a warning and a list of what to fix. Old saves stay right-hand.
- 2026-11-12 (agent lhtscope, T-3297): a country's default side is its railways' (Wikipedia "Left- and right-hand traffic", Rail traffic; chiark.greenend.org.uk rule-of-the-road tables): left where roads are, but Indonesia (railways right); left too in Argentina, Belgium, Bolivia, Brazil, Cambodia, China, Egypt, France, Iraq, Israel, Italy, Laos, Monaco, Morocco, Myanmar, Nigeria, Peru, Portugal, Senegal, Slovenia, South Korea, Sweden, Switzerland, Taiwan, Tunisia, Uruguay, Venezuela, Vatican and San Marino; Spain, Norway, Finland, Russia, Austria right (their main rule). Mixed countries take the rule most lines follow. `game/runningSide.ts`.
- 2026-11-12 (agent lhtscope, T-3297): a real network keeps its metro's side, which can differ from the country's: London, Tokyo, Osaka, Sendai, Hong Kong (the Tuen Ma line aside), Singapore, Madrid (metro left, Spain's railways right), Cairo (metro as national rail) and New Delhi (Indian practice; no source found for the metro itself) left; Paris, Seoul (Korail-run lines keep left, the rest right), Taipei (weak source: the MRT right, TRA left), São Paulo and mainland China's metros right. The nine left saves were made by setting the save's side byte (`pipeline/realside.py`), which for realsave's networks (every junction flying) is the file `realsave --left` gives; the 9 fingerprints were identical before and after.
- 2026-11-12 (agent lhtscope, T-3297): switching a game's side turns round every single-track piece the player set one way on a run that meets double track where a join counts (joins are ground left or right and stay, so a flyover's ramped leg is again the crossing one; a pre-T-3137 leg with no join too), moves lines it breaks through their same stops, settles rates again, and drops the undo history. Listed for the player: lines left broken, junctions newly crossing flat, one-way track no train can use; fixed ones listed too. Chosen over re-laying flyovers (geometry changes, and the reversal already keeps them grade-separated).
- 2026-11-12 (agent lhtscope, T-3297): `?new=1` stays right-hand whatever the city unless `&side=left`, so checks built on Tokyo or other left-country starts keep their layouts; the start menu's sheet follows the country.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3332 scope): a one-track station on double or quad track stops trains going its way and passes the other way, always drawn as a half dot; a line running both ways through it stops the way it can. One track -> both pays the price difference; both -> one is free with nothing back. Orchestrator's calls on her silence, per the scope's recommendations: a line may end or turn back there; on single track the 1-line icon makes a station with no passing loop and today's passing station stays the default.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3333 scope): a line on quad track runs on both pairs through a hidden twin line the sim keeps in step, so the player sees one line; T-3317's Japanese quad report is this wish, not a bug. When an edit stops the line qualifying, its switch stays on, it runs on one pair until fixed, and a notice says why (the missing stop or the like). Orchestrator's call: the second pair's route may share a short stretch with the first where the pairs meet.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3316): a browser whose languages carry no region (plain ja, ko, zh) starts a new game in that language's country's currency (yen, won, yuan) instead of US$. The account does not store it.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita): agents may read players' saved games on the server when a report needs it, but it is a long dig, so only when a report can't be settled otherwise; mizushin's one-off same-device report is left until it recurs.
- 2026-11-12 (agent bugs, T-3313): a download the game waits on (demand packs, market rows and drives, boundaries, place lists, the account's calls and the like) is given up after 45 s with no headers or no byte of its body, 120 s for the account's calls since an upload of a few MB is sent before any header comes; the limit is on silence, not the whole download, so a big pack on a slow line still loads. The callers' existing retries then run. A tab frozen in the background can give up a healthy download on waking; it is fetched again.
- 2026-11-12 (agent money, T-3303): a fare curve node and the transfer fare are held to curve US$1,000 (`FARE_MAX`, 400 times a US start's base), in `tidyNodes` so a loaded save is held too. A player reported typing 90 billion yen; the fare input had no limit while the high-speed, ferry and bus multipliers (5) and a country's scale (1000) did. 1,000 is far past any real fare (a 5,000 km node at $0.20/km is $1,000) and bounds anything that prices riders wrongly to a thousandth of what a typed number could.

- 2026-11-12 (agent lightrail, T-3289): the Real world network setting is None, Passenger or All. All draws every feature in noritetsu's tiles (no-line track, trams, monorails, funiculars, narrow gauge, heritage); there is no freight because build_tiles.py drops yards, sidings and freight-only track, so true freight needs a tile rebuild of 118 regions. Snapping stays on passenger track (the graph is passenger and light rail only) and the layer shows only that while snapping, so the ring never sits on track a click cannot follow. A stored on becomes Passenger. notes/T-3265.md
- 2026-11-12 (agent stations, T-3286): lengthening a platform joins the pieces of track under its new reach exactly as placing a station does (`lengthen_op`, `unjoin_near`), so a node that is only a clicked corner never stops it. A loose end, a junction, another station's platform, a crossing and a joint that cannot be joined (constructed beside blueprint, a different track count or type) still do, with the same refusals as before.
- 2026-11-12 (agent stations, T-3288): one name per complex is a display rule, not stored names: the stations of a complex that carry no typed name show the name of the oldest of them (lowest node id), worked out where the worker reads its rows (`foldNames`), so a joining station takes it at once and a split complex gives each part's stations their own names back. Chosen over renaming stored names because complexes are worked out in the app from geometry, links and buildings and change with edits. Oldest rather than busiest so the name does not move when lines are added. A typed name (T-2683's flag) keeps its own; renaming a station in the inspector's single-station view, or typing on a station that already has a typed name, names that station alone; from the complex view or a Stations tab row of an untyped complex a rename sets the oldest station's name without the typed flag, so the whole complex follows. No toggle: default plus per-station rename covers it. Saves: nothing is migrated; stations whose names were typed before T-2683 carry no flag and fold like auto names (their own names stay stored).
- 2026-11-12 (agent lightrail, T-3285): the real network layer and snapping graph draw all `railway=light_rail` track (as other rail; kind 0 in the graph, rank 1), since noritetsu's tiles carry no usage to tell S-Bahn and S-tog from Stadtbahn, US light rail, the DLR and Metrolink. Trams stay out. Reason: the layer is rail the player can build on, and Berlin's S-Bahn had no track to route on. notes/T-3265.md
- 2026-11-12 (agent parentsplit, T-3275): a university or college site with no count whose point lies inside a bigger site's outline is a building of that campus: it takes the outer site's institution (a small share, as a non-main campus), or is left out when the outer site has none, since the outer site's area already holds its land; an uncounted outline drawn around a counted site is that site's. Mostly duplicate drawings of one campus and buildings mapped as amenity=university (IU Indianapolis's "Lecture Hall" 7,318 -> 33). Not done: capping area-sized sites lower (Georgia Gwinnett 25,188 in atl against about 11,000 real): at 2x the curve it sends 16.6% of the world's students near home (from 5.8%, 2,143 packs), at 1x 29.4% (2,972 packs), a demand-wide change for its own task (T-3276). notes/T-3273.md
- 2026-11-12 (maps-5e via agent parentsplit, T-3274): Taiwan, which the World Bank leaves out, scales its university students to the Ministry of Education's national figure as World Bank countries do (1,094,829 students in universities and colleges in school year 2023-24, all levels from junior college to doctoral, over the 23,400,220 registered at end 2024: 0.0468 a resident). Kinmen (jiw) 6,487 -> 2,567 students, university_rate about 0.036. notes/T-3273.md

- 2026-11-12 (Anita): no Resend Pro ("no dont wanna do resend"); at its free daily cap (hit at about 200 on 11-10 and 11-11), recovery-email confirmations and password resets fail until the next UTC day. Area-sized campuses in scaled packs stay as they are (T-3276). Japan tourism: option A of notes/T-3280.md, resort towns' published visitors in Japan's long-trip weights ("A makes sense. lets do it"). Server deployed 15:01Z with the raised caps and place flags.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3260 follow-up): the cursor tag's greyed "Hold Ctrl to draw straight" line is gone ("dont want it to be in a super prominent place cuz most people prob dont wanna do this"); then the Snap to roads and tram streets hovers went back to "Hold Ctrl to draw by hand" and "Hold Ctrl to draw off streets" (Anita: "lets just drop this. i think most of the time people will not care enough"). Ctrl still draws straight; nothing says so. Also her wording: "View a copy", "This network isn't saved. You can save it to keep it.", "High-speed track can't be single track."
- 2026-11-12 (agent parentsplit, T-3273): tier 0 universities: an institution's Wikidata count is shared over all its campuses in the world (`campus_pools.py`, rebuilt by the light pass's wikidata step), not over one pack's, by area, each campus but the main one (its own tagged site, else its biggest) capped at the curve 1,872 x ha^0.31 with the rest on the main: branches hold more land a student (Tamkang's Lanyang, NTU's Yunlin, UBC Okanagan). A counted member's count is taken off its counted parents' (a system or federal university), so a site linked only to a used-up system takes none (Texas A&M Galveston 0; undershoot, as Anita's rule for unsure fits). An untagged campus joins a counted institution whose Wikidata label its name holds, within 500 km, labels a sister's name also holds left out as generic ("Texas A&M University", 国際大学). Open universities by name in the main languages and by Wikidata label (P31 doesn't mark them), and university farms and forests, are left out. The lies: the area split still overgives a big-land branch below the curve (Ming Chuan's Kinmen branch 3,172, real a few hundred), and in a country scaled to its national total the students a branch gives up go to the pack's area-sized sites. notes/T-3273.md

- 2026-11-12 (agent hsrsave, T-3272): a line in a real save runs high-speed trains when over half its length is on high-speed track, the rule the game already uses for a player's new line (`hsrShare` > 0.5, clock.worker); the 1.5x fare, the 320 km/h top speed and the high-speed fleet follow from that flag. A through-service onto a conventional stretch (a mini-shinkansen like Yamagata or Akita) is high speed when most of it is Shinkansen track, and its conventional end then runs at that track's own limit (a fast line is capped by the track under it, T-817). Track is high speed where the network JSON says `hsr` (N02's Shinkansen class); an edge shared with an ordinary line stays ordinary. jpn7's saved network predated T-1625, so it carried no `hsr` at all: rebuilt, 14 routes are high speed. The OSM-built saves (the hand-made seven, the automatic ones) have no high-speed lines to mark: their builds drop routes tagged or mostly on high-speed track and carry no `hsr`.
- 2026-11-12 (maps-5e, T-3218, Anita "raising attendance probbaly ok? idk"): university attendance 0.6 -> 0.8 of enrolment everywhere (`everyday::RATE[3]`), from UBC's daytime estimate of 0.83; applied before the world rebuild's light pass so its gate runs on it, kept if the campus figures after it look right.
- 2026-11-12 (agent buildfix, T-3262): single high-speed track stays forbidden (spec, T-3126). Picking Single on High speed keeps High speed and double and says "High-speed track can't be single track. Pick Regular first." instead of switching the type; the count the player picked is kept across types, so single shows as double on High speed or Tram and comes back with Regular, and the browser remembers the count picked.
- 2026-11-12 (agent buildfix, T-3260, Anita's TODO "hold ctrl to snap track placement to straight line"): Ctrl already meant "snap to nothing" in the track tool (T-1887), "draw by hand" with Snap to roads and "flip the street snap" with Tram, so the straight line goes on the same key rather than a new one: with Ctrl held, a segment drawn on from track (a chain, a loose end, a branch) ends on the straight line on from its start, the pointer's nearest point on it. The cost: a curved segment that also snaps to nothing is no longer drawable in one go (draw it without Ctrl, or straight then reshape). Ramps are unaffected; Ctrl joins nothing, so it makes no junction or flyover. Its instruction is a greyed last line of the cursor tag, "Hold Ctrl to draw straight".
- 2026-11-12 (agent buildfix, T-3261, Anita's TODO "snap to existing rail forces quad rail to merge to 2"): read as the snapped routes (real network and roads), whose quad merged both pairs into one node at every click because T-423's carry-on was only for routes without corners. Now any drawn quad route carries both pairs on from a loose quad end and ends them apart on open ground. Quad ending on existing track (a node or the middle of a piece) still merges there, as the spec says, since that track has one pair.
- 2026-11-12 (agent buildfix, T-3263): the road snap's ghost lagged because every pointer move dropped the route being fitted or priced, so with a slow track model (a big save) no fitted ghost landed until the pointer rested. A spot being fitted or priced now finishes and the newest pointer goes next (as the ordinary ghost's queue does); a click still asks for its own spot at once. Not reproduced here: clicks being lost (every click placed in the tests); the search itself still runs on the main thread, about 80 ms for a 9.5 km leg across Tokyo.

- 2026-11-12 (agent split, T-3212, Anita: "when zoomed out we should have the whole thing just look like a single track, and be thin"): a station's split on single track shows only once double track's two tracks part (about zoom 17); further out the track and its lines keep single track's width through the station, where double track is already drawn wider. notes/T-3212.md.

- 2026-11-12 (agent flags3, T-3328, on Anita's "maybe add spanish region flags... lisbon... jakarta"): list 3 adds 21 flags: 13 Spanish communities, Lisbon, Porto, Madeira, the Azores, Jakarta, Brittany, Corsica, Paris. Only flags whose Commons file is public domain/CC0 or MIT are used as they are; a flag whose file is CC BY(-SA) (Andalusia, Madeira, Brittany; Aragon, Cantabria, Castile and Leon, La Rioja left out) is redrawn or skipped, and heavy emblem files (Lisbon, Porto, Navarre, Jakarta, over 200 KB) are redrawn small. Bali and Yogyakarta not added. Names stay English in every language but Japanese and Chinese. Needs a server deploy (set 3); notes/T-3328.md.
- 2026-11-12 (agent flags2, T-3279, on Anita's "show an empty flag rectangle outline and then our display name"): flags are 4:3 rectangles everywhere (flag-icons, MIT, for countries, the EU and UN; Japan's 47 prefectures from Wikimedia Commons, public domain, Hiroshima and Kagawa drawn for the game since their Commons files carry no free licence; New York City drawn, its seal simplified to a ring), shown after the name. Tokyo is Tokyo Metropolis's flag and Osaka the prefecture's (no separate city flags, so no name appears twice). The picker's first country is the browser's time zone's, since many people anywhere keep an en-US browser; its language's region only when the zone says nothing. Prefecture names are romanised without macrons in every language but Japanese and Chinese, which get Wikidata's, as the game names cities. New codes carry a list version (`set`) the server announces in config, so a game ahead of the server never offers a code it would refuse. notes/T-3269.md.
- 2026-11-12 (agent flags, T-3269, on Anita's "let people add a flag next to their name"): one flag per account, none by default, picked in the account panel and shown beside the name on the leaderboards and Community cards. The list is every ISO country (249, plus Kosovo, which the game has), not only the 86 the game has cities in, since a flag says where the player is from; plus Train, Earth, Pride, Transgender, the EU, the UN, Pirate, Checkered, and three city flags with plain public domain designs (Amsterdam, Chicago, Washington, D.C.). Left out: NATO, the Olympic rings (trademarked), regional and disputed-state flags. Pictures are round, from circle-flags (MIT, 173 KB for all 261, one small file each, loaded only when shown); Train, Amsterdam and Chicago are drawn in its style. A moderator hide of the name (T-3227) hides the flag too. notes/T-3269.md.
- 2026-11-12 (agent sbahn, T-3265): in an automatic real network a `route=light_rail` on main-line track (over half its ways `railway=rail` or light rail with `usage` main or branch, not tagged unelectrified; 5 or more stops inside, at least 1 km apart on average) runs commuter trains (200 m) at commuter speed, keeping light rail's own track and frequency: OSM maps Berlin's and Hamburg's S-Bahn that way, and Berlin's ran 3-car trains. A commuter kind outright would lay it on the main-line track beside it (the graph has no light-rail track). The lie: Dallas's DART and other light rail tagged usage=main would get 10-car trains if built. notes/T-3265.md.

- 2026-11-11 (agent boards, T-3248, on Anita's "current (or most recent) score rather than max"): a game's figures were already its last send, so the only max was across a person's games, and that stays: on every board a person's row is their best game there, each game at its current figures (Anita, after the live boards showed her US row from her Asia game: "do we not take the best save we've played, for each leaderboard case? ideally we do that"). A most-recent-game rule was tried for city and country boards the same day and dropped. Stale rows after a big nerf are hidden with a cutoff date in the feedback inbox (games last sent before it count nowhere; nothing deleted). Community's Biggest is unchanged (a share's points are fixed when it is shared). notes/T-3248.md.
- 2026-11-11 (Anita's option A, agent hsr, T-3239): the regional market's route search charges fares at a longer trip's money weight, 0.4 of a local trip's (every country's three income groups average 0.40 by their trips), so a long trip stays on a high-speed line rather than changing to a slower line for the last stretch to save its per-km extra (Sendai - Omiya - Tokyo at 1.5x: 74% changed at Omiya, now 0). The lie: commutes and outings between zones share that search, so they choose routes at 0.4 too while their mode choice stays at the local weight; one search per income group would cost about 2x the market's search time. notes/T-3239.md.
- 2026-11-11 (agent realauto, T-3225, on Anita's "30 biggest"): the automatic real networks are the 30 biggest metros by annual riders in Wikipedia's List of metro systems (a city's systems summed) that have a pack and no hand-made network; Tehran skipped (no Iranian packs). The seven hand-made saves stay: each has timetabled frequencies the automatic build cannot match.
- 2026-11-11 (agent realauto, T-3225): automatic real networks run OSM's `interval` where tagged, else realnet's default by kind (metro 20/10/4 an hour) shared among a route master's patterns each way, and a suburban train with no route master and no ref every half hour (Russia's elektrichki: the commuter default filled a terminal's tracks many times over). The lie: most lines run a guessed frequency; a sandbox player sets their own.
- 2026-11-11 (agent realauto, T-3225): automatic real networks name stations and lines in Latin letters: OSM's `name` when it is, else `name:en`, `int_name` or `name:latin`, else Cyrillic transliterated (BGN/PCGN, simplified) and Chinese in toneless pinyin run together as OSM's own name:en has it ("Xianxinlu"), by `unidecode`, which misreads some characters (厂 as "han"). A Japanese name with no name:en stays in kana and kanji (7% of Osaka's stations). The hand-made Tokyo and Sendai saves keep their Japanese names.
- 2026-11-11 (agent realauto, T-3225): a route whose line the track model cannot run in an automatic real network (a terminal or junction with more tracks than a game node's 8 ends) is left out and the city built again without it, rather than shipping a line that never runs.
- 2026-11-11 (agent realauto, T-3225): the new game sheet's Real world network is a list of every city with one, blank to begin with; picking a city makes it the start. A checkbox shown only once the start city had a network could not tell a player which of 37 cities have one.

- 2026-11-11 (agent savehang, T-3238): at a passing station a slower train waits at most one faster headway plus its lead (beside its own headway minus the spacing), and plans timetable a line rarer than every 4 hours as every 4 hours. The own-headway cap alone let a local's round trip grow with its headway, so a local with fewer trains than such waits had no settled rate and the settle ran it to zero while the sweeps grew without end (Tokyo saves that never loaded). The lie: a local that could never get clear goes after one express and the express behind is slowed instead.

- 2026-11-11 (agent split, T-3212): two-way single track is drawn splitting into two tracks through every station on it (Anita approved the mock), and trains take their lane from the drawn track, so T-3202's separate train-only split is gone and the lie it left (trains half beside one drawn track) with it. Calls the notes left open: a split that reaches double track within its 60 m ease stays two tracks to it (no pinch of single track between); one that reaches a junction first eases over the room there is; one-way single track and single track joining one track of a double at the station stay single, and such an end keeps one place for a line ending there. Tram stops need no rule: tram track is never single. notes/T-3212.md.

- 2026-11-11 (orchestrator, agent lonslow, T-3233): every dead-end station of a real network gets a terminal, since real termini turn trains on all their platforms (without one, T-2986 turns them one at a time, which capped Tokyo's lines at 15 trains an hour and left London's settle circling). Real saves are built and settled in the game's Mercator frame and carry their pacing (`LPC1`), and a load with saved pacing keeps it instead of pacing again: pacing can flip lines in and out of step every round (London: about 240 changes a round, never settling in 3 rounds), so re-pacing a loaded save only moves it. notes/T-3233.md.

- 2026-11-11 (agent realnet, T-1773): real networks' track lies on OSM's levels, with these lies: `layer` is an order and is clamped to -3..3 (so most of Seoul's and much of Tokyo's subway draws at -3), bridges and cuts under 400 m with no other line crossing them are dropped, a ramp (200 m a level, 4%) moves to clear platforms, and where a real line climbs faster than 4% a station takes a neighbour's level (Brooklyn's F and G at 7 Av and 15 St draw at +2, really underground). Every imported edge stays `sep`, so the 5-37% of OSM-separated crossings still on one level are no flat crossings. London keeps its level -1 save for now (its settle, T-3233). notes/T-1773.md.

- 2026-11-11 (agent search, T-3224): a Community search lists every matching share, not one per person, since one per person would show only one of a player's networks when searching their name. Each view keeps its order and cap (Random 12 shuffled, Biggest and Most liked 50), and a rank stays the share's place on the whole board, so only a player's best share has one; numbering the matches 1, 2, 3 would have claimed places they don't hold. The game shows the box only when the server's config says `trainworldCommunitySearch`.

- 2026-11-11 (Anita, T-3213): a university campus gets no new "daytime destination" demand factor (meals, errands, events, visitors); UBC's low figures are fixed through its data instead (students spread over the campus, census jobs, the parent's student count: T-3214..T-3216), queued for the next world rebuild with the airport items. Raising university attendance 0.6 -> 0.8 is a tentative yes ("probbaly ok? idk"), applied with that rebuild and kept only if the campus figures look right (T-3218). notes/T-3213.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent varyings, T-3192): every shader program's varyings fit in 8 vectors, the limit Firefox with fingerprinting resistance sets (LibreWolf, Mullvad; WebGL2 itself guarantees 15), held by `checks/t3192-varyings.mjs` in smoke. The station program meets it by having its fragment shader read and project the platform's centre line from u_stline itself, exact but 13 texel reads and projections per station fragment; quantising the points into 16-bit pairs was the cheaper choice that fit with no room to spare. Transfer bubbles, chevrons, grade marks, car parks and building floors are left out, logged, where the browser refuses their programs; the rest still stops startup. notes/T-3192.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent flyover, T-3169): the flyover target max speed is 80 km/h by default. On a branch drawn as one curve at 160 km/h, 80 takes 519 m of main at 30 degrees and 842 m at 60 (the tightest flyover 453 and 447 m) and lifts the slow side from 45-54 to 80 km/h; 100 needed 1.45 km at 30 degrees and did not fit at 60. A target with no room falls back 20 km/h at a time, then to the tightest shape, never flat; it is never faster than the target max speed, whose curves hold the route to that anyway. notes/T-3169.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent flyover, T-3170): a high-speed route's flyover legs are one-way single high-speed track (priced and upkept as high-speed, 320 km/h cap, 55 m least curve), so `set_edge` allows high-speed track as one-way single; two-way single high-speed track is still refused and nothing else lays it. Regular legs were the other choice, which would cap a high-speed junction at 160. notes/T-3169.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent flyover, T-3168): the map labels each flyover crossing leg's slowest curve beside it, one label per leg (its five curves at the default target are all near 80, so one per curve is clutter), listed before the route's own so where the two overlap the slower shows. notes/T-3169.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent flyover, T-3188): a flyover's J3 may be where the route runs anywhere from 80 degrees off the main down to parallel with it (was 4 degrees and up), at least 24 m off the main when under 4; the crossing leg then crosses the main steeper than the route and turns back along it, case A's crossing no longer pinned to where J3's tangent meets the main. notes/T-3169.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent stuck, T-3196): Construct waits at most 20 s for the ground under the blueprint, then is refused as not loaded (the downloads go on for the next press), since every message waits behind it; road tiles are given up after 20 s, range fetches after 60 s. A new hour's trips skip the queue only while it waits on a download with the model at rest (`parkedOn`), not in general: a load or panic rebuild also awaits with the model half made. notes/T-3196.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent roads, T-3129): Snap to roads for rail. Roads rail may follow: motorway, trunk, primary, secondary, tertiary (the design's pick; minor streets, service ways, links and pedestrian streets out, as a train on minor streets is a tram, so the `LINK` rule stays unused for now). Centre line at every level, ground, viaduct and tunnel alike (Anita: snap below too); no cost benefit for following a road at any level (Anita: "no cost benefit, though actually i guess we could maybe add a slight cost benefit"): it can be added later, a slight one for viaducts and level -1 and weaker than the tram's at ground, which needs an edge flag, save bits and a price row. A turn costs the time lost slowing, as metres at the target speed, at most 600 m (the cap keeps a fast line's search from covering a city for an unreachable end). Curves as wide as the target allows within 20 m of the road corner, else 40 m, slow and never refused. A road meeting track at under 20 degrees is not a crossing, for all track (changes old saves' crossing prices a little). notes/T-3129.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent flatbadge, T-3164): the unusable one-way badge is local to a node's move table (a one-way single end bringing trains in with no move on, or taking them out with no move into it), not a walk over the network, so a dead stretch whose every node looks fine is not marked. Bare ends (a stub being drawn) and stations (a line that runs once may end there) never get it; where one node has both kinds, it shows converging. Red like the refused crossing, and not hidden by the flat-marks setting, since it marks a mistake rather than a cost.

- 2026-11-11 (agent flatbadge, T-3161, Anita's option 1): a two-way single end joined to double track draws the link the move table already gives it to the other track, as a 40 m crossover along the double leaving the node on the other side (the most nearly opposite double end), from the joined track at the node to the other track, drawn and shown close in only like the turn-back crossovers. Drawing only; the model was right (notes/T-3161.md).

- 2026-11-11 (agent flatbadge, T-3189): the ramp mark is a trapezoid, its narrow end a quarter of its wide end (Anita's ratio), same size and placement, so it no longer reads as a one-way chevron.

- 2026-11-11 (agent roads, T-3172): buses and trams use any road in the basemap data (Anita: "if we have it as data, its ok to go"): every service way of any kind and `access` (the 60 m link rule is no longer used for buses), tracks, bus guideways, and `path` only with subclass pedestrian (pedestrian streets), besides the street classes, busways and (buses only) motorways. Footways, cycleways, steps, bridleways, platforms and plain paths stay out as not drivable-looking; raceways, piers, rail and ferries are not roads. `access=no` is ignored (busways carry it). The cost is that a bus may cut through a car park when it saves road; speeds are the existing rule. Trains following roads (T-3129) can take a stricter keep. notes/T-3172.md

- 2026-11-11 (agent phonemem, T-2802): on a small device (`smallDevice()`) the WASM memories of the clock and demand workers grow 8 MB at a time, not 32 (`set_heap_step`, sim/src/heap.rs), so each ends nearer its peak: a demand worker held 31-40 MB in 65. Nothing a player sees changes; the cost is a few more grows, each about as dear as the memory then is, which at a phone's 40-50 MB should be a few ms (not measured on a phone). Desktop keeps 32 MB, where T-1594's grow costs were measured. Also from T-2802, on every device: only the page fetches and parses `outlines.json`, and the clock and shade workers take a copy of its typed arrays (notes/T-2802.md).

- 2026-11-11 (T-3159): a stop click draws its marker in the new tier at the click, before the worker answers: a stop taken out shows as a route station and one added as a stop, whatever tier the worker then gives it. The worker's tiers replace the guess with the world (about 0.1 s) and a refused click puts the marker back. The stop list's row is not guessed: its rows line up with per-stop figures by index.

- 2026-11-11 (agent lanes4, T-3137 C3-C4): the pointer picks a track of a double only while double draws as two tracks (`laneState(zoom).t > 0`), the snap ring sitting 2 m x t off the centre line; zoomed out a branch joins the beside track as before. The inspector keeps one switch showing the actual way (inferred included) plus Reverse and a "Use automatic way" button, not a three-state control, because a segmented Auto would hide the way; off stores 3 only where the run gives a way, else 0. Setting a way moves the piece's counting joins to the track that carries it (in `set_edge_oneway`), but not the run's far end: a contradicting way works badly, as Anita expects.
- 2026-11-11 (agent todo3, T-3152): a train or bus count set by hand is held to the track's limit in the panel only: a plus or typed number at or past floor(round trip / shortest headway) (a loop's even, one at least; 1000 where the track has no limit) is refused with the shake, a typed number above it sets the limit. The stored count of a line already past the limit is not clamped down in the sim: T-752 keeps held trains on purpose where other lines' fixed rates overfill track, and cutting the count on a recompute would sell trains the player bought and change saved games, so such a line keeps its red headway and held-out alert until the player lowers it.
- 2026-11-11 (agent traincap, T-3201): on single track a line with one train at a level, on a stretch no other line runs over, is held for its run time without the 240 s margin (`occ_at` in sim capacity.rs, used by the utilisation, `line_limits` and `fill`), since the margin is for the next train and a lone train has none; so it never shows "Single track is full" or a red headway from it, and its cap (T-3165) stays at least 2. With two or more trains, or another line on the stretch, the margin counts as before. A lone train on a stretch shared with another line keeps the margin. Counts set by headway (no hand count) are not covered.
- 2026-11-11 (agent traincap, T-3165; supersedes the T-3152 line above on where the cap sits): the cap on a hand-set train or bus count is the whole trains the track lets run plus one (a loop's two), 1000 where there is no limit, so a player always sees that one train held with the held messages, the issue and the red headway (Anita's ask, after T-3152's refusal hid the cause). Buses use the same panel code and so the same rule. A count past the cap is lowered to it once, when a game loads (`game/capLoad.ts`, asking each line's `lineHeld` limits; lines on a headway and lines with no limit are left alone). A cap that falls later, from a track edit, does not lower the count: it stays, red with the held-out alert, until the player edits it, as T-752 keeps held trains on purpose.
- 2026-11-11 (agent todo3, T-3154): a complex's view lists one row per station (name, chips of its lines, riders waiting, boardings today, terminal button) in place of one row per line. What the line rows gave that is gone: the line's name as text, its waiting and boardings summed over its stations (now per station), the "to <ends>" hover and the per-line "waiting for <line>" hover; kept: a click opens a station alone, a chip selects its line.
- 2026-11-11 (agent lanes3, T-3137 C3): the pointer picks one track of double track only once the two tracks have started to part on screen (`laneState(zoom).t > 0`, about zoom 16.7 in New York); zoomed further out the dot stays on the centre line and the sim takes the track beside the route, which never crosses the other. The dot is drawn 2 m x `t` off the centre line, where the track under it is drawn, and the ghost's ends use built track's `shiftedEnds`, so ghost and built track agree.

- 2026-11-11 (agent lanes2, T-3137 C2): a join changes the move table only through the way it gives its run; a joined end's lanes keep their own slot rather than taking the joined lane's position (C1's note). The slot already makes a near join running its track's way cross nothing, and a far join or a two-way branch cross the near track, so nothing else was needed.
- 2026-11-11 (agent lanes2, T-3137 C2): with no pointer side (`set_route_join` NaN, and every route until C3 sends one), a single-track end takes the track beside it: by its side of the double it leaves, else of the other single track at the node, so two singles at a double's end take one track each. A route that makes a junction of a plain joint between double and single track also gives that single end (join 0) its track beside, or a hand-laid flyover's first leg would stay two-way. Only edits do this; loading never touches a join, so old saves behave as before.
- 2026-11-11 (agent lanes2, T-3137 C2): the reroute on a way change is in `TrackWorld::commit_with` for every checked edit (a route, a station, a delete), not only routes: whatever changes a run's way moves the lines it broke onto `route_hops` through their stops in the same edit, and leaves a line with no way broken. Undo, redo and planners' sketches skip it.
- 2026-11-11 (agent lanes2, T-3137 C2): `oneway_edges` gives the way, inferred included, so the map's chevrons, the hover's "One way" and `legShifts` already show a stub joined to one track as one way before C3; `edge_oneway` stays the stored value. The sim takes `oneway` 3 (pinned both ways) now, so C4 is the inspector only.

- 2026-11-10 (agent loops3, T-3140): a click never turns a line round a loop or sets it one way (Anita: "assume people do not want to loop"); `auto_turn` is gone and the click is `insert_stop`'s answer as `set_stops` takes it, as on v0.2.1. Kept from T-3122: a loop stops at every pass, one answer for which way a stop is served, and a turned balloon's stem growing past its turning stop when clicked on (the player looped it already). Her barbell (Amsterdam Line 1) now takes a right click at each loop; turned in any order it comes out the same circle.
- 2026-11-10 (agent loops3, T-3141): a one-way rail circle that turns back at exactly one place, a stop, is started at that stop (`Network::branch_end`, in `set_line`, so every edit, undo and load): the same lap and path, now a balloon turning there with the terminal turnaround. No routing, one pass over the path. Only one way, as Anita put it; a both-ways circle keeps its reversal, and one turned into a balloon by this stays a balloon if later set both ways (equally valid, just not undone).
- 2026-11-10 (agent loops3, T-3139): the chevrons were mirrored, not mis-aimed in the sim: `line_stop_ways` gives `node_heading` (the track's tangent, counter-clockwise from east, y north) and `headingOnScreen` laid it out in the map's local frame, whose y runs south, so a chevron sat reflected across east-west (right on east-west track, reversed on north-south, off by twice its angle in between). Fixed in `headingOnScreen`; the half fills had the same flip. The map chevron is 18 px (was 24), its middle 7 px past the dot's edge (was about 1.5 x (12 + its radius) from the centre).

- 2026-11-10 (agent onewayui, T-3142, T-3143): a bus or ferry loop run one way answers `line_stop_ways` like rail (keyed by the stop's id): every stop is one way except the stops a route that loops back passes twice and its first stop, where the bus or boat turns; a hairpin at a ferry berth is no turn. Shown as half dots on the bus stop markers and the ferry tool's port circles while the line is edited, and in a ferry loop's stop list. The station hover says it only by the chevron beside the line's bullet: a "One way, towards {next station}" row was tried and dropped (Anita 2026-11-11: "its already in the arrow"). Bus stops drawn in the line's own view (street rings) and the bus stop hover are not covered (T-3147).
- 2026-11-10 (agent flyover2, T-3120, notes/T-3085.md "T-3120 and T-3121"): both legs of a real flyover leave the main at the node clicked, J, which then has four ends (main back, main ahead, the plain leg on the branch side, the crossing leg on the far side; no move through it crosses another), replacing T-3092's J1 at the click and J2 on the main 450-550 m behind it. Nothing is laid behind the click, so a branch right after a station gets a flyover, and the plain leg is the route exactly as drawn. The crossing leg jogs 12 m out on the far side, runs beside the main, crosses it ahead and comes down between the main and the plain leg to J3.
- 2026-11-10 (agent flyover2, T-3120): a flyover's length is now set by its two 200 m ramps (4%, 8 m a level) with 14.5 m level each side of the main and 20 m spare: 234.5 m of leg each side of the crossing, about 470-500 m along the main for a route that leaves gently (any 160 km/h route) and 270-380 m at 60 km/h. Curvature was not the limit. Steeper ramps (6%: about 135 m shorter) are Anita's call and not assumed.
- 2026-11-10 (agent flyover2, T-3120): J3 is the first point from 160 m out along the route where a leg fits; per J3 the variants are tried neatest first: 12 m beside J3's tangent all the way down, straight from 20 m past J3 to a crossing far enough ahead (up to 86 degrees across the main), then the far side swung out 20-50 degrees. The leg's corners are as wide as their legs hold up to the target speed's radius, never under 30 m; its ramps are laid by raising a run of its own corners (`geom::vertical` spreads a ramp over corners, T-1895) since `bridge::ramp_over` found no room on a leg curved at every corner.
- 2026-11-10 (agent flyover2, T-3120): one-way leg ends at a junction are drawn off their centre line by exactly what keeps them half a double's drawn gap (2 m) from a single leg beside them, or on a double's own track line, from the real distance, in place of a fixed 2 m eased out over 60 m: the drawn gap is the larger of the real one and 4 m, so it never closes in after the node (Anita's "kiss"). The sim leg also curves off from J3 itself, not after a 40 m straight.
- 2026-11-10 (agent flyover2, T-3121): the target max speed takes any value from 20 to 320 km/h, typed in the shown unit or stepped through 20, 40 ... 160, 200, 250, 320; 20 is the floor because its radius (29 m) is just over rail's smallest (25 m). Under 160 every auto corner of a drawn route, the clicked ones too, gets the design radius set where its curve would be wider, so a low target lays tight curves everywhere (above 160 only the corners the tool makes are widened, as before).

- 2026-11-10 (agent ferryport, T-3127): a ferry port click's reach to the shore is 40 screen pixels of ground, 200 m at least (the old fixed value) and 1500 m at most, so a click a short way inland or out on the water finds the coast at any zoom (200 m was 5 px at zoom 12). 40 px is about where a pointer still reads as "by the water"; the 1500 m cap keeps a far-out click from snapping to a distant shore, and the snap raster stays about 600 cells across so wide reaches cost no more per hover.
- 2026-11-10 (agent sharecopy, T-3115, notes/T-2887.md "Share link and open a copy"): a copy of a shared network is tagged by a save header field `copy` (the share id), not made a sandbox: it plays as a normal game but sends no figures or achievement counts and, when shared, the sandbox notice, so it never ranks anywhere. Sandbox mode would have hidden the money and refused nothing, which is not the network that was shared.
- 2026-11-10 (agent sharecopy, T-3115): a share link to a hidden, banned or deleted share gets one answer, "This network isn't shared anymore.", so a link never shows that a share was taken down (nothing that marks removal as a badge, the Spore lesson in notes/T-2887.md).
- 2026-11-10 (agent sharecopy, T-3115): a shared save keeps its line and group names (free text the server cannot filter, as it never parses saves); only the Getting started progress is dropped. Reports hide the save with the share, and the inbox can download it to read them. Stripping names would need the track model on the sharer's side to rewrite the network.

- 2026-11-10 (Anita, T-3107): a dead end and a through station turn alike: "lets remove the dead-end advantage and make dead-ends and through stations identical from a gameplay perspective ... all players should have to understand is terminal = 30tph". A turn (a line's end or a reversal partway) holds one platform for max(turnaround + 60 s, 200 s), pooled over every platform track with a terminal and one track of each pair without: 15/h, 30/h with, at 3 minutes. Gone: the 90 s crossing move for a turn at a through station, the through station's platform hold and the terminal's two sidings, the reversal limiter (`REVERSAL_LIMIT_S`, `TERMINAL_REVERSAL_S`), and wire kinds 6, 8, 9, 10 and 14 (never made now; the numbers stay for old saves). Hints never say dead end: kind 3 says build a terminal, or with one, turn some trains at another station, and never "add platform tracks" (Anita: stations are two tracks, one line cannot split across pairs).
- 2026-11-10 (agent rebuildfix, T-3088, notes/T-3052.md round 2): built track can be rebuilt as tram (kind 3, not street tram) and tram as rail or high speed. Rail and tram never join, so a change of family takes whole joined stretches: a piece that would meet the other family (unpicked track, a crossover, single track, a refused piece) stays, with everything joined to it; only the pieces refused for their own sake are drawn red. Stations are fitted, not refused: the platform is clamped into the new kind's range (rail to tram cut to 80 m; tram to rail out to 60 m) and every station whose track changes family is charged, picked or not. Lines need no refusal (a line's path is connected, so it changes whole); they lose high-speed trains when they become tram lines and take the new kind's dwell and turnaround only where they had the old kind's defaults. Chosen over refusing long platforms, since rail networks players think of as trams have 100-200 m stations everywhere.
- 2026-11-10 (agent rebuildfix, T-3087): the level and type planner (`plan_edges`, rebuild and blueprint level buttons alike) blames a refusal on the pieces its problems name and tries the rest again whole, instead of falling back to groups and then to every piece alone with its ends kept. The old fallback turned one refused piece into ramps at every joint of the run and refused the ramps that dipped to ground over water (Anita's Diemen-Weesp rebuild to -1). Problems naming no piece still take the old path. With nothing changeable, Rebuild now runs the edit without asking so its refusal says why, instead of being greyed.

- 2026-11-10 (Anita): no more silent updates. Every publish gets a version and an entry in `app/src/ui/updates.ts` listing what changed ("btw in general lets just stop doing silent updates i think. everything can be listed. just makes it less confusing"). Replaces the silent updates 2026-11-10b and 10c.
- 2026-11-10 (agent hsrplat, T-3078): a station's length is accepted or refused by the kind of track under it (Anita: "we probably just shouldnt be restricting car count at all when placing stations... reject or accept it based on what type of track"). Cause of the bug: `TrackApi.edge_kind` is the raw TrackKind (1 is high speed) and `stateRows` copied it, so every truthy `e.kind` test (tram platform length, tram snapping family, map flags, inspector, regionMoney, blueprintPick) took high-speed edges for tram. Now `stateRows` keeps kind only for 2 and 3, as `types.ts` says, so all tests are right at once. The tool keeps its two lengths (rail `buildPlatform`, tram `buildTramPlatform`) and a spot takes the one for its track, so a long rail length never reaches tram track; a stop placed with the tram length is within 20-80 m by its own setting, and the sim refuses a longer one. Chosen over one shared length clamped on tram (a 10 car setting would then silently become 80 m on a tram). Sim unchanged: its platform range is per kind and high speed has rail's 60-400 m. checks/t3078-hsr-platform.mjs.
- 2026-11-10 (agent loopend, T-3021/T-3022, notes/T-2988.md): a line's end turns round a loop of track as a one-way loop line whose stops run out, round, and back over the stem's stops (every pass a row; Anita: one row per station waits for T-3025). Offered on the last stop at or before where the loop rejoins, only when a rough round trip comes out at most 120 s or 10% longer. "Click to close the loop" on the first stop is now only for a ring that does not run back over the line's own track (Anita: "yes i guess we can remove first stop click to close loop balloon"); saved express-return balloons still load and run as before, nothing migrates. A loop's closing hop now carries on round only when that costs at most 300 s more track time than the quickest way in (before, at any length), so a closing never runs far round the network to avoid one turn; where a short loop lies at the far end, turning one end turns both.

- 2026-11-10 (agent tramyield, T-3032): trams yield to trains at flat crossings (Anita: "trams yield to the trains so that trains dont get slowed down"). The train route's crossing resource drops tram users (none is built if only trams cross it); the tram route's holds its own passes at 30 s and each train pass at 90 s, and the trains there are never cut back for it (`Capacity::caps`). So a tram's capacity is what the trains leave, and no tram frequency changes a train's. The map's train guard likewise never holds a train for a tram. Chosen over giving trams a fixed share: a busy train line can then starve a tram crossing completely, which is the price of the rule.

- 2026-11-10 (agent numfit, T-3020): the long words for million and billion ("1,2 Mio.", "$2,97 млрд") get room by widening, not by shorter words. German, Russian and Turkish start with a wider dock (344-396 px against 304-352) and wider figure columns in the Lines and Stations lists; French, Spanish and Portuguese get slightly wider columns; the top bar's cash box is 5.8em in those six (5.2em in Japanese, Korean, Chinese). In the Cities list the mode bar drops first (below 350 px of list in de/ru/tr, 330 in fr/es/pt), since the figures say more than the bar; the star and Profit column drop 20 px earlier in de/ru/tr. The Money tab's labels wrap so the figures keep their room. The Lines Money column fits a figure in billions in every language (Anita: "ok we can widen").

- 2026-11-09 (Anita, T-2963): New Zealand is one realm, "New Zealand", replacing T-1738's North Island and South Island: "why are north island and south island NZ two separate regions when australia is one? lol". 5M people in 6 zones is one realm's worth; island realms stay where an island holds tens of millions (Indonesia, the Philippines).
- 2026-11-10 (agent busloop, T-2899): bus and ferry loop lines. A loop is a bus route of four points or more whose last point is its first, or a ferry line of four stops or more whose last port is its first; nothing else stores it, so no save field and no save level (as T-798 stores a train loop). In the service it is a train circle (`LoopKind::Circle`), so it runs both ways, a vehicle each way on an even fleet, with the line's dwell at the first stop and demand riding on round. Both ways because that is what a circle does everywhere else, and one-way needs a per-run service in demand, the boarding account and the renderer: it stays T-798 L3, which now covers buses and boats too, and is probably the first thing a player of a bus loop will ask for. The first stop and the closing stop are one stop (the station nearest the closing point within 200 m, else a plain stop; a ring with no stop between gets one at half its length). In both tools a click on the other end, with three points or more, closes the loop and makes the line in one click (a closed route has nothing left to add), which replaces switching the active end by that click; with two points it still switches. A closed route has no active end: a right click on its first point opens it, and taking a point out of a ring of three leaves an open route of two. A bus loop drawn out and back along one road counts as a loop.
- 2026-11-09 (agent staletab, T-2962): a publish and an 8800 rebuild keep the previous build's hashed files (assets/, fonts/) beside the new build's and delete only older ones (`tools/keep_assets.py`, `build-files.json` lists a build's own files), so a tab open across a publish can still load its language messages and workers; a failed lazy load shows "There's a new version of the game." with a Reload button that saves first. One build back is enough: a tab older than that is asked to reload.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramlook, T-2928): street tram track pays half the hills and water surcharges (Anita: "maybe lets do half hills and water surcharge"); off-street tram track and rail unchanged. `TrackSpec::surcharge_mult` 0.5 for StreetTram; the preview multiplier's base is now its own `price_scale` (1, 1.5 for high speed), so a street route's multiplier stays the level figure plus what is added. notes/T-2921.md.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramlook, T-2927): rounding harder. Refusals came from the street path doubling back (a U-turn in two turns across a divided road's median, or past a junction and back) and from corners so sharp a 25 m curve passes over 30 m from them. Now the search never turns back more than 135 degrees within 60 m of street; the fit tries 10, 20, then 40 m and takes the first whose worst point is within 1.5x its tolerance or 45 m (a 25 m curve round a 135 degree corner passes 40 m off). To keep the looser fit honest the route carries the street line (`set_route_street`), and any curve or straight lying more than 10 m from it is off-street tram track, not only curves by their own shape (a straight across a dropped corner stayed street before). Chosen over a single looser tolerance (40 m from the start straightened whole routes) and over cutting a straying straight at its exact stretch (a joint-placement rule of its own; whole legs are what the corner-run machinery already cuts). Sweep: 83/93 placed to 93/93. notes/T-2921.md.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramlook, T-2927): the tram look. Street and off-street tram track look alike (Anita: "i feel like i dont want different look for on street vs off street"); the off-street centre line is gone. Tram strokes and the lines over them are 0.75 of rail's width (from 0.6, so the wires fit), with two wires in the map's ink (black in light, near white in dark) once a stroke is 3-5 px wide, one along each track once they part. Blueprint tram track gets them only once its tracks part: between its two blue rails two more lines read as four stripes. notes/T-2921.md.

- 2026-11-10 (agent tramsnap, T-2927): rounded corners. A street route is placed as pieces: each curve whose middle lies more than 5 m (`OFF_STREET_M`) from its two legs (the street line it was fitted to) is its own piece of Tram track (kind 3, 0.5x, cap 80), cut 1 m beyond each end of its arc; the rest is StreetTram (0.1x, cap 60). Every right-angle corner is off-street (25 m: 7.3 m off), a 60 degree one up to 37 m and a 20 degree bend up to 329 m stay street. Where no joint fits between such a curve and the next (a street corner's arc, a ramp, under 20 m of straight to an end or to the next off-street curve), the off-street piece takes it in. Chosen over a per-edge off-street share (a new saved field to keep right through splits and edits) and over cutting mid-arc (a node per cut, exact to the millimetre): with whole edges, price, upkeep, speed cap, the inspector and the quote follow from the kind with no new code. Street track reshaped by hand becomes Tram track (it no longer follows the street). Off-street tram track draws high-speed track's thin centre line on track strokes; street tram track has none. notes/T-2921.md.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramsnap, T-2927): street snap. The search is over directed road segments with 200 m per turn over 30 degrees and no turn sharper than 135 degrees, no motorways, from and to the 12 nearest roads by each click (3 m per metre from the click, as the bus tool's free end: the nearest road is often a stub joined to nothing). The fit is `Tracer` with `tight()`: a street's line has a vertex only at each junction, so after the rounds each corner gets the radius that passes 10 m from its junction (at least 25 m, at most the 60 km/h radius of 253 m); tolerance 10 m, then 20, taken when the worst point is within 1.5 times it. A pointer with no street within 100 m is an ordinary hand-drawn segment (off-street Tram track), not a refusal; no road path uses the bus tool's tag, no fit says "The streets are too tight here. Add a point on the way." Per-kind smallest radius: `geom::fit_min`, used by the edge check, the stroke cut, the tracer and bridges (`bridge::with_min_radius`; Zurich's street bridges were refused as ground over water without it); rail is unchanged. notes/T-2921.md.

- 2026-11-10 (agent tramapp, T-2926): the app's tram slice. Tram is a third track type (`tramTrack` in ui.ts; `trackType` is 2, "hsr" or "tram"); picking it saves the level and sets 0, hides the ramp switch, the Double/Quad pair and Target max speed, and putting Regular back restores the level. The Build tab's Tram button picks the track tool plus the type and has no key (Y is Cities). Hand drawn tram is kind 3 only; no Ctrl flip until T-2927.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramapp, T-2926): the family rule in the tool is a filter: with Tram picked, train nodes and edges are not snap targets for the track tool and the reverse (also Snap beside); the station tool still sees both, and a stop takes its platform length from the track it lands on (rail 60-400 m, tram 20-80, default 40, each remembered). The issue text for PlatformLength and RadiusTooSmall follows the kind in hand (`onTram()` in issues.ts).
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramapp, T-2926): a tram line is told by its first stop's node kind: `addLine` gives it the `kind_defaults` dwell and turnaround and 2 cars; its car count is capped at 4 and no longer sets the next train line's length. Tram cars are a fifth fleet with their own Money rows (owned, spare), `fleetTram` in the header (only when nonzero); `trainKind(m, fast, tram)` prices them as US$2M a car with a train's running cost.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramapp, T-2926): sim changes made on the way. (1) Tram kinds paid road crossings in the sim although Anita's rule is none: `edge_price`, the tile loader and `road_charge_of` now skip them for kinds whose `road_crossings` is false. (2) The quote row for tram cars is kind 15, not 14 (14 is the ferry routes' metres, so the two collided). (3) Tram track rows carry `TRAM_ROW` (4) in the track-count field so the quote's hover prices them by the tram table, not rail's.
- 2026-11-10 (agent tramapp, T-2926): map look. `F_TRAM` (4096) narrows track and the lines over it to 0.6 of a rail line, no centre stripe; a tram line's `lineCars` is its sections plus 0.5 (`TRAM_MARK`) so the car shader tells it from a train (readers use `carsOf`), drawn 2.4 m wide with no gap between sections; a tram stop's dot below zoom 14 is 0.7 of a station's, its box is its true length from 14, and its name waits for zoom 13.

- 2026-11-09 (T-2935): "held out of service" counts whole trains the line owns and never runs at that level (rounded down; it was rounded to nearest, so 0 could print), and a dot is marked as holding trains back only for a whole train. A share under one train still runs part of the time, so it gets no line. Trains standing in the queue for full track are not counted: they are in service, and show as "Waiting at busy track" in the round trip.

- 2026-11-09 (Anita, T-2919, overturning T-2186's "a bus stays 80, rated and full alike" and "buses are settled": "i think bus crowding actually can be like train crowding. i dont see a reason for it not to be. ferries too. i think itd be nice to have them directly comparable ... lets just say buses are unsettled"): every vehicle's rated load is two thirds of its crush, as a train car's 160 of 240. A bus keeps its crush of 80 and is now a third of a car (was half): rated 53, 15 seats (was 22; agent's call, so the bus is one multiple of a car in demand, the sim and the app). Crowding is minded from 45 aboard (85% of rated; a train's is 70%, 37 would have been the same), `BUS_CROWD_START`, T-2922: Anita, "since so many people have played with buses and calibrated to how they currently work, id rather just increase the poitn at which crowding starts ... targetting less than a 10% drop". A boat stays 320 of 480. Demand's crowding weights, the dots, the gauge and Average load read the same on all three. On real-ferries.save with seven bus lines at 8/10/20 minutes, weekday bus riders go 85,883 to 78,231 with the later start (-8.9%; -14% at 70%), ferries stay 51,113. The dots stay a share of the rated load on all three. notes/T-2919.md.
- 2026-11-09 (Anita, T-2919): a new bus line starts at a bus every 8, 15 and 30 minutes (was 8, 10 and 20: "a bit much"); trains stay at none ("sometimes u wanna make a line and then decide on train scheduling later"), ferries at 20, 30 and none ("i liek that"). Prices unchanged.
- 2026-11-09 (agent schedunify, T-2919): Delete line asks only where it cannot be undone, a constructed bus route; a train line now goes at once as a ferry line does (an undo brings it back). A schedule copied to or from a bus or ferry line carries only its vehicles or headways. A bus panel's "Dwell at stops" is worked out in the app (the line's dwell at each train station between the ends, 20 s at each other stop), not asked of the sim. The bus and ferry panel's sections follow the train panel's order.

- 2026-11-09 (agent marketwindow, T-2878): the market window takes its pairs from the market part it sits beside only when both serve the same zones and join them by track the same way (compared by which zones share a component, since component numbers move on an edit). An edit that really changes either still deals from nothing: the far deal runs over served origin zones in order, so patching only what moved would be a much larger change, and exact figures were the condition. The part is borrowed for the one call, so no copy of the tables is kept (32-bit browsers, T-2867).
- 2026-11-09 (agent schemwidth, T-2888): the diagram pauses the game while it is up (any way of leaving it, including the build keys and tools, goes through one signal). Leaving starts the clock again only if the diagram paused it and it is still paused, so a player who was paused stays paused, and one who pressed play or a speed while it was up keeps running; the speed is the clock's own and never touched. The tutorial needs nothing: a new game starts paused so the diagram leaves it so, and the "play" step keeps its tick once met. A pause from something else while it is up (the graphics notice) is not told apart from ours and is lifted on leaving; that is rare enough to leave.
