The line width slider runs 1x to 1.2x and is applied when painting, not in the layout: strokes, pills, passages and casings scale together, the neighbour gap goes from 0.22 to 0.04 of a pitch, and names keep their places, so at the largest a station's pill sits a little closer to its name. Wider than 1.2x would make neighbouring lines touch without a bigger pitch, which means a new layout.
- 2026-11-09 (agent busstops, T-2885): a bus line's stop spacing is one per-line number, a stepper in the bus panel (Auto, 400 m to 100 km, or typed); automatic stays the default and saves and old lines don't change. Not adding and removing individual stops, which is bigger UI and can come later (T-2891). A line with its own spacing skips stations when a station is more than about half a spacing from its place, so a long-distance bus can pass a city's stations; in Auto every station on the route is still a stop. Two costs make a wide spacing a real choice: a stop catches homes along only 400 m of route each way, and a hop drops the 20 s stand of each 400 m it no longer stops at (capped at 40% of the hop), so spacing buys speed (about a quarter faster at 2 km) and loses people between stops. A stop itself has no price, as before; a stop that goes away stops catching riders through `cover`. Highway speeds are separate work.
- 2026-11-09 (agent firefox2, T-2867): a 32-bit browser is told by behaviour, a resizable ArrayBuffer's 4 GB maximum being refused, since 32-bit Firefox's user agent says "Win64; x64". It starts with 1 solving worker whatever "Use more cores" says, and no focus pool, with intercity trips on: New York's real save fits (367 MB and 65 MB) and 2 solving workers do not. Out of memory on any browser halves the solving workers (figures unchanged) before anything is shown; the start has to be right as well, because a forced pool of 5 in real 32-bit Firefox fell to 1 and still ran out, its address space left in pieces. "Play without intercity trips" replaces "Play without demand" on a memory failure while there are intercity trips to drop, and lasts for the tab (sessionStorage) so that one bad evening does not remove them for good; no demand cache in such a tab, as on phones. "Play without demand" stays for failures that are not memory, and for memory failures with intercity trips already off.
- 2026-11-09 (agent builtday, T-2871): the save records one number per constructed station, edge and line: the game day it was first built, for a later playback, in a trailing `LBD1` block (save level 6). An edit never makes old track look new: a split's or a station's two halves, a join (the earliest of the parts), a redraw or upgrade keep what they had, undo and redo restore the exact day because it lives in the same data they put back, and genuinely new track takes today. Blueprint has no day until it is constructed; Construct is immediate (there is no building time), so the day pressed is the day built, and a day is `Network::today`, which the clock worker sets from the game clock before each edit (`TrackApi::set_game_day`). Sandbox games record the same. A line's day is the day it was added (a playback can still hold it back until its pieces are built), a bus or ferry line's the day its route was constructed; buildings are not dated. An older save reads as "before recording began" (`DAY_UNKNOWN`), so a later playback shows it from the start; a network with no known day writes no block at all, so an older save saves back byte for byte. A damaged `LBD1` costs only the days, never the game. Read: `TrackApi::edge_built_days`, `station_built_days`, `line_built_days`.
- 2026-11-09 (agent quickbits, T-2860): the currency picker has 44 currencies, one per currency of a country the game has that is not tiny (a euro-area country is EUR; the rates are round and fixed like T-2798's). USD, EUR, GBP, JPY, CNY, KRW, CAD, AUD, INR and MXN come first, the rest by code. Dollars and pesos that are not US$ carry their own symbol (C$, A$, NZ$, HK$, S$, NT$, MX$, AR$, CL$, CO$) and a symbol ending in a letter its space ("CHF 2.90"). A player with no stored choice starts on the currency of the first browser language that has a region (es-MX gives MXN, en-GB GBP, de-DE EUR); plain "es" or an unknown region gives USD, and the start city is never used. Every setting is stored whether chosen or not, so `VERSION` 14 drops a stored USD once (a stored other currency stays). Each currency's `grain` must exceed rate x 0.0012 (the game stores a fare to about 0.0012 US$), or a typed fare reads back a step off (NOK at 0.01 did). `price()` rounds to the grain in every currency, so a fare shows on the step it is typed on. `moneyCash` drops a figure, then goes to thousands, when a long symbol would make the top bar's figure over 10 characters; the box is 6em in another currency.
- 2026-11-09 (agent crashfix, T-2820): a save with a broken line keeps the line broken on load, by pointing its deleted edge or stop at one dead slot, in place of dropping the line or refusing the save. That is the state the game was in when it saved, and the player can see and repair the line. A panic while a save opens shows the Rust message in the notice, English in every language as `clockClient.cannotStart` does, because a player's paste of that text is what located this bug.
- 2026-11-09 (agent reviewfix, T-2840): Map detail has no Auto; High is the default for everyone and a stored "auto" reads as High. Auto sampled 40 moving frames and went Low for the rest of the page load, so one slow zoom just after a load blurred the map and dropped the small streets for good. Low was measured (notes/T-2840.md, real New York save at DPR 2, and an emulated phone with a first line, interleaved): it saves no resident memory, the JS heap and WASM are the same, and the committed GPU bytes fall about 470 MB on desktop (15% of Chrome's committed total) and about 100 MB on the phone setup (4%); frame times at 4x CPU throttle are 0-2.5 ms better. Low stays as a manual choice.
- 2026-11-09 (agent reviewfix, T-2833, T-2838, T-2839): 4 GB desktops keep the market and the demand cache, as before 309ff65b; the next two T-2802 and T-2794 entries below say `smallDevice()` for them, which now holds only for worker counts, the focus pool and the lean map. The market, cache and no-market note follow `mobileDevice()` (a phone or tablet by user agent). On a phone the Settings toggle "Calculate intercity trips" (per browser, off) opens the market again, from the next solve. A browser that reports `navigator.deviceMemory` of 4 or less, on any device, gets a note that the game probably will not run well.

- 2026-11-08 (agent mobile, T-2802): a small device (`smallDevice()`) opens no market pack, in or out of the tutorial, so a phone has no trips between zones. The market took its solving worker from 33 to 184 MB of WASM and the page's total on a first line from about 650 to 1,010 MB. Off for the whole game and not only the tutorial so a phone's figures do not jump, and its memory with them, the moment the tutorial closes. The cost: the real New York save reads 2.8% fewer rail trips on a phone than on desktop (notes/T-2794.md). The other reading is a phone opening the market after the tutorial, one `smallDevice()` check in `marketZones` and one in `stationsAhead`.

- 2026-11-08 (agent mobile, T-2794): a phone's lean ways use the existing `smallDevice()` (a phone or tablet by user agent, or 4 GB or less), one threshold for all of them, since a phone's tab is limited whatever the phone holds. On such a device: pixel ratio capped at 2 (not 1.5: DPR 2 phones already ran with MSAA, and 2 keeps it), coarse outlines compact and held only near the view (no coarse zone outlines at world zoom), the realm shade's worker started per cut, no demand cache (a phone reloading a save solves again). A WebGL context still lost after 6 s pauses the game and offers a reload; phones often get one back sooner. Desktop is unchanged. Figures and what is still over: notes/T-2794.md.

- 2026-11-08 (agent currency, T-2798): the currency setting is display only, at fixed round rates in one table (`app/src/game/currency.ts`): 1 USD = 0.8 GBP, 0.9 EUR, 7 RMB, 150 JPY, 1,300 KRW (market rates of 2025-26, rounded, never updated, so a figure reads the same on any day). Every saved and simulated amount stays US$; `game/format.ts` multiplies on the way out and divides on the way back in. Rounding follows the shown currency: prices in two decimals, yen to 1 and won to 10 (a fare typed or stepped to stays on that grid, so it reads back as typed after the game's 1e-4 storage rounding at New York's scale); steps are 0.25 (pound, euro), 2 (RMB), 50 (yen), 500 (won) for a fare and a cent-like step for rates per km (0.01, 0.05, 1, 10); "$0" under $500 is "0" under 500 of the shown currency, and the loss red follows that line. Compact figures add T (兆) above B for yen and won. RMB and yen both use "¥"; the picker says "RMB ¥" and "JPY ¥". The leaderboard's points are not money and stay as they were.

- 2026-11-08 (agent's call, T-2787): a line's money history plots fares minus running cost per day, the past 7 whole days, not profit with upkeep: the panel's Profit row subtracts the line's upkeep as it is now, and upkeep was never kept per line per day, so a past day's figure with it would be made up. The save keeps 8 days a line (the day now, unfinished, is kept but not drawn); a week keeps the save's growth to about 60 bytes a line gzipped. Revisit if Anita wants upkeep in it: it would need a per-line upkeep share kept each day.

- 2026-11-07 (agent newgame, T-2748): Anita's "20% on the up side and 60% on the downside" read with the score (trips a km², higher is easier): medium takes 20% of its count from easy and 60% from hard, so the plentiful reds give most; the other reading is the two shares in `pipeline/start_difficulty.py` swapped (counts in notes/T-2748.md).
- 2026-11-07 (agent newgame, T-2748): the real network's faint lines start at zoom 6, not 4: at 4 a view of Europe drew 41 regions' tiles (about 0.6 px wide), most of a frame's CPU and GPU time on the map a new game picks a city on; line widths keep their old zoom stops.

- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2625): the diagram's 2D condensation lowers the plan (the floor falls from each network's own scale as the square root of distance, a fall a map with 2:1 stretch can draw) instead of loosening the fold guard: a looser guard (4:1) with a faster fall gave folds, no settling and networks drawn at half their ask. Networks are pulled 3 times as hard to their size there and drawn somewhat over their ask (New York 8.4 of 6.8 pitches a km); far land is about 4.5 times smaller than the networks, not 10-20.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2716): both WASM modules allocate through `sim/src/heap.rs`'s `Heap`, the `dlmalloc` crate (std's wasm32 allocator) over a system side that grows the memory by at least 32 MB (clock worker: or 1/8 of it), with its `GlobalAlloc` methods never inlined. It replaces T-1594/T-2699's pad allocated and freed inside std's allocator: that grew the same way but exposed an LLVM fold of dlmalloc's inlined header check (an abort in the release profile), and any inlined allocator can expose it, so the allocator stays out of line.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2681): registry cities (not zones) show in ja, zh-Hans and zh-TW by their Wikidata labels, kept in `i18n/place-names.json` by metro id (outside game/*.json, which joins rewrite) and bundled (37 KB). A city is matched by its shown name to the best-linked place item within 60 km; a weak or missing match falls back to places near the centre whose label starts with the name; three hand pins in `tools/place_names.mjs`. No label: the Latin name. The admin suffix is dropped as players say it (東京 not 東京都, 上海 not 上海市, 釜山 not 釜山広域市), kept where the Latin name has "City" (吉林市).
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2691): a map AJAXError with status 0 (the fetch failed outright, mostly a basemap tile from tiles.openfreemap.org fetched in MapLibre's worker) is a console warning like T-1510's TypeError; one with an HTTP status stays an error, now written out with status and URL instead of the bare minified "Nn" (`warnOnNetworkFailure`, map/mapView.ts).

- 2026-11-06 (Anita, T-2693 follow-up): the bunny's train is back on the main loading card (ui/LoadingScreen.tsx), the one shown while a save or game loads at the start; the pill ring stays on every small status (status tag, diagram tag). Anita: "i didnt mean to get rid of the bunny train from the main loading panel. we should keep it in the one case when we are loading a save, at the beginning. just dont want itfor the small loading things that display elsewhere". The small bunny of the status tag stays gone. The small pill ring is 13 px (was 20), Anita: "35% smaller radius ... quite discreet". The failure screen is centred with a Back to start button beside Try again when the address names a game.

- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2695): `world_plan.country_units` takes a unit of the chosen level that the units taken
  leave half of, as well as those whose representative point lies in a first-level unit: a coastal unit whose point fell
  outside the simplified first-level coast was in no unit (11 of Buenos Aires's comunas, Avellaneda, Ushuaia). The cached
  plan state got Argentina's 14 lost units by hand (backup `data/work/worldplan/state.pre-T2695.pkl`) and bue the
  comunas and Avellaneda under the plan lock; shipped Brazil and Mexico have the same gaps (1.46M people), left for
  T-2702. notes/T-2695.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2695, T-2696, T-2697): Argentina, Chile, Colombia, Peru, Ecuador, South Africa, Saudi
  Arabia and Oman are zoned on Brazil's reach rule (towns in empty land; at the default 20 a km2 Argentina left 10.5M

- 2026-11-07 (agent difficulty, T-2690; Anita 2026-11-06: "we can maybe weight weakly by fare money. like ^0.2", "even thirds of well known cities"): a start's tier is its weekday trip legs a km² with both ends within 10 km of its centre, times (fare scale over the US's)^0.2, cut at thirds of the 408 well-known starts (every metro built as a city, the suggested starts, zones with over 1.5M people in the circle). 10 km fits the first view; 5 and 15 km ranked alike (Spearman 0.89, 0.95). Trips between packs and attraction visits are left out (11 of 408 have over a quarter of the circle in another pack). A metro is measured at its OSM city of the same name nearest the registry centre, since 112 registry centres are over 5 km off (Atlanta 23 km). The coloured dots last until any line, bus or ferry included, runs with service, in every game, not only a map start: the colours matter before a first line wherever the player looks. Notes: notes/T-2690.md.

- 2026-11-07 (agent loadui, T-2693): the bunny train is gone; loading shows a pill like the map's trains (darker accent rim, accent fill, bent along a ring, a CSS turn of 1.1 s) on the card and before every status text. Every status tag has a bar. Measures: "Loading data" its real share; a zone wait and bubbles on their way the zone's share when it has one; recomputing, behind, capped and click-waits-on-a-solve the solve's rounds published of all (`demandStep`); terrain downloads finished of the peak waiting (time when only one); anything else, or a solve not yet begun, the share of how long the last such wait took, capped at 95%, 3 s the first time (an honest "about as long as last time", never a made-up percentage; only the load's bar has a percentage hover). The small `.spin` dot in line rows, the purpose breakdown and the map's selected-bubble outline stays: no room for a bar. The busy tag (a frozen page) has neither ring nor bar: nothing can move while it shows.
- 2026-11-07 (agent loadui, T-2694): a region's loading bar is at most 0.5 of the region's width on screen (box corners projected), kept between 24 and 96 px (the old fixed width is the top), so a small region at world zoom gets a short bar and a region wider than 192 px gets the old one.
- 2026-11-07 (agent keepnames, T-2683): a complex has no stored name, only its stations' (and an optional building's); the "lost" complex name was the Stations tab row and the diagram taking the member with the most lines, which a new station or line can change. A station name the player typed (rename, or the cycle button) is now flagged (`StationData::named`, save node-flag bit 8) and `leadOf` puts a typed name before the line count. Split and merge move no name: each part shows its own typed member, else its busiest. Two typed names in one complex both stay on the map; the row and diagram show the busiest one's, since a merge leaves no record of which complex was bigger (a "bigger one's name" rule would need a stored complex identity; not worth it). Old saves have no flags, so their names fall back to the lines rule until retyped. Load's capital-letters tidy skips typed names; a line named exactly "<feed key> <word>" (up, go, nr, osm...) with no symbol is still tidied on load, since lines carry no flag (only pre-T-1442 saves need it; drop it if the real save is rebuilt). A line group's name typed equal to its first line's name is now kept instead of cleared.

- 2026-11-06 (agent l10npolish, T-2680): zh-TW has no bold hanzi. Huninn ships only 400, so bold was the browser's made-up bold, which fills dense hanzi (圓圈, 顯), worst in dark. A real bold borrowed from the system (`@font-face` Huninn 700 from local() Noto Sans TC Bold, Microsoft JhengHei Bold, PingFang TC Semibold) came out lighter than Huninn's regular, so headings looked weaker than the rows. So `font-synthesis-weight: none` under `:root:lang(zh-TW)` and `.h` headings at 16px there (their `.right` figures stay 14px); Nunito's Latin and digits keep real bold. ja (Zen Maru 700) and zh-Hans (YaHei / PingFang bold) have real bold faces and were left alone. Shots: artifacts/trainworld/l10npolish/fonts/.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10npolish, T-2680): the Cities list is one CSS grid (subgrid rows and heads), so a column is as wide as its widest cell, its head included; the old fixed widths are minimums. A head longer in another language (ja ポイント, 48 px in a 40 px column) widens its column on every row rather than running into the next. Rows without a star now end their names where starred rows do. The Lines and Stations lists keep fixed widths (none overflows yet); the same grid fits them if one does.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10npolish, T-2680): ja "Riders" is 乗客 on both the Lines and Stations tabs (stationsTab.riders was 利用者), the glossary's first rendering, so neighbouring tabs agree; 利用者 stays in station hovers and the demand selector's station rows.

- 2026-11-06 (agent l10nunits, T-2672; the orchestrator's call that ja/zh take ICU's forms): short counts and money outside English are the value English shows, to the same number of significant digits, through ICU compact notation (English "763k" is 76.3万, "$74.0M" $7400万, "5k" 5000), so no language claims more or less precision than English; "$" stays before the figure rather than ICU's "US$" for zh. Durations are a table of our own (`DURATION_UNITS`, intl.ts), since `Intl.DurationFormat` is missing from some browsers and ICU's unit style puts a space in "5.5 分": 4分8秒, 1小时1分, a zero last part dropped, a whole minute in zh 分钟/分鐘 to match `inspector.min`. es and de, when they come, take ICU compact for figures and English's durations until given words. The language's messages are awaited at the top level of i18n/index.ts, over a wait in main.ts alone, so modules that make text at import wait too; English pays nothing.

- 2026-11-06 (Anita, agent fareref's calls on the details, T-2679): the Money tab's country fare multiples are of the reference country's fares, not each country's own default. The reference is the country of the constructed station with the lowest node id (a save from before: its oldest surviving station), pinned in `Fares.ref` when the game first has one, so deleting that station later moves nothing; with nothing built the panel uses the start zone's country, else the home city's. It is display only: switching it changes no stored scale, and `ref` is kept out of demand's network and the save's demand cache key, so no fare, income or solve changes. Own scales stay stored as multiples of each country's default (so a switch needs no rewrite), now unrounded: a set multiple is rounded to 0.01x of the reference and stored as whatever makes it charge exactly that (T-2021's rule moved from the country's default to the reference); a save without `ref` still has its scales rounded as it loads, so it charges what it did. The control is an anchor icon with a hover, after the stepper and before the undo. The stepper now goes to the next 0.05x up or down (0.63x to 0.65x) since defaults are odd multiples. Where the reference charges its round default fares (T-1662), "2.50x" of it is 2.50 times its scale, which can differ from 2.50 times its rounded fares by the rounding.
- 2026-11-06 (agent namespread, T-2674; Anita: "lets spread them"): the diagram's names are picked to cover the view, not busiest first. The busiest name with a place in view goes first; each next one tried is the one with the highest score, the square root of its stations times its distance on screen to the nearest name already shown (`SPREAD_RANK` 0.5 in cityNames.ts; 1 was stations times distance and still crowded the east coast, 0 is distance alone); a name that does not fit is dropped and does not count toward the distance. The budget, 4 to 40 names at 150,000 CSS px squared each, and the clearance rules are as T-2640. In the game page's 1100 by 820 canvas whole North America now names New York, Los Angeles, Chicago, Denver, Seattle and Toronto (2 on the Pacific side, 1 in the middle) where it named San Francisco, Los Angeles, Chicago, New York, Boston and Washington DC; whole Japan names Tokyo, Kansai, Nagoya, Fukuoka, Sapporo and Iwate. checks/t2438-city-names.mjs gains a spread check on the whole drawing (NA: 2 in the west third, 1 in the middle, 1 in the east; Japan: one in each third north to south); its other expectations did not encode the order.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita's ask, T-2675): diagram zone borders are drawn within 30 km of built track (was 50) and a zone's whole outline when over 50% of its border would show (was 30%): `trackKm` and `wholeShare` in diagram/sheetLines.ts. Anita: "i think the distance threshold we're using might be too big actually. maybe we drop to 30km and raise threhsold to 50%".
- 2026-11-06 (agent searchleaf, T-2652): the strategy search takes leaf stations out of the queue (T-2642's (i')) but not the exit step ((i''), a run's first node settled with the node it rides into): on Japan the step made the searches 1.60x faster against 1.56x, and moved a handful of loads by one float rounding where (i') keeps every load to the bit, which later speed A/Bs rely on. There is no `?perfOff=` switch for it: no app setting reaches the kernel without a new WASM call; `kernel::set_plain_search` turns it off natively. notes/T-2652.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10nfix, T-2655): a phrase a message puts straight after a word ("Trips per day{where}") goes in through `tSpaced`, a space in front in languages that space words and none in Japanese and Chinese, and items in a row with no "and" through `tSeries` (", " or "、"); both over rewriting each message as a select on an empty phrase, since the reviewed translations were written for pieces with no space. English reads as before.
- 2026-11-06 (maps-b9's call, done by agent l10nfix, T-2655): English dates and times follow the browser's first English locale (en-GB's 14:05), else en-US's; English numbers stay en-US's. A browser in another language that picks English gets en-US dates, not its own language's month names inside English text.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10nfix, T-2655): the Terminal upgrade's hover and the reversing hovers did not disagree: a line ending at a through station turns 15 trains an hour on its platform at the default 3 minute turnaround (180 + 60 s) and 30 in a terminal's two sidings; trains reversing partway are held 300 s a side (12 an hour), 120 s with a terminal (30). The Terminal hover now gives both.

- 2026-11-06 (Anita's ask, details agent's call, T-2639): the diagram draws all water again (T-2609's drop-out reversed) and a zone border only within 50 km on the ground of built track (chamfer distance on a ground grid, within 8%), still only beside a zone a line passes through; a zone a line passes through with more than 30% of its zone-to-zone border (coasts not counted) drawn that way gets its whole outline. Zones no line passes through never go whole: allowing it drew extra outlines (New York's Lehigh, Lackawanna, Albany). Applied on city maps too: New York's look is unchanged, as nearly all of its land is within 50 km of track. notes/T-2639.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent stopnote, T-2657): when demand stops for good the old red tag under the demand buttons is dropped for the top-centre notice (the status tag already gave way to it); the pause is in `loadFailed` (game/loading.ts), once, after startup only (a failed startup keeps its own screen and choices); the notice is not a modal and the clock can be run again. Reload page waits up to 1.5 s for the autosave's mark, since the page-hide flush alone may be cut off by a reload.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10n-lead, T-2636): a language is offered (the Language row, and a first visit's pick from the browser) only once its whole translation is reviewed: `OFFERED` in `i18n/intl.ts` is English alone until then, and `?lang=<code>` previews all four; a part-translated UI looks worse than English. The row sits at the top of Settings under the icon buttons, a globe icon (hover "Language" and the AI note) and a select, so a player who can't read the current language finds it first.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10n-lead, T-2636): fonts by language: Traditional Chinese gets Huninn (rounded like Zen Maru, self-hosted Google slices, 2.5 MB of which a page loads only the slices used); Simplified Chinese the system face, as no rounded free Simplified face exists to host; Chinese never falls into Zen Maru. CJK text carries no space beside Latin or digits: the page's `text-autospace` adds the gap.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10n-lead, T-2636): glossary settled for ja, zh-Hans, zh-TW (notes per term in `i18n/glossary.json`); calls someone might revisit: zh-TW trip 旅次 (出行 reads mainland in Taiwan), capacity never 運量 in zh-TW (it means ridership there), complex 乗換駅 / 换乘站 / 轉乘站 (站群 is SEO jargon), Construct 着工 / 开工 / 動工 kept apart from Build, running costs 運行費用 in zh-TW (營運 would include upkeep), zh-Hans passing track 越行线.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10n-lead, T-2636): the translation review is blind back-translation (a Sonnet agent that never sees the English) plus one Opus reviewer per unit for meaning and naturalness, not two separate reviewers; the test run found no meaning errors and 13-17 rewrites per language in 91 keys, so a separate naturalness pass was not worth its cost (runbook: docs/localization.md "Translating").

- 2026-11-06 (Anita's yes to T-2621's option c2, details agent's call, T-2641): a far trip's end zone is sampled more coarsely the farther it goes, and a far city's long-distance riders arrive at its stations in lumps (one zone's station can show twice its far riders and the next none; totals by line and by city hold, a few small lines of 1,000-3,000 riders move 2-5%). The market's chunk is `MARKET_CHUNK x clamp(d / 50 km, 1, 16)` and place pairs 250 km or more apart are dealt once over both places' zones, as prototyped in T-2621; each pair is still priced on its own zones' stations, so rail's share is unbiased (NA rail -0.002%). Details I chose: the far place pair's run is dealt over the served origin zones only (dealing over every origin zone would price every zone pair of the far places once per network), so a new served origin zone shifts the later origin zones' far pairs by sampling noise; `?perfOff=farChunks` (job flag `nearChunks`, wasm `set_far_chunks`) gives T-780's chunk at every distance. NA's pass 1.9x (pairs halved), Japan 1.2x (its time is the searches, T-2642). notes/T-2641.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent l10n-ui1, T-2632): in the moved ui/ A-L strings the Credits licence notices stay English (docs/localization.md: the English is the binding text; only the "Data sources" heading is a message, the lines carry `i18n-ignore`); a count that was `n trains` or `n riders` with no plural is `{count, plural, one {# train} other {# trains}}` where the count is a plain integer (group trains hover: "1 trains" is now "1 train"), and left as it was where the count is formatted text such as "12.3k"; a day-type tail (" on weekdays") is a select on `dayed` over two whole sentences, and `splitTip`'s `where` still comes with its leading space (core's `game/splitTip.ts` and `AllCitiesView` read it); `ALL_CITIES` stays as the English constant, `allCitiesName()` is the translated name; weekday names come from `i18n/calendar.ts` `weekdayLong`, not a list per file.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita's ask, details agent's call, T-2629): the diagram's cut narrows an empty band by half every 20 drawn pitches, twice the sheet field's own limit (T-2609 kept 40): on her North America save the Los Angeles zone's empty north-east is drawn about 15% smaller each way; the sheet's last rounds settle less (stations still moving up to 7 pitches a round), with no folds and no station off its land. notes/T-2629.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2629): sheet zone borders are fitted straying up to 24 pitches from the traced border (was 6, and less near a station), with each station kept on its side by a test on every run (no station between the traced border and the run, a pitch clear) rather than by a tight fit, and each run kept on the bordering zones' land. A border may now lie up to 24 pitches off the zones' real outline; at 12 New York's north-west border was still stepped. Bends per 100 pitches of border: New York 4.2 -> 1.3, Japan 5.8 -> 1.4, North America 3.5 -> 1.1.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-428): `intercity.json` moves to `data/work/` and nothing in the publish path (vite.config.ts, freeze.py, deploy.py, build_inputs.py) names the registry's `market.pack` any more; the registry keeps the `pack` field (unread), and older freezes keep their hard link until they rotate out. T-2380 still deletes the file and the unused no-`--packs` mode of joint_gravity.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2605): while a whole solve runs, the zone in view going fine or edited is solved aside by a one-worker focus pool and shown from it (figures, bubbles, and lines the shown result has none for) until the main pool's solve of that network shows it. The lie: the zone's figures leave out the market and other zones' crowding (Salt Lake City 1.0% and 0.3% off the main pool's); lines already shown keep the old figures, since from the zone alone they read about 25% low (T-2624). Finals unchanged; no aside on small devices. notes/T-2605.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2609): the network diagram condenses a continent by cutting empty bands (columns and rows of the sheet more than 10 km across from any station with 2 others within 10 km drawn narrow, halving every 40 drawn pitches, the field's own limit) rather than by shrinking far land in 2D: a 2D shrink needs land rings stretched 10:1 or more round each city, which the sheet's fold guard forbids and its solver does not settle under (notes/T-2609.md). Only on maps 600 km across or more; on New York's map it shortened Long Island (shape kept 0.88 -> 0.81). Zone borders in a cut band are drawn as thin slivers.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita's ask, details agent's call, T-2609): the diagram draws only borders of zones a line passes through (a station in it or a hop's track across it); on a map 600 km across or more, water only where the nearest land is such a zone's (the rest plain background, edge fitted octilinear), and land farther than 25 km from every network station costs as little as water to stretch unevenly (25 km, not 10-12, because at 12 km Shikoku's shape moved).
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2613): Construct is refused while any terrain file, water mask, the world water or a road tile under the blueprint could not be downloaded ("The terrain here did not load. Try again.", the roads' own line), rather than priced as mountains and refunded later: the old flat-price fallback let a bad connection build mountain track at the flat price. The check covers the whole blueprint even when one piece is constructed. Previews still quote flat ground until the file lands. Downloads give up after 60 s.

- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2614): a change that has not reached the account for 2 minutes shows on the existing yellow "Saved only in this browser" dot, hover "Not on your account yet!" and the reason, a click saving by hand; no bar or card, since the game is safe in the browser and resent every minute.

- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2617): String of pearls is a running rail line 3,000 km long whose profit over the past 24 hours (fares minus running cost minus upkeep, as Money train's) is above zero, judged as each game hour turns; it sits after Longest road, and an older save that already has such a line records it quietly. Longest road's 5,000 km applies to new earnings only: a save that has it keeps it.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2616): "Change here" becomes "Transfers world" and its rule and earned text say "transfer" ("One transfer per trip on average, with 100k trips/day", "1.02 transfers per trip"), the word the game uses for station transfers. An unearned hover adds "Now 0.56 transfers per trip" (the average day's transfers over its rail trips, two decimals, whatever the trips/day) once a demand result is final; other firsts show no figure now.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2611): in the saves panel a card's buttons sit on the foot row under the picture, the "N other versions" line at the left and the buttons at the right; a game with one version has the row to the buttons alone. The start menu is unchanged.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2612): the grey real headway beside a headway typed at the track's limit is gone with its hover; the count is still clamped at the limit (T-679), only the readout is dropped.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2600): Anita's "normal ui text size" read as the map controls' 12 px (the demand buttons'), so the track and station bars go back to 12 px and only the level buttons' numbers are bigger, 15 px; T-2522 had set the whole bar to the panels' 14 px.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2623): Anita's "make the plain view more similar" to the related view is done literally: the related view's soft edges come from its SVG not being snapped to the pixel grid (its spine sat at x 28.5), the plain list's CSS bar and dots snap, so the plain bar, dots and stretches are shifted half a pixel (`--soft`, 0 in the related view) to look the same; at device ratio 2 a half pixel is a whole one, so both stay crisp there. Side lines and crossing bars are 2.5 px (were 3) with the gap 2.5 (was 2), so the pitch stays 5. To make both crisp instead, set `--soft` to 0 and snap the related SVG.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2602): "passed stations" in the line diagram are the stations the line runs through without stopping, shown while its stops are edited (the related lines view's side rows); their names go to 36% of the text colour (muted is 58%) and their pills' rims to 25% (was 40%).
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2603): the Marey chart is a "timetable chart" to players (Marey is jargon, and "diagram" is the network diagram's word); time across and stops down by distance, both directions in the line's colour (lifted toward white in dark), the window 2 round trips or 3 longest headways (2-8 h, a quarter before now) following the clock with no scrolling, opened from a third icon beside the schedule's copy and paste, in a panel at the dock's foot that follows the selected line. notes/T-2603.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2603): the chart draws the timetable (`TrackApi.line_trips`, new: one line's `trips` over any window), not the display guard's lags, so a train held on the map shows on time in the chart.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2437): Brazil's joint commutes are solved by IBGE's five macro-regions
  (`SOLVE_REGIONS["BRA"]`; one group held 934,191 gravity zones, the guard is 400,000); twin cities across state lines

  neighbour held fixed, as China's provinces are. notes/T-2437.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2437, T-2594): a world-run build parks its row when any of its id's caches is older than
  the build's start (`world_run.reused_caches`), and parked rows clear their caches as planned ones do; AWS landings
  clear them too. 36 Brazilian rows had shipped on European rows' caches of the same ids, past every gate check.
  notes/T-2437.md.

  the default 20 a km2 left 30.6M people dead, no floor made 29 roadless Amazon zones over 100,000 km2, and `join_km` 150
  kept 72 interior zones under 0.5M. 62 Amazon municipios (1.86M people, Altamira and Itaituba among them) are dead space,
  as Canada's north. `fua_whole` 0.5M joins Florianopolis and Sao Jose. notes/T-2437.md.

  is zoned: its jobs, curve and work from home come from the Metro's OD 2017 survey of exactly those municipios, which a

## 2026-11-05

- **T-2528: the distortion dial's default is 0.5** (was 0.8), Anita: "i kidna like 0.5". T-2556's one fail there (a
  channel skipped for a river later left out) is fixed, so every water check passes at 0.5.
- **T-2528: a river band is its real width at the drawing's smoothed scale**, a geometric mean of the nearest stations'
  local scales with Gaussian weights (R 1.5 km), smoothed 1.5 km along the river, floored at half its widest stretch at
  the real widths' ratio, clamped to 3 lines to 18 pitches. A smooth field rather than each station's own scale, after
  Anita's "the factor by which we expand an area should be a little continuous": per-station scales jump 2.6 times
  between a hop's ends at the 90th percentile in New York.
- **T-2528: a band narrows to its room** (never over a pill, nor a line running alongside within 30 degrees that it does
  not cross; lines across it pass over it). Covering stations or parallel lines with water reads as wrong geography, so
  thin spots stay where the layout leaves no room (Manhattan's core) until the layout keeps rivers' sides.
- **T-2528: land a voter carries a cell to across the sea, nearest a far-shore station that is not one of the cell's
  voters, votes sea** (waterSea.ts `farShore`): a far shore is drawn where its own stations are. A layout term giving
  rivers room (river part of a crossing hop at 2x, bank pairs held apart) was tried and left out: no gain in New York.

- **T-2556: the diagram's distortion dial is set at 0.8** (1 is the drawing as tuned before, 0 every hop at its real length). Anita loves today's look but leans a little less distorted: 0.8 keeps the cores and the close-up schematic as they were, draws Hokkaido and northern Tohoku about a quarter bigger against the Kanto core (0.15 -> 0.18) and Kyushu's and Shikoku's shapes closer, with layout figures as good as master's or better. 0.5 reads most like Japan but shrinks Tokyo and New York's subway to small knots, a different look. Turn `DISTORT` in layout.ts to revisit; `?distort=` tries a setting. notes/T-2556.md.
- **T-2081: Mexico's jobs are DENUE's units at their points plus the jobs DENUE leaves out, spread by tier 0's proxy.** DENUE has no farm work, street trade with no fixed site or paid domestic work, so its 38.3M workers are 0.304 a resident against the World Bank's 0.455; the 0.151 gap goes over each row's people by the proxy, so the country's total stays tier 0's. Each band counts at Censos Economicos 2019's mean sizes (0-5 people 2, 251 and more 900), which bring DENUE to 38.3M against the census's 36.0M. notes/T-2080.md.
- **T-2080: Mexico's rural people sit at ITER's locality points**, each locality over the cells nearer it than any other within 2 km, by GHSL; rural AGEBs (median 93 km2) are not used. notes/T-2080.md.

- **T-2550: big sparse regions get an absolute scale floor of 0.25** (a quarter of a kilometre-hop core's), lifting Hokkaido from 0.10 to 0.16 of the Kanto core and northern Tohoku by about a fifth: "a bit" bigger, Anita's middle ground. A floor against the network's own median lifted city suburbs too (Manhattan parallels 0.73 -> 0.62), so it was dropped. notes/T-2550.md.
- **T-2550: an island with no station is left out under a tenth of the land in the 100 km square round it, else drawn once by the stations near the one nearest it** (Anita: "omitted (if small relative to their surroundings) or at least not expanded"). A tenth keeps Awaji (12%; she called it too big, not unwanted), Amakusa and Sado, and drops Shodoshima, Okushiri and the small Seto islands.
- **T-2550: in the drawn sea, a station's map carries offsets past its hops' reach at its region's scale, and another peopled land mass's land is sea to it**, since a city's map carried far out drew empty land at city scale (the spit east of Hakodate). A stricter rule (land drawn only near its nearest station) cut holes inland and was dropped.

- **T-2541: the diagram's layout gives straits and bays room before land.** Hops across water are held near twice their real length at the scale round them, and stations with the sea between them 1.25 times their real distance at the scale of the way round by track (so a bay is as wide as its shores are drawn, not as a city on one of them). Land scale is not evened directly (Anita, 2026-11-05: "peninsulas dont hae to be huge especially if theres nothing on them. middle groud, i guess"): Boso and Izu grow only as the water round them opens (Boso about 1/8 to 1/3 of the Kanto core's scale); sparse land such as Hokkaido keeps the lonely hops' short targets (T-2550). The layout worker finds the sea on the ground before a first layout (all Japan about 2.7 s on ~70 s), which the water then reuses; a rebuild takes the last sea found. notes/T-2541.md.
- **T-2540: the diagram's sea is sampled, not cast by rays.** Each of a cell's four nearest stations carries the cell back to the ground through its local map and asks whether that place is sea; where no water mask covers the ground, the nearest known place answers (open sea far from the stations is sea). The rays (sea past the first sea a station saw that way, land past none) drew wedges to the drawing's edge and made the coast between cities up. The lie: a zone with no mask beside the sea reads as sea, and a peninsula far from its stations takes its nearest core's scale.
- **T-2540: land masses are parted by the sea and by water about 600 m wide (three 200 m cells) all the way across.** Rivers narrow upstream, so they part nothing; the Kanmon strait (650 m at its narrowest) is at the edge and parts Kyushu. Straits: where cells nearest stations on different land masses meet, or nearest two stations with at least 2 km and 30% of sea between them on the ground that the drawing squeezes (drawn under half their ground distance at their maps' scale), the drawing is sea, but for a pill and half a pitch and half a cell round it; a strait cut off inside the land is dropped. The coast strays seaward at most 0.35 of the way across the sea, so bays and straits stay open. Cost: the coast has more bends (all Japan 2.3 -> 4.6 per 100 pitches, New York 2.5 -> 6.8), the price of real bays and straits.

- **T-2536: a line's profit shows only once it has trains and a demand result has come for it** (an entry for the line, or none and its edit no longer pending, so a line nobody rides cannot stay on "calculating..."). Until then the Lines tab's Money cell is blank (the cell is narrow, and a blank matches a line with no counts), the line panel's Profit row reads "calculating..." (the project's word for unfinished figures) when the line has trains and is left out when it has none, and the line is left out of group and list totals. Top bar and money charts already count real track upkeep from the world, not per line, so they are unchanged.

## 2026-11-04
- 2026-11-05 (agent uismall3, T-2519): the headway cap is 4 hours (14,400 s), up from 2. No reason for 2 hours was recorded. The limit is the timetable: `lattice_deps_local` lays departures per stretch of one demand level (0-6 low, 6-20 high, 20-24 medium, so 4 hours at the shortest) and puts at least one in each, so a headway past 4 hours runs more trains a day than it asks for (24 hours gives about 3, not 1) while demand reads it as 1 a day. The era look-back (4 h) and `overtake::cycle` (2 h, falls back to one slower headway) also stop there. Demand itself takes any headway (waits are half of it, loads are per hour). Past 1 hour the stepper moves in 15 minutes. Going to 24 hours needs a Rust change that lays a long headway's trains by day, not by stretch.
- 2026-11-05 (agent uismall3, T-2526): a new line's auto-name skips every name used by a line with any stop in the city's zone (`stationZone`, the Lines tab's City column), whatever that line's home, so an intercity line "Line 1" that stops there counts. The zone, not the cluster: the Lines tab's City column and `nextLineName`'s argument are already zones, and a cluster is demand's grouping. A line with no stops counts in the city being viewed, as before. The colour pick still counts only the lines homed there.

## 2026-11-03

- 2026-11-03 (Anita's call, T-2266): riders pay the fares their demand was solved with, for a cut as for a rise (simple and symmetric): a fare edit shows in the Money tab at once, and a line's income and region shares switch when its riders solved with the new fares show. Kept per line (`game/fareInEffect.ts`), not per view version, because a cluster's final is merged into the shown view under the old version and a view made again from an old solve (a holiday coming near) holds old riders in new objects. main.ts wraps the demand client's `daysOf` to mark each line with the fares of the solve it came from (a stand-in for a field set in `daysOf` itself, T-2286), so nothing is saved and a reload prices its riders with its saved fares; riders from the cache or with no mark pay the current fares. The lie: when a cluster's final lands before the solve's, its lines pay the new fares while the view's totals (rail trips, used for the base fare) are still the old solve's, so income overshoots for that stretch (the real save: 419M a game hour against 270M at the final, and 1,487M without the rule). The fare estimate rows now start from the old-fare income, as their "today's figure" says.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2257): a road closed to motor traffic (access=no, motor_vehicle=no) still joins land parts when it lets some cars, buses or lorries through (motor_vehicle, motorcar, bus, psv, hgv, goods, delivery, taxi, coach, agricultural, forestry and the like set to anything but no), since such a bridge carries the island's own traffic (Rikers Island: access=no, bus=yes). Emergency, foot, bicycle and motorcycle exceptions do not count, and an emergency access way closed to the public takes none (Suomenlinna's service tunnel, access=no, motor_vehicle=permit, stays no link). notes/T-2257.md.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2238): after an edit, line bands are worked out again only for the lines it can change (new, on another path, reading an edge that changed, holding a ribbon near one, or with a ribbon change linked within reach to one that moved); kept lines beside one worked out are lined up with its changes as they were, so where a path's metres move in the last bits (an edge cut in two) a kept band can be off a whole pass by about 1e-9 m. A Traffic ribbon is one number per lines and edges (a whole pass gave two alike shapes two). A dragged corner snaps both its edges only where they meet within 8 px of the pointer, else the edge nearer its line alone: with both always snapping a rectangle's corner could not leave its place until dragged past 10° of the far edge. The icon is in the station view's row too (for the station's complex). Removing asks with the usual Remove and Keep. The tool counts as a build tool (demand views beneath the network).

- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2195): a boat is two train cars in demand and the track sim alike, 320 riders at the rated load and 480 at crush; T-2193's `BOAT_CRUSH` of 320 was the design's "two of demand's 160-rider cars" from before T-2186 made a car 160 rated, 240 at crush. Ports need no move inland for demand: the walk grid already starts a station's walks from the nearest land within 500 m when its pixel is water. Ferries pay no boarding cost and take the fare curve times the Ferry multiple, as the design has it. The solve-time bound is read on 3 workers, the game's own on 12 threads or more (+8%); on 2 it is +8 to +20%, from one extra night crowding round where default boats crowd at Staten Island (notes/T-2177.md "T-2195 results").

- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2162): diagram names are horizontal only, one size (the map's 12 px), placed per zoom level (2^(1/4) apart) rather than every frame, so a name past its level only shrinks toward its pill and stays clear; an "interchange" is two line colours stopping (the 1, 2 and 3 at 72 St are one service's local and express); names of 12 characters or more may go over two lines; interchanges may go 3 or 4.5 pitches out (at most 32 px) where lines meet all round them, beyond the design's two distances, for hubs where lines leave every way. The hard rules (no overlap with a line, pill or name; never nearer another station's pill) leave the densest knots unnamed at the opening zoom (in the Node check's layout Times Sq, Penn, Grand Central, Shinjuku at 7 px) until the zoom grows; the fix is room in the layout. notes/T-2162.md.

- 2026-11-03: the diagram's rebuild keeps to the drawing nearest the last one, each spacing term's pull capped at 0.3 of a hop, so an edit moves the median station 0.02 hops; the price is that a rebuild leaves more close pairs than a fresh layout (Tokyo 13 against 7) until the next fresh layout (a page load, or an edit changing over a tenth of the graph). Uncapped, it drifted 0.4 to 1.9 hops. T-2205.

- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2192): ferry routes measure the 5 m off land from a cell's centre to land's (the design's 10 m channel; Amsterdam's 11 m canals close otherwise), with an always-on pull to the middle of water narrower than 30 m so a boat keeps its room there; the 2.5 m check finds 1-4% of the Venice and Amsterdam routes under 5 m, none on land. A bridge fills only land with water both ways across its line, 60 m or less (a closing of the water, the prototype's rule, turned Circular Quay's wharves under the Cahill Expressway into water). Since the player places no waypoints (Anita), long legs are planned coarse with fine 700 m ends, up to 150 km, instead of a 40 km cap.
- 2026-11-03 (T-2226, Anita asked, orchestrator and agent agreed): player text says "level" for the track's seven steps, not "elevation", reversing T-526 (the setting is discrete steps, the code and "ground level" already say level, one word per thing). The Level view keeps its name though it is the track's level, not terrain height (Terrain is its own switch). The blueprint breakdown keeps "Road crossings", since "Flat crossings" sits beside it; the track tag says "crossings" beside the road icon.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2193): a port is a station node with a berth and no track (platform length 1, read by nothing), so names, walking, crowds, passages and bus stops come with it; its upkeep is listed apart from stations'. A ferry line's stops are its ports, so a port a line uses cannot be removed (the line would no longer fit its route) where a rail station is taken out of its lines. Removing a ferry line is undoable (the route is free); a constructed line redrawn to a new port pays for that port at once, as a bus route pays for new road. A stretch runs at the lower of its two points' speeds (T-2192's rule), kept on a mm/s grid so saves round trip to the bit.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2221): the achievement toast's rise and fade are exempt from style.css's general no-animation rule (`!important` on `.ach-toast`, as `.spin` is), since that rule was why the toast vanished at once; the fade is 1.2 s ease-in. Nothing else's animation changes.

- 2026-11-03 (Anita, T-2186, adopting part of T-2147: "ok we can do trains fill to 150%. cut i thought we already have people minding crowding from less than 100%? why dont we keep that?"): a car stays rated at 160 riders (100%, 44 seats) and now takes up to 240 (150%): the queue, the wait for a train with room and the boarding account fill to 240. Crowding is still minded from 70% of 160 (112 a car), and the agent's call is the simplest extension past 100%: the same curve, 1 + x(1 + x) with x = (load - 112) / 48, unclamped, so a minute aboard counts 3 at 100%, 5.4 at 120% and 10.8 at 150%. A bus stays 80, rated and full alike. The gauge, Average load and the track hover's Average load count against 160 and may pass 100% up to 150%, replacing T-820's and T-2007's 100% cap. notes/T-2186.md.

- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2159, T-2160; Anita: "perhaps could be a little more compact", "dont need to hide it, jut expose it"): the diagram's graph is built only while the diagram is shown, so players who never open it pay nothing. "More compact" is alpha 0.3, a pitch of the median hop over 9 and edges 1.4 bundle half widths (round 2: 0.35, 12, 1.6), about half the area; spacing stays at half a hop, the done-when's figure. A rebuild runs 10 B rounds held to the last layout (30 rounds moved Tokyo's median complex 0.2 hops). notes/T-2146.md.

- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2185): shared track that lines come onto from two or more edges at one node is a merge (wire kind 12, "Merge"), and its map dot and trouble list row sit on that node, where its trains stand waiting for it, rather than midway along the section's first edge, which could be the whole trunk away (why Anita's merge looked unmarked). Same thresholds as every dot, 75% on the map and 90% in the list: at 75% a two-line merge's wait is 30-40 s a train, about what a stop and start costs, so that is where trains begin to stand there. notes/T-2185.md.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2179, on Anita's layout): each tiered achievement has one ring, filling from zero toward the next tier (not from the tier before), so the ring agrees with the figure and "of 1M" written in its middle; past the top tier it is full with the figure alone. The tiers' medals sit in a row under the name, an unearned tier as a faint outline, so the ladder's length shows. Colours: Cities served blue and Trips per day red (hers), Busiest station orange, Busiest line green, the firsts' ribbons purple; metals bronze, silver, gold, platinum, emerald, the four-tier ones stopping at platinum. The leaderboards' trophy sits before the achievements button and shows in a sandbox too (it has no achievements button). Firsts follow after a gap, no divider and no heading.
- 2026-11-03 (T-2181, Anita: "not sure why we'd want that?"): the History charts' Linear / Log switch went; every chart is linear from zero. The week and month columns (T-2143) stay.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2202): on a map column under 520 px the status tags (recomputing, loading, busy) move under the day and night picture at the top right, right-aligned and as wide as their text; wider maps keep them beside the demand buttons, where they take no row.

- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2124 stages 1-2, on Anita's tiers and firsts): Sleeper (her "any passenger on a trip over 12 hours") counts time on one train only, as a sure lower bound from the line's loads and boardings (riders on stretch b minus those who boarded since stop a), at least 1 rider a day; a trip with changes counts only its longest ride, since per-trip times exist only in the trip explorer's per-pair queries. Train town ("Monopoly?" was her draft name) is any region's 20% goal, so it lands with that tick. Grounded (20% of a region's inter-city trips) is the city view's "To other places", both ways, over regions with a built station, and needs 100k such trips a day so a small zone beside one big city cannot win it on its commuters alone. Money train is checked each game hour and on each new demand result, from the Lines tab's profit over the past 24 hours. Rail lines only (buses never count). Sea crossing is left out until T-2150, so 27 steps for now. An older save's quiet working-out waits until every running line has figures for its stops in the average day's final result, since after an in-page load the last game's result can stand for a while.

- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2143): the day ledgers keep every day, the network's and each region's, so the History chart shows all time on the network and on a place. A save grows by about 30 bytes compressed per region and day (100 regions over 1,000 days is about 3 MB, near the account's 4 MB cap); folding old region days into weeks is T-2148, for when saves get big. Past 90 days a column is a week, then a month, each a day's average (not its total), so the scale does not jump when a game passes 90 days. A Linear / Log switch, linear by default; net stays linear since it goes below zero; the log scale reaches at most four powers of ten down.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2145): the real network's share is a fixed table (`game/realShare.ts`, from `checks/t2145-real-share.mjs`), not worked out live, which would mean solving the real network beside the player's. It is the city zone's own share only (the other zones the saves reach get 0.1% or less). The 30% and 40% goals stay goals, still reached, kept and listed under Goals reached; only their ticks are gone. Nothing else hangs on them (achievements, T-2124, are per game).

- 2026-11-03 (Anita, T-2142: "we should prob have new games start paused yeah, and say to unpause game at end of first tutorial"; agent's calls): every new game from the start menu or the saves panel starts paused, a sandbox and a real network's included (nothing in a sandbox needs the clock before the first line runs); a `?new=1` check page runs as before so the checks that count on a moving clock keep it. The first card ends with "Unpause the game", ticked by the clock running, its ripple on Normal speed. Anita kept "Confirm?" as the close dialog's heading.
- 2026-11-03 (Anita, T-2123: "i think first line stops running at 8pm is reasonable"): T-2092's fill of every level from a new line's first count or headway is gone; each level is set on its own again. The first tutorial card ends instead with "Hover the dot by High to see if there are enough trains" (ticks on the pointer over a crowding dot) and "Add trains at high, medium and low demand" (1/3, levels with a train on the rail line with most).
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2123): the ripple's rings come one every 0.5 s (1.4 s each, half T-1822's speed) without end until the step's own control is pressed; a press on a stand-in (a tab icon) only moves the ripple to the control once it shows. After the press, the 20 s idle reminder is three rings as before. Steps whose action is on the map (a bubble, a trip, the train share) ripple nothing. The view rows fold for the first card as a view over the remembered folds (`guideFolding`), so the player's own folds come back when it goes; the second card leaves them as remembered, since its first step needs Homes.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2123): the second card reads demand in four steps: Show Homes, a bubble, one of its destinations (the trip explorer), and the zone in focus's star at 10% train share (the T-2087 idea 4 goal, a real task with a counter; ticks at any region's star). The browser still remembers "finished" after the first card, so a later game has no card even if the second was skipped. A world-map start (no zone picked, or a location far from every zone) is marked `zoom` in the save, which adds "Zoom into a city" first, ticked by a zone at city zoom with its demand drawn; its ripple is the find button.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's calls, T-2123): the new game sheet lights New York to begin with, and a click on a lit city no longer unlights it (Choose on map is the way to no city; the checks' "click nyc, then Start" stays a New York start). The close confirmation keeps the dialog's "Confirm?" heading, so Anita's "Are you sure?" became that heading and the text is "You can restart the tutorial in Settings."
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2059, T-2060): a band's S-curve at a node goes on the side with more lines when the ribbon on the side with fewer is shorter than the curve (Tamachi's 370 m loop track kept its line a full line width off the loop), and a ribbon change along the way just ahead of the node where the tracks join (or just after one where they part) is placed as at that node (the Jersey City merge, whose groups overlapped at the node). The side with fewer lines for every along-the-way change was tried: clean at both places but kinks on the real save up to 220 / 25 at zoom 13 / 14 (from 182 / 16). The price at a big merge: the joining lines slide over the curve's length before the node, their track showing beside them. `?perfOff=bandsPlace` for stage 2's placement. notes/T-2058.md 3c.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-1738): New Zealand's zone rule `NZL`: its wards grouped by territorial authority, no density floor, no reach, zones not joining over 250 km. The default 20 a km2 left 1.19M dead (Selwyn, Gisborne); Australia's reach left Wanaka and Westport dead; with no join limit the South Island was one zone, at 150 km seven zones of 0.11-0.25M. So the countryside is four big zones and Northland (0.17M, cut off by Auckland's row), the Chatham Islands dead space. notes/T-1738.md 14.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-1738): `world_plan.reach_people` measures great-circle km, as `join_km` already did: in Mollweide metres it ran 1.3-1.9x long away from longitude 0, so the reach rule was in effect about 80-115 km for Canada and Australia. Their shipped rows keep their units; a replan would keep more towns. Other Mollweide distances: T-2094.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2084): tier 0's census totals by unit give a unit's cells its total times the share of GHSL's people over the whole unit that those cells hold (the brief's census / GHSL over the whole unit, times GHSL in the part), not the whole total; a unit with no GHSL people outside the outline (by pixel centre) takes its total exactly. The share is GHSL's because nothing else says where in a cut unit its people live. GHSL people the shore clip already leaves out of a pack stay out, with their share of the census (pti's coastal towns, T-2099). notes/T-2084.md.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2084): the Philippine config scales by municipality and city (USCB ADM3, 1,634 units), not barangay: in cities many barangays are smaller than a 0.1 km² cell, and a unit holding no cell centre loses its total. Muslim Mindanao's gap is at province level, so municipalities fix it. T-2076 said barangays.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-1966): trains wait for each other at flat crossings and crossing junction moves on screen only, from zoom 14 (`CONFLICT_ZOOM`, where trains are drawn as cars at about their true length; Anita: "only where we're looking closely"); further out they are drawn through each other. The sim already charges the average wait at each conflict as a timetabled hold, so a drawn wait changes no run time. A train's time over a conflict comes from its timetable and lag (front 20 m short to back 10 m past), not from where it is drawn between frames, which was what made T-1965's version pile up. notes/T-1966.md.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-1966): turns at a conflict are first come, first served, with no slipping in ahead of a train already waiting even where one would clear in time, and trains already over another conflict go first; a train that would have to stop on a crossing to wait at the next one stands short of both. No wait is longer than 240 s (`CONFLICT_WAIT_MAX`): past it the train is let over, drawn through what is there (on the real save, a train standing at a terminal over a crossing through its layover). A shorter cap lets more through; a longer one draws 4-minute stalls.

- Anita, T-2058: "lets try A then C, and thin and faded." Muting a selection's other lines is now thin (45% width), faded (colour halfway to the casing's) and solid, replacing the 55% see-through muting (`?perfOff=muteThin` for the old look); lines side by side use A (long S-curves) and C (one order per pair of tracks for the network) whenever the Settings checkbox is on, still off by default until she has seen it (`?perfOff=bandsSmooth`, `bandsOrder` for the older ways).
- (agent's calls, T-2058 stage 2; notes/T-2058.md) C is on with A, not behind a flag: order changes on the real save go from 643 (523 with A alone) to 2 and Kew Gardens' yellow line no longer crosses nine bands, while Newark's junction looks about the same either way. A track leaving a ribbon along the way still eases centred on where it leaves, so its line floats off its own track for about 125 m (Tamachi): a 120 m ease there brought back 209 kinks at zoom 14, and easing on the side with more lines crossed bands at merges (Jersey City), since lines of one ribbon can see the same change as a node or as along the way (T-2060). At a node the side with fewer lines is counted on the line's own edges first. D (eases in screen pixels) not built: at zoom 15-16 an ease is long but gentle, and it would need a stroke attribute and a texture.

## 2026-11-02

- (agent's calls, T-2058 stage 1; notes/T-2058.md) The prototype for continuous bands (`?perfTry=bandsSmooth`) eases every change of shift over one length shared by all lines of the ribbon there (250 m, plus 50 m per line past two, at most 800 m), not a length per line's own move, so a ribbon moves as one; a node change keeps stage 4's side (starting at the node on the side with fewer lines). The muted-lines prototypes draw the other lines at 45% width, solid (`muteThin`), or also mixed halfway to the casing colour (`muteThinPale`); trains and buses keep the 55% dimming.

- (agent's call, T-2031) A right drag (and Ctrl with a left drag, MapLibre's own, for one-button mice) turns the map, off by default (Anita 2026-11-02: with no tilt it adds little) behind a Settings switch; never tilted; fingers and keys still do not turn it. Turning is off while the blueprint or bus tool is on, since their right button pans or steps back. The way back to north is a needle beside the find button shown only while turned. Measured on the real save: panning turned 35 degrees cost about 4-7% more median frame time than north up (5.6 vs 6.0 ms at zoom 11.5), within run-to-run noise (artifacts/trainworld/t2031/perf.json).

- (agent's call, T-1907) Faces at stations take a skin tone and hair colour by the station's country: a fixed table (`map/faceTones.ts`) puts about 170 ISO codes into 13 broad profiles, each weights over six plain skin tones and the four hair colours (black about 9 in 10 in East, Southeast and South Asia); unlisted countries keep the yellow cartoon face. Mood stays readable on a skin-toned face by its features plus a tint (tired 35% towards drained yellow, sad 45% towards blue), which on darker tones reads greyer than blue. Drawn once per face at spawn, as before; nothing but colours differs between countries.

- (agent's call, T-1907) A click where several lines are under the pointer picks the nearest, lines within 2 px of each other larger first (riders per day on that stretch, trains an hour times cars before demand answers); further clicks within 6 px go on round the same order, then to the track, then back to the first. This replaces T-654's "shared track selects the track first". The hover tag still names the line drawn on top, which can differ from the one a click picks.

- (agent's call, T-1907) The map can no longer be turned or tilted at all: Shift and the arrow keys did it (MapLibre's keyboard handler, the only way left on) and nothing turned it back, so keyboard rotation and touch pitch are off and any bearing or pitch is eased back to 0 when a move ends. No compass button: the game never wants a turned map.

- (agent's call, T-1907) The track tag's riders row shows in every track view (Line, Height, Speed), not only Traffic; Average load stays Traffic only. Height and Speed draw no line strokes, so there the track's tag gives all its lines' riders on that stretch together. The faded bullet of a line with no trains goes from 0.4 opacity and 60% grey to 0.7 and 30% (Anita: "weaken fading a lot").

- (agent's call, T-2018) A train's following loss behind slower trains (T-1924) is run off as one lowered speed over the whole shared stretch, not as up to 4 stands outside the slower line's stations, so its timetable never stands between its stops; drawn, it closes on the train ahead while that one stands at a station and the display guard (T-1965) holds it behind, so it trails. Totals are unchanged; only where on the stretch the time is lost moves. A loss too big even at 0.5 m/s waits its rest at the stop before the stretch (Anita's own fallback).

- (Anita, T-2017) Ground level track's multiplier goes from 0.3x to 0.5x (US$37.5M a km), since with road crossings it was still too cheap in cities (T-1928 had set 0.3x); the other levels stay. The tag's chips never wrap inside themselves: a whole chip moves to the next line (Anita, T-2017 follow-up).
- (Anita, T-2021) A country's fare multiplier is kept and used to the nearest 0.01x, in a new game's multiples and in an older save's scale (turned to a multiple of its default, rounded, and back), so what the Money tab shows is exactly what acts.
- (Anita, T-2019) The Select tool's box picks only track with both ends inside it; a piece it cuts across is not picked or drawn picked, and it stretches when the pick is dragged. A click on one piece still picks it.
- (agent's call, T-1903) Total demand bubbles are each place's trips from homes plus trips to destinations, drawn at the destinations' density (160,000 legs/km²), so job centres look as under Destinations and home areas a little smaller than under Homes (0.75 the radius). A click opens the usual bubble panel, "Trips to and from here", with both ends' legs and a far end of both directions merged cell by cell; the trip explorer counts one way, so from a Total selection it opens as homes or destinations, whichever way has more of the pair's trips. Asked as two `flows` (and two `intercitySelection`) answers rather than a new WASM call.
- (agent's call, T-2007) The track hover's "Average load" for a stretch is the demand result's riders on it for the day shown (the figure the tag already states) over the boarding account's trips that day at crush, capped at 100%: the account keeps each trip's load per stop but no stretch figure reaches the app, and a new WASM call was not worth it for a hover. The line panel's Average load is the account's rider-seconds, so the two can differ a little where trains run full.
- (agent's call, T-2005) The memory drops (zones going coarse) spare the zone built in last (a station placed or a stop added there; track alone does not count, since a zone no station reaches holds nothing to keep), however long ago, and the one before it while its build is under 10 minutes of wall time old. They are skipped by the 10-minute left-alone rule and dropped last over the budget, so under pressure with nothing else droppable they may still go. Not saved: a reloaded game starts with no last build.
- (orchestrator, T-1985) The gate fails a zone's `drive_factor_median` only above 3.0 (WARN from 2.0); metros keep 2.0. Made on T-1975's count of over 230 hand relaxations for hills, deltas and fjords, none kept parked. The replay of every stored zone report (notes/T-1984.md) found the largest at 3.13, not 2.85: Yuanyang (Yunnan), relaxed and shipped; it keeps its relaxation, so every verdict on China's, Vietnam's and Canada's zones is unchanged. 3.2 would have covered it too; 3.0 kept.
- (agent's call, T-1984) Planner defaults, every country: a zone under 50,000 people is dead space (was 10,000 with `contiguous`, none elsewhere, 100,000 in Vietnam); zones each holding a fifth of a GHS-FUA's main urban centre (FUA over 1M, not a metro) are one zone over the cap, named after the centre, while over the cap shedding its biggest outlying piece of 50,000 that holds none of the centre (Adelaide 1.48M and Port Pirie's countryside, as T-1857 set by hand); a zone's unit, not its most populous, with 1,000 people over 100 km from the rest is dead space (Darwin's "Unincorporated NT", by hand in T-1857). The urban centre, not the FUA, decides the cut: Vietnam's delta FUAs (Nam Dinh, Haiphong, Long Xuyen) are countryside round smaller centres. A `--replan` of built countries would now drop six shipped zones under 50,000 (Vieques, Nantucket, Trebinje, Gorazde, Aland, Ceuta, each shipped only with a top_zone_jobs_share relaxation) and Canada's parked lel, and reunite Nanyang's and Longgang's centres and San Juan's (notes/T-1984.md).
- (T-1929) Road crossings: US$6M each at level 0, a motorway 3, kept after measuring (Midtown, central Tokyo and London 11-13 a km make ground 90-100 a km against the viaduct's 75; open country under 1 a km stays at about 27; suburbs 6-11 land near the viaduct; notes/T-1929.md). Agent's calls: roads on bridges or in tunnels count none (already out of the way); counted in the clock worker's track model like terrain, from tiles the worker fetches, not on the main thread and sent with each edit as briefed, since many edits get their shape inside the model (drags, reshapes, bridges, splits); so crossings are derived, not saved, and a tile landing reworks the edges it reaches at once, outside any edit, so no charge picks it up. Old saves need nothing.
- (Anita, T-1928) Track prices: base US$75M a km, factors -3..+3 = 2.0 / 1.6 / 1.0 / 0.3 / 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 (US$M 150 / 120 / 75 / 22.5 / 75 / 112.5 / 150), surcharges added to the factor: low hills +0.5, high hills +1.2, mountains +3, water +2.5. Her words: "lets keep to round numbers", +1 and -1 at 1.0x; cost changes "are all reversible". Replaces the first proposal in notes/T-1897.md (base 25, factors 6 / 5 / 3 / 1 / 3 / 4.5 / 6), same dollars at the extremes. Ground is the cheap level (country track), so cities need T-1929's road crossings to make it dear there. Stations unchanged. Junctions, crossings and the terminal upgrade are track at their level, so they follow the table (ground junction US$5.6M, was 11.25; a -2 flyover junction US$120M, was 99+). Saves keep no prices: edge prices are worked out on load, so constructed track in an old save shows its new price, nothing is charged or refunded.
- (Anita, T-1930) A viaduct (+1 to +3, and the bridge a ground segment becomes) or a cut and cover tunnel (-1) is refused over more than 30 km of unbroken water: real rail bridges reach 29 km (Hangzhou Bay), the Dover Strait (33 km) and Tsugaru stay refused. Counted across edges meeting at a node in the water. Agent's reading: a bored stretch ends the count (bored tunnels are unlimited), and track built before is not refused again until an edit touches it. No depth rule: no depth data.
- (Anita, T-1931) Upkeep by structure: US$20k a km a game day on the ground (level 0), 25k on a viaduct (+1 to +3), 35k in cut and cover (-1), 40k in a bored tunnel (-2, -3), stations unchanged at US$50k. Her words: "ok lets do 20 25 35 40. so the advantage of tunnel is cheaper in mountains and can cross long water, and the advantage of viaduct is cheaper maintenance." (She called her first 15k and 40k "a bit aggressive".) Per edge, not per metre, so the app and the sim agree from `edges_info`'s lowest and highest level: an edge pays the dearest structure between them, so one that ramps into a tunnel pays the tunnel rate throughout.
- (agent's call, T-1894) North up for line and group diagrams: the north end on top, but a line within about 22 degrees of east-west (north-south distance under 0.4 of east-west) puts its west end on top, reading like text; a loop stays as built (its first stop on top), having no north end. A group follows its longest line. Stop times then run from the top stop, the far direction's times taken as the total less the built direction's (`game/northUp.ts`).
- (agent's call, T-2661) Anything a line draws left to right (the crowding bar in the demand level hover, in the line panel and the Lines list) reads west to east: the west end on the left. A line within about 22 degrees of north-south (north-south over east-west distance 2.5 or more) puts its north end on the left, a loop stays as built (first stop on the left). This is not T-1894's top-end rule (`drawnReversed`), which would put a north-east line's east end first; `lineReversed` / `leftToRightReversed` in `game/northUp.ts` are the left-to-right ones. The vertical diagrams keep T-1894's rule.
- (agent's call, T-1894) Clicking stops in the line panel's diagram works only while stops are edited (the group view always edits), uses the group view's keep-the-path edits (`dropStop`, `endLineAt`, `stopPassed`) where the remove button still re-routes (`setStops`). Copy line no longer focuses the copy's name, only opens its stop editing. Related lines show side lines' stops only while editing and only where the line passes a station of that complex (interlined), each then a click to add, leaving the "+ N passed" row.
- (agent's call, T-1894) Line colours: the palette's first six (red, blue, green, orange, purple, yellow) are required for a city's first four lines and favoured after by counting their distance 1.3 times; buses get the favour but not the rule.

## 2026-10-31

- Lines sharing track drawn side by side (T-1799 bands and T-1848 corridors, Line and Traffic views) is now a Settings checkbox, "Lines side by side", off by default (T-1886, Anita: "it doesn't look super great yet"); off draws lines over each other as before T-1799. Turn the default on again once the bands look right.

## 2026-10-30

- Terrain classes at 115 m from GEDTM30's 30 m bare earth, recalibrated to the old mix (T-1854; notes/T-1854.md 6-7): rolling from a mean 30 m slope of 2.5 degrees, hilly 5.5, mountain 11 (were 2 / 5 / 10 on 240 m slopes), then a 3 x 3 majority at 115 m (drops one-cell specks, keeps a 230 m valley floor; the old 3 x 3 at 460 m erased 1.4 km), then capped at the class of the ground round it (slope between 1/240 degree mean elevations, 1.25 / 2.5 / 5 degrees). World mix by area on the zones that had classes: flat / rolling / hilly / mountain 58.9 / 16.2 / 10.8 / 14.1% before, 59.8 / 14.3 / 10.7 / 15.1% after. The cap exists because 30 m bare earth keeps towers and raised roads (central Tokyo came out 47% hills without it); its lie: a narrow steep feature in gentle ground is priced at the gentle ground's class.
- What the AWS run kept is a compact intermediate, not the DEM (T-1854, Anita: "don't have to redownload"): mean slope per 1/960 degree cell and mean elevation per 1/240 cell, 3.1 GB for 7,484 one-degree blocks, covering the registry, the plan's unbuilt metros and Australia, Korea, Turkey, Vietnam, Mexico and southern Canada; slope maximum and relief planes were dropped since they doubled the size on a disk with 16 GB free.
- **Australia's plan set by hand where the rule cut cities** (T-1857): Adelaide one zone of 1.47M over the 1.5M-cap split
  (Salisbury and Onkaparinga), its rural north a Port Pirie zone; Sydney takes Central Coast, Campbelltown, Camden and
  Wollondilly (Greater Sydney; GHS-FUA gave Campbelltown its own area); Whyalla (24,000 alone) and geoBoundaries'
