  scattered "Unincorporated NT" dead space (`ALONE_MIN` 50,000). Every Australian row takes census people by SA1;
  jobs stay tier 0's, since 2021's place of work was counted in lockdown. notes/T-1857.md.

- Everyday sample sizes (agent's calls, T-1874, Anita's rule from T-1866): a school or university takes one home sample per 30 trips, rounded down, 2 to 100 (the minimum of 2 kept from T-397, so a small school now has fewer samples than its old one per 8); every everyday sample's share is times a factor uniform in 0.5-1.5 (not a lognormal: a bounded spread keeps the largest home at most 3x the smallest, enough to look uneven without one sample carrying a campus), seeded from the fixed cell's H3 index, the purpose and the sample's place, and a cell's drawn samples are scaled together so its total is what equal shares gave (samples dropped under 150 m don't count). Coarse zones keep T-1222's one per 64, 1 to 8, for campuses too.

- **Dragging a curve's middle moves its PI so the curve runs through the pointer; a platform end moves the whole station** (T-1869). The curve keeps its radius rule (auto stays auto), as T-079 made the radius follow the points: the PI steps by the preview's miss, times the measured ratio of PI to middle movement, until the middle is within 0.3 m, and the shape made is the one last previewed. Changing only the radius (PI fixed) was the other choice; it moves the curve on one line only, so the curve would not follow a sideways drag. A platform end drags the station node by the pointer's move, heading and length kept: pivoting or stretching the platform from one end would need a move that sets the node's heading and platform length in one edit, a new `TrackApi` call (a WASM interface change), and was left for Anita to ask for. The Select tool's pick shows a square on each movable node from zoom 9, as the other draggable points, and its drag the node drag's speed labels and refusal tag.

- **Ramp marks are one triangle per ramp, at its middle, pointing away from the ground, in the colour of the far level** (T-1871). Replaces the wedge at the ground end and the half disc for drops, so every change of level (1 to 2, -1 to -2 included) has the same mark, and the hover's "Tunnel portal down to" became "Ramp down to". A ramp is a stretch where the height keeps rising or falling; one that crosses the ground gets a mark each side.

- Copy line names and symbols (Anita's examples, agent's reading, T-1872): a name or symbol ending in a number, or a number and one letter, gets the next free letter ("Line 1" gives "Line 1a", then "Line 1b"; symbol 1 gives 1a); any other name becomes "<name> clone N" and the symbol its first letter and N, N the lowest not taken by another line's name or symbol ("t" or "tm" gives t1, then t2). Her "tokaido line clone 1" for "tokaido mainline" is read as a typo, so the name stays whole. A copy of "X clone 1" gives "X clone 2" (Anita); symbols stay within 5 characters, else the clone rule. Headway steppers step by the whole minute (5.37 min gives 5 or 6) and minus from none gives 5 min, so the old 30 s and 5 min steps are gone. An opened save starts on the city with the most built stations, by its registry view, not the fit of all its rail.
- The station tool snaps to track only, and a spot short of room moves to the nearest that fits (agent's calls, T-1870): the 18 px node radius it shared with the track tool pulled stations onto plain joints across up to about 100 m of track at zoom 13, so it is gone for stations. A spot whose platform would reach a station, junction or unjoined joint on one side moves away along the track until 2 m clear, at most half the platform and 2 m, so a pointer on another station's platform stays refused rather than jumping past it; near a loose end the station still goes on the end node (T-671), and near a joint between unlike track (built beside blueprint) on the joint when that is nearer than the moved spot. The room refusal names one cause (station, then junction, joint, track end), split into its own kinds in sim/src/track/net.rs (`PlatformStation`, `PlatformJunction`, `StationJunction`). Revisit the half-platform limit if sliding off a junction feels too far at street zoom.
- Anita, T-1859: keep the old straight-segment station rule beside the drawn-box one ("it seems fine to keep"), so complexes only ever join.
- Anita, T-1866: everyday trips get more sample homes, uneven in size (at most 100 homes, at least 30 trips each, sizes varied at random), built as T-1874; the drawing-only spread (T-1867) waits until she has seen it.
- Corridors back on, decided per place along each line (agent's calls, T-1848; notes/T-1799.md "T-1848"): a ribbon change shorter than 200 m along a line is smoothed over (the brief said about 100 m; 200 is a platform's length, and 100 left a bump at every island platform where the tracks splay); tracks within 6 m of each other are ordered by line number, not by which is a metre to the left (the real save's per-line edges swap sides at interlockings, which drew bands crossing at stations); no shifting a ribbon to put a stopping line in its station's box (tried: wide ribbons swerved at every station, worse than the miss), so a box on one track of a wide ribbon holds only the middle bands (Forest Hills' yellow band is outside its box); a ribbon may be 70 m and 32 tracks across, not 30 m (the Long Island Rail Road with the Port Washington branch at Woodside is 58 m, and no ribbon there drew 17 lines over each other between two ribbons). The metre to line width turn stays fixed (more gap at zoom 16 on wide bundles). Revisit the 200 m and the 70 m if a parting looks late or a yard turns into a ribbon.
- A cluster whose lines moved only by noise keeps its result (agent's call, T-1862, from T-1850's design; notes/T-1850.md): same shape, same whole-solved populations, run times within 0.5 s and trains an hour within 0.2% of the network it was last solved on, span exactly equal. The lie: a cluster no edit touched keeps riders worked out on run times up to 0.5 s and trains an hour up to 0.2% off what the clock now shows, until something in it changes past that; today those figures move by about as much at every re-solve anyway. `?perfOff=noise` gives the exact rule back.
- Complexes link on the drawn platform box as well as the old straight segment (agent's call, T-1859): two platforms whose drawn centre lines (curved, from the node at a dead end) come within 50 m are one complex, and so are those the straight segment centred on the node already linked. The drawn rule alone split five New York complexes the real save has (Grand Central-42 St from the terminal, 34 St-Penn from Penn, Atlantic Terminal, Times Sq, Franklin Av; drawn gaps 61-104 m), since at a terminal the straight segment reaches past the buffers, where the concourse is. With both, complexes only ever join: the real save goes 855 to 854 (Grand Central's two halves). Drop the straight rule if splits like those are wanted.
- Members of a complex whose boxes do not touch get a drawn passage, whether built or not (agent's call, T-1859): "touch" is centre lines within 16 m (`BOX_TOUCH_M`, a box's width on the ground at zoom 15); the shortest joins that connect the complex are drawn, built passages and touching boxes counting as joined, so nothing is drawn twice. Drawing only: it costs nothing and demand still walks it at the plain change pace, so building a passage there still speeds the change (T-1281). The real save draws 30.

- Save previews follow the current theme by recolouring the stored picture in the page, not by storing both themes (agent's call, T-1852; `ui/thumbShade.ts`): old saves then match without being saved again, and the stored picture stays one PNG (also what the account keeps). Land, rule and grey track are swapped, a line's colour stays, edge pixels are re-blended against the nearest line pixel (mean error 0.25 of 255 on a test drawing against the same drawing made natively). A thumbnail drawn some other way than `slotSummary` would need its colours added there.
- Deleting the game being played reloads the page to the start menu (agent's call, T-1852): the worker deletes the slot, its backups and the account's copy, drops its lease and autosave, then the page reloads as if opened with no game chosen. A page already at that address is reloaded, since `location.replace` to the same address is no navigation (this also fixed the sign-out path, `leaveToMenu`, for a page opened without a game switch).

- Corridors off (agent's call under the brief's fallback, T-1846; notes/T-1799.md "T-1846"): stage 3's per-edge corridors stepped a line's band sideways wherever the next edge fell in or out of a corridor, since side-by-side tracks are cut into edges at different places (446 steps of 0.15 line widths or more on the Tokyo shared save against 94 off, 449 against 0 on the real save). A local-ribbon variant and a 3 m "near-overlapping only" variant did not fix it (449, 351). So `CORRIDORS = false`: lines on one edge stay side by side (stages 1, 2, 4), parallel tracks draw over each other as before stage 3, which loses Queens Boulevard's nine-band ribbon on the real save. The proper fix (side by side decided along each line's length and smoothed, not per edge) is written up there.
- An edit's follow-up waits behind the previews, and a placed segment shows as its ghost for a frame or two (agent's call, T-1407; notes/T-1407.md): the clock worker answers previews and station checks asked during an edit before it builds and posts the edit's state, trips and boarding hour and answers it, and works the boarding lines the edit moved three a task with previews between. The edit's answer still follows its state, so every caller finds `world` up to date, except the track tool's free blueprint segments, which ask `early`: answered right after apply, the next ghost asked from the answer's node, the ghost just placed drawn as the blueprint's stand-in (the same outline, rails and middle) until the network with it is set, that state taken after the ghost paints. The lie: for one or two frames the new blueprint is the ghost's drawing, not the network's. Not done: answering a train count before its settle (the settle is the change), or a ghost asked during it going first (it would show the network before the count).
- NUTS stays for grouping and names, geoBoundaries fills its gaps (agent's call, T-925; notes/T-925.md): no NUTS polygon ships in a pack (GISCO's terms are non-commercial and non-transferable, the packs ODbL), but replanning Europe's zones off NUTS was out of scope, and units and names are attributes. The fill takes the finest geoBoundaries level with an open licence (Spain's ADM2 under INE's own licence left out). The EuroGeographics notice stays, since NUTS still decides the zones and their names.

## 2026-10-29

- Panel reads wait for track previews (agent's call, T-1815; notes/T-1815.md): the past-24-hours and crowding reads the panels make (`today`, `linesPeriods`, `linePeriods`, `lineAccount`) are held in the client while previews or station checks were asked in the last 250 ms, so the Lines tab, trouble list and inspector counts can lag a quarter second behind the ghost; a preview waiting 40-60 ms behind each read during a solve was the felt cost. The clock worker also hands the same `today` answer again while nothing it reads has changed.
- Group view refinements (agent's calls, T-1817; notes/T-1809.md): crossing bars stop 10 px past the outermost line they cross (the diagonal joining two crossings of one line not built); unnamed rows of branches apart from each other share rows, named rows never move; the row order is swept down, up and down again so a meeting's order reaches back up the branches; group pill colours capped at 0.45 relative luminance on the light shade and floored at 0.14 on the dark, mixed toward black or white so the hue holds.
- Key map (agent's calls, T-1819, from Anita's "number hotkeys for height, letters for menu panels"): 1 to 7 set the elevation (no Shift); B, L, S, F, Y open Build, Lines, Stations, Finances, Cities; the tools take R (track, rail), P (station, place), X (delete), M (select, marquee), U (bus, her ask). Her "c cities?" gave way to C staying Construct blueprints (a real action key, used while planning), so Cities is Y (the last letter of city); L now opens Lines, so Edit stops moved to O; T stays the trip explorer, so the track tool could not have T. D and E stay the blueprint tool's disable and enable. The tab key only switches the tab (a tool or half-drawn route stays on). The Money tab shows as "Finances" in its hover; the tab id `money` and the Lines tab's Money column keep their names.
- Stations only on track, a wider snap (agent's calls, T-1820): the station tool's snap is 1.5 times the earlier radii, 18 px to a node (was 12) and 15 px to track (was 10), set in `SNAP_NODE_PX`/`SNAP_EDGE_PX`, which the track tool's join onto a node or piece shares (one radius pair for both tools, so a click lands the same way in each); the overlap band (4 px), "beside" band (40 px) and the real-line snap (12 px, `realSnap.ts`, a different feature) are unchanged. A click on open ground with the station tool says "Place stations on track" (notice and a red tag by the pointer, no platform ghost, no rings) rather than a red platform box, since a box needs a track to lie along. The page's open-ground flow is removed; the sim's `add_free_station`, the worker's free `StationAt` and `preview_free_station` stay unused.
- Lines-tab dragging and group bullets (agent's calls, T-1813): the hand order covers every line in every place and is set by any move, so the viewed place's reorder keeps the others' places; it lives as a rank per line and per node in the sim (`LOR1`, so ids stay right across a save's compaction) outside the undo history like the sort; a header click drops it, and new lines go last. A gap inside an open group puts a line into that group at that place; the gap under a group's last line is inside only for its own lines. A group's row drags into gaps (the whole group moves) but never groups. The bullet's text strips " line"/" main line" and, beyond the ask, a trailing 線/线/本線/ライン/선 unless a number is left (the shared Tokyo start's names are Japanese); the line panel's row hides the group's name where the pill reads the same. A set bullet colour goes back to the lines' colour with a button beside the Bullet box.
- Group view layout v2 calls (agent's calls, T-1809; notes/T-1809.md): Copy line moved to a button in the group view's Stops header that opens a row of the group's bullets (a click copies that line), the bullets at a line's ends open its panel; branches are stacked (one branch's block of rows, then the other's) so each branch's names read together, at the cost of a bare bar beside the other block; both stops either side of a stretch one line skips are named, not only the next, so an express's stops all get names; a row every line passes stays as a bare 7 px row so a ghost there can add a stop back in the default view; the order per row costs a crossing as 8 rows of likeness.
- Panel and map calls from Anita's TODO (agent's calls, T-1812): only the logo's t is turned over about its own middle (its lead-in becomes a bar across the top, a capital T; the r goes on from the stem's foot as its own stroke in `map/logoNetwork.ts`); the whole logo upside down was tried and read upside down; a selected line or group draws every other line and train at 55% (the 30% of stop editing stays; selected stroke width unchanged); every inspector's title row is pinned, not only the line and station ones; the station panel keeps the "Complex of N stations" link back in a station opened alone (the one way back to the complex but a click on the map), and loses the label in complex view. A symbol typed by hand sticks (no new save field: the line's `symbol`, "" for none, already was it); the cause was the symbol box resetting when a rename landed and a stale name or colour carried by the symbol edit.
- The track tool's node drag starts at 3 px of movement, no hold time (agent's call, T-1811): T-734's threshold, the same as MapLibre's click tolerance, so every press is either a click or a drag; a larger one would leave presses moving 3-5 px doing nothing (no click, no pan). A join ending the run under keep placing still carries on with Shift held, so a deliberate chain through a junction stays one gesture.
- Track no train runs on loads no capacity (T-1805): a flat crossing whose other route carries no running line makes no resource, and a node where only two edge ends carry running lines joins track sections like a plain two-way node, so blueprint or unused constructed track beside a line changes none of its holds. Before, a crossing gave lines on its own edge a queueing delay among themselves, and a branch cut the section. T-007's demand figure is unchanged; saves with unused flat crossings or stubs on running track can move slightly on load.
- A line an edit's settle moves only on the side (not the edited line) goes to demand when its trains change, a rate moves by 0.01 tph (`DEMAND_TPH_EPS`) or a stop-to-stop time by 5 s (T-1805); below that it is redrawn but demand keeps its last figures (the lie: thousandths of a train an hour on shared track). A route edit sends trips only when a line changes for demand, also when it pays for a junction cut into constructed track.
- Which edits cap the clock at 4x (T-1803, Anita's rules, `game/editCap.ts`): a train count or headway edit is quiet at a level not in force on that line now, or when it moves the value by less than half (counts in trains, headways in trains an hour, so 10 to 6 minutes counts as +67%), measured from before the edits since demand last settled so a run of small steps adds up; from 0 or to 0 always caps (mine: service starts or stops); a stop edit is quiet only on a line with no trains at any level; going back to counts from a headway is quiet; every other edit caps, and one capping edit among quiet ones caps. The lie: a level not in force can come into force before the solve ends at 16x, and for a moment shows the old figures.
- A headway step is checked against the cash up front (T-1800) only where the panel can count the trains: a headway at or over the level's shortest (read by the line's panel, `lineHeld`), where the count is the round trip over the headway and matched the worker to the dollar. A shorter headway, a line whose panel has not read its limits and a bus line go to the worker as before (pop, then knock), because the sim trims those counts to what the track takes over several rounds (line 105 at 60 s: the panel's 50 or 185 trains, the sim's 61).
- Buses stand 20 s at each drawn street stop (T-1798), but the time is taken out of the hop's running time, so trip times, the schedule and demand are unchanged (the lie: between stands a bus runs about 1.3 times the street speed, 24 km/h on 18 km/h streets, and riders still board only at demand stops). They did not stop before, only at train stations. Drawn 1.4 times larger from a distance, 1.2 close up.
- A far zone ignores a one-train-a-day change in a line's service span until the next whole solve (T-1764): the touch rule takes a span within 0.06 as the same view, so a train added or dropped from a far line's evening no longer re-solves every zone on it; trains an hour and run times still touch as before.
- Opening-day bunting dropped (Anita, T-1759): pennants under names don't fit a map game.

## 2026-10-28

- **Touch skips the new line's name focus only; the pencil and a tap on the box still focus** (T-1756, my call): "touch" is the last press being a finger (`touchInput`), and only the editor opened by `renameLinePrompt` is left unfocused. The opening-day bunting under station names (T-1434) is kept as it is, with no hover (labels take no pointer events).
- **Owning a car 5% dearer** (T-1755, Anita, in place of T-1752's parking and drive-weight levers): $2.10 a trip + $0.1365 a km (was $2.00 + $0.13). Parking stays at 7 ("already weird feeling how high it is"). Small enough that the ceiling tests were not rerun (Anita); both are lower bounds on rail's share, which this raises.
- **Imported crossings are marked separated, not levelled per hop** (T-1751, my call): `EdgeData.sep` (bit 7 of the edge flags in the save) on every edge `realsave` writes; two `sep` edges never make a crossing, flat or bad, whatever their levels, so the lines keep their one of six levels each (bridge and tunnel looks as before) and the 26 central Tokyo crossings that held lines at 40 trains an hour are gone. Levels per hop would put ramps (200 m a level) into 1 km hops and make crossings of the ramps invalid; six levels cannot separate central Tokyo's lines anyway. Visible cost: two lines at one level cross on the map with no bridge, as on any transit map. Track the player draws crosses imported track by the usual rule. notes/T-1751.md.
- **Tokyo's peak trains an hour: tier top 27, real peaks for 12 named lines** (T-1751, my call): `calib_tyo.py` TIERS 24 to 27, and `REAL_PEAK` (T-1750's figures, 20-28) for the Yamanote, Keihin-Tohoku, Chuo, Sobu local, Tozai, Marunouchi, Ginza, Hibiya, Oedo, Den-en-toshi, Toyoko and Odakyu. The section rule (70 s plus speed over 0.6) holds a 160 km/h line to 25 an hour, so Chuo (real 28), Odakyu and the tier top ask 27 and run 25, within 10%; asking 28 ran 25 of 28, 89%. Ginza's real 30 is held at 28, which its slower sections allow. A trip is now timed so that it passes the count point (Tokyo station) in its schedule hour: before, long lines' peaks were diluted into the hour shoulders (Chuo asked 16.6 for a tier of 24).
- **No per-place rail factor; Tokyo nearer right than New York** (Anita, after T-1742): rail use stays on the network and the shared mode rules. Where real cities differ, the game should come closer to Tokyo's volumes than New York's ("I lean toward making the game easier"): look first for what holds Tokyo down in the sim (train speeds as drawn, capacity and crowding, frequency limits), T-1750. notes/T-1742.md.
- **What an edit touches, and the wait before the whole solve** (T-1733, agent's call): a city population is re-solved locally after an edit when its view (the network within its reach) changed, run times and trains an hour within 1% counting as unchanged (they move by float noise and by small knock-on changes beside a new line) and complex numbers compared as groupings. The whole solve that follows waits 8 s after the local results are shown, so an edit right after (stations, then the line) is solved locally too. A game load's own time no longer counts as a zone left alone (T-1223): all of Japan's 17-minute load had made 44 of 45 zones coarse at once. notes/T-1733.md.
- **The load card always shows a time left; "Taking a bit longer" is gone** (T-1654, my call, replacing T-1613's wording): a round past its expected length is estimated from the periods landed. They land in waves; the round's first period in gives its length times a ratio the previous round measured (2.6 of 3 waves on Japan, 2.1 on New York with 2 workers; 3 less 0.4 until one is measured), and a late round with periods still out takes 0.7 of the average wave so far per wave left (the last wave holds fewer periods). The count never climbs by degrees: an estimate over 1.4 times it (and 10 s more) replaces it in one step, a smaller excess slows it to half speed until it meets the estimate. Text "About 2 min left". The fits are from two loads on 2 workers; another worker count changes the waves, which are read from the pool.
- **Big networks: freeze longer trips, don't cut by region** (T-1650, Anita): an edit re-solves the city populations it touches against everything else frozen, the market included (T-1733-1735), since Japan has no weak links (26-39% of market trips cross any 3-6 part cut). "Constructing..." clears zone by zone, on intercity lines too, though their longer-trip riders land only with the whole solve (until T-1735). notes/T-1650.md.
- **The local refine** (T-1609, agent's call within Anita's "approximations welcome"): a zone whose level changes (fine or coarse) is solved alone against its cluster's other populations' loads frozen from the last solve, not with its whole cluster. The approximation: until the next edit that touches the cluster solves it whole, the other zones' riders do not give way to the refined zone's change of crowding on shared lines. Measured on all of China: Guangzhou's rail +0.18% against the whole solve, every other zone within 0.05%, shown in 4.7 s instead of 53 s. The frozen loads are scaled back to before the capacity fit (kept per cluster where the fit changed them), so the zone crowds on what the whole solve's last round crowded on. notes/T-1609.md.
- **Weekend timetable, the simpler version** (T-1720, Anita): a Saturday, a Sunday and a holiday that runs as a Sunday run each line's medium trains 6-24 and its low ones at night; a superholiday's days keep the weekday's; running cost charges the trains run, and the boarding account works out a weekend day's crowding on those trains. The approximation: riders stay the weekday solve's scaled by the day's load factor, so they do not react to the thinner service (no longer waits priced in, no riders lost to it, no other route chosen); a solve per kind of day (T-261) would. A line with no medium trains runs none in the weekend day. The drawn trains follow the same timetable (the sim lays departures by each line's calendar), so the account's trains are the drawn ones. Replaces "the same trains run every day".
- **Holiday parts named by their own dates** (T-1697): out, away and back days each read "Golden Week, <dates of that part>" (the calendar hover heads a run with its away dates). Reason: the day-name words are gone and each part still has to be told apart in the Day choice.
- **Light-map even-mix grey 160** (T-1694): Anita asked for "about #bbb"; at 0.8 bubble alpha and 0.9 opacity a drawn 170 already lands near #bbb on the land, so the drawn grey went a little darker (160, about #b7 shown). Her call if she meant something else.

- **No automatic crossovers; one quad station per click** (T-1687, T-1688; Anita: "we can just click twice", "people can just put crossing tracks where they need"): stations, doubling up and drawing add no crossovers, and a station click on quad goes on the pair clicked only, replacing T-733's crossover past each quad station and T-255's twin. A line whose stops are on two pairs with no way across is refused (`LinePath`) unless a longer way round exists; saves keep their crossovers. My calls: a station placed level with one on the pair beside takes its name, so the two stay one labelled complex; doubling up still twins the stations on its run (filed as T-1689 for Anita).
- **While demand is being worked out the clock is capped at 4x** (T-1665; Anita asked for about 4x): "being worked out" is any solve running (`demandUpdating`), a load going, or the zone in view shown rough, so the cap also holds for the minutes a big save re-solves after an edit; it lifts by itself and the chosen speed (or the slider's 0.5x to 100x, T-1664, not saved) returns. A line edited while running reads "constructing..." until that solve ends, or after 8 s if none started (a line with no riders to count); it is per solve, not per line, so one slow solve holds every edited line.
- Removing constructed track and stations is undoable, and the undo is free (it charges only the trains needed), reversing "removing constructed is final" (SPEC 6.4): a mistaken delete of a whole network had no way back. Nothing is gained by it, since nothing is refunded. A delete over 50 km or a tenth of the network's track (never under 5 km) asks first, in the existing dialog (T-1649).
- **The high-speed fare multiplier applies to the fare above the base, by the trip's share of km on high-speed lines** (T-1626, my call within Anita's design): fares are by whole trip length, so a trip pays base + (curve(km) - base) x (1 + (m - 1) x high-speed km / km). The base stays once a trip because the solver charges it per trip, not per ride; this makes the rule exactly "each ride's rate and curve share times its line's multiplier", which demand and income already split per line. A 15 km high-speed ride at 1.5 so pays 1.25x, not 1.5x, of its whole fare. One small lie: route choice still sees only the first stretch's rate per km times the multiplier, the curve's extra entering per station pair, as before.
- **The load's time left says "Taking a bit longer" once its estimate has run out, rather than count a guess** (T-1613, my call): when no stage in hand has expected time left, or the shown count is over 2x behind a raised estimate and 10 s off, any number would be a guess (a late round was guessed to go on a fifth of its time so far, which held "A few seconds left" up while a slow round went on). Raising the count at once was the other way; it throws the count back up, which T-1479 chose against. "A few seconds left" now means under 5 s (was 10).
- **The ramp switch is on by default, and a ramp that would land on a flat crossing at the next level counts as not fitting** (T-1615, my call): it changes only newly drawn track (saved track and edit API routes without `ramp` cross flat as before), and a flat crossing is rarely what a player wants since it costs every train on both lines waiting time; one click turns it off. Swapping one flat crossing for another a level up gains nothing, so that way is skipped like one without room. A route at 0 that also crosses water and finds no room going up does not try a bridge plus a dip under the line: going down leaves the water unbridged, fails the check, and the route gets the plain bridge and a flat crossing.

## 2026-10-27
- **"Returning visitor" is a saved game here or on the account, or the remembered last slot in localStorage; such a visitor with no saves opens on the menu, not the new game sheet** (T-1612, my call): the remembered slot (`anitabuilder-save-slot`) is written once any game is played and outlives deleting its save, so someone who deleted every save still sees Load from file. T-1441's straight-to-the-sheet now applies to a first visit only. No new storage key.
- **The ground curve fades out over the tail's own 300-700 km window, and its normaliser stays the plain curve** (T-1603, Anita's option 1 of T-1508): past 700 km only the tail carries trips, with no new constant; a closed world (no tail) keeps the plain ground. The normaliser is unchanged because trips under 300 km are the same either way and the pipeline's rows and packs need no rebuild; the cost is that a place's ground departures now total a little under 0.107 per resident (the share that went past the fade is dropped, not given to nearer towns), which is what the double count needed.
- **A move of the draw anchor shifts the drawn pieces rather than laying them again** (T-1170, my call, under Anita's "slight lie" rule): the clock worker keeps every edge's and line's points in float64 mercator (exact at any anchor) and moves the cached stroke pieces by the anchor shift in float32, so a segment can sit up to half a float32 step (at its distance from the old anchor) off its exact place: measured 3-5 cm near the view on Japan, never past 12.5 cm since a move longer than 2^22 local units (about 3,000 km) is laid exactly, as is every 8th move so the rounding cannot add up. Station boxes are exact. `?patch=check` lays every move exactly; `?patch=shiftcheck` logs the error. notes/T-1170.md.
- **The Lines and Stations tabs list the world a task after it arrives; a line chip's symbol is measured in idle time** (T-1021, my call): `listWorld` follows `world` by a `setTimeout(0)`, a new symbol shows as plain text until measured (a frame or two), the chip's CSS zoom is read after the paint, and the track's zones (`followTrack`) are worked out in a task right after the state's. Each was part of one 700-900 ms task when a big save loads; none of them changes what is shown a moment later. notes/T-1001.md "T-1021".
- **Planned lines and the blueprint are priced only when a panel shows them, a moment after the last edit** (T-1171, my call): the Build tab's quote and a planned line's Construct price are clock queries, asked 250 ms after the world last changed (at once for the C key), one answer per world version. The figure shown can lag an edit by about 300 ms; a state no longer pays for the trials (1.3 s an edit on the real New York save with 12 planned lines). notes/T-1171.md.
- **Region money shares are worked out in idle time** (T-1021, my call): each line's and the upkeep's split between regions is posted to the clock worker from `requestIdleCallback` (at most 2 s late) rather than inside the state's task; the ledger's region split can use the last network's shares for up to an hour tick after an edit. It was about 200 ms of a big save's first state. notes/T-1001.md "T-1021".
- **The market's pass goes through destination zones west to east, a window at a time** (T-1545, my call): zones by their centres' x; a station's route costs are held from its first read to its last and its trips to its assignment. On all of Japan 197 stations are read at once and 1,954 touched (the commutes' trips home make the long spans), against 562 and 6,508 in zone order and more in a Hilbert order. Searched strategies are kept to their station's assignment within 128 MB (`WINDOW_STRATEGY_BYTES`), those wanted soonest first, the rest searched again (698 of 8,877 searches on Japan). Figures move by summing order only (at worst about 1e-6 of a line or station, rail a day within 1e-7). The lean's 8,192-station gate (`LEAN_OD_BYTES`, T-1503) stays although its table is gone: lifting it costs a walk per destination.
- **The network canvas has no MSAA at dpr 3 and up** (T-955, my call): the stroke shader rounds only its caps, so a line's long side edges rely on MSAA and stair-step without it (visible at dpr 1 to 3 magnified); stations, trains and crowd outlines look the same either way. At dpr 3 a step is a third of a CSS px, and the 4x buffers there are about 80 MB on a phone, so `overlay.ts` asks for `antialias: dpr < 3`. The stencil stays (track through station boxes). Analytic side edges in STROKE_FS would let it go everywhere but would bead at the segment joints.
- **A bus runs at 70% of the car's local street speed, at most 18 km/h** (T-1577, Anita's yes to the idea; the factor and the rest my call): min(18, 0.7 x the car's density-based street speed in the zone), so 18 below about 10,000 homes and jobs a km2 and 11.55 in the densest; no extra floor, since the car's own floor is 16.5. Taken from the zone's pack in the clock worker (the track model has no density), kept equal to demand's by a test. Running cost stays $9 a bus-km, so a slow bus costs less an hour ($104 at 11.55 km/h against $162). A bus line is marked in demand by its bus cars, not by having a bus-only stop, so a short bus between two stations pays the boarding cost too. notes/T-1466.md.
- **Far out, bubbles come before lines and stations for the whole overview** (T-1550, my call): the threshold is zoom 8.5, the one where the zone bubbles replace the local ones (`OVERVIEW_ZOOM`), not a separate number: at 6.5, 7.5 and 8.5 lines cross the New York bubble in all three, and stations are not drawn before zoom 9. With a city selected the inter-city bubbles show only up to 8.5 too, so the rule is "while `cityOverview`". Closer in T-1515's network-first order stands.
- **The fare scale shows in Japan's terms, stored in India's** (T-1554, my call): the panel divides a stored scale by Japan's default (6.1, `incomeScale("JP")`), so Japan reads 1.00x, India 0.16x (the brief said about 0.17x, from a baseline of 6.0), the US 1.90x; typed values go back by the same factor. Nothing saved or sent to demand changes: `FARE_BASELINE` stays India, the curve stays in India's dollars, the fare scale in baseline units. Stepping by 0.05 shown needs finer storage than a tenth, so a player's scale is kept to a hundredth (saved tenths read back the same). The heading names the country ("Fares in Japan") on a line of its own above the stepper, so no name is cut.
- **Crowding colours gain steps above 9 minutes** (T-1553, my call): crimson at 20, plum 25, purple 35, indigo 50, and red-orange at 12, since long lines' figures fall in 9 to 20 and the real save's highest is 23 (notes/T-1553.md). The ramp up to 9 is Anita's, unchanged; the grades for text (6, 9, 15) are unchanged.
- **The solve's results carry each station's transfers** (T-1530, my call): every period result has the transfers in per route node and kind of day (the sims' `transfers_in`, scaled as the query scales its pairs), so the station bubbles' split shows with the figures; a solve's final still asks the transfer pairs and takes their answer, so finals are the query's to the bit. About 140 kB more a period result on the real save, a few ms a population; asking the workers instead waited one or more rounds (40 s on a load) and got nothing after an edit.
- **A worker's first figures estimate its other periods from its first** (T-1538, my call, over T-1206's coarse level and bigger commute chunks): in a rough solve and in the free-flow preview after an edit, each population's other periods are its first period's free flow scaled by the periods' trips (everyday purpose by purpose), on their own graphs; the preview's periods are solved after it and the rough solve is never reused, so finals are unchanged. The coarse level would need a second copy of the zone in every worker for a 15-20% shorter period; the pool is at most 3, so dropping a worker's second period saves 35-40%.
- **A cached demand result is found by the save's network inputs before the network** (T-1539, my call): a SHA-256 of the save's track bytes and the header fields the snapshot reads, the result holding the snapshot that load worked out; the arriving network is compared with it. A game opened from the menu also fits the camera to the save's built stations and shows only their realms as soon as the save is read, since the zoomed-out bubbles need the camera there.
- **A game load shows the map at the zone in view's first figures** (T-1533, my call, replacing T-1051's "drawn once, when the card goes"): the shade, outlines and bubbles come in one step when a cached result, a rough solve or an estimate gives the zone in view figures, behind the card; the card and the held clock still wait for the final (T-347 unchanged). Anita asked for the city in view's demand within about 5 s, and the blank map hid figures that were in for a minute or more.
- **A rough solve of the zone in view goes first** (T-1533, my call): while a whole solve waits for packs, the zone in view alone, no market, as an estimate never final; it holds the other packs' opens back until its free-flow round, which can put the final a few seconds later on a game like the real save (5 zones), traded for the zone in view's figures 4-8 times sooner. City packs open one at a time and a game's market packs go to the workers after its city packs, since composing China's 370 blocked a worker for a minute.
- **A loose-end ring means track you can see** (T-1534, agent's call, completing T-1485's "only on drawn track"): the rings show at the loose ends of built track and active blueprints, not a switched-off blueprint (drawn grey and faint) and not a piece under 4 px long on screen at the current zoom (`MIN_STUB_PX`; the length is summed along the piece, so it comes back as the map zooms in). The old filter tested `net.edgePts`, which holds every edge, so it passed all of them.
- **The market's rides reserve a line per track component; the local layers' do not** (T-1511, my call): past the 6 cheapest lines a market zone's rides take up to 2 more, the cheapest line of each track component the 6 miss (`RIDE_TRACK_MAX`). Applied to the local layers too it moved New York's rail trips +3,820 and China's +29,000 with nothing the market reads changed, so it stays the market's. Market passes on China about 1.1-1.3x, New York and Tokyo unchanged (notes/T-1511.md).

## 2026-10-26
- **The pointer is off the map over anything laid on it** (T-1522, my call 2026-10-26): the loose-end rings take the pointer from the window and count it on the map only over the map itself (MapLibre's canvas container), so over the view tabs or any control inside the map's box they go, as when it leaves the map.
- **A terminal draws a buffer stop only where the track ends** (T-1527, my call 2026-10-26): the dark bar is at the platform end with no track beyond it, found as a station with one track leaving it (flag 512, the -axis end, where the track stops at the node); a terminal with track on both sides has no bar, since there is no buffer stop to show, and its sidings already mark both ends. The hover on the bar follows.
- **Station ridership circles are one disc shaded by the transfers' share, with a faint edge** (T-1531, my calls 2026-10-26, replacing T-1448's nested discs): the colour runs from the entries blue to the transfers green, evened in OKLab as the bubble ramp is, linear in the share (no skew), the end colours kept (light #2456e0 and #169a50, dark #8aa6ff and #52d68e). Origins and Transfers are the two ends. Edge 1 px at half strength over a 2.5 px casing at 30% (was 1.75 px at 95% over 3.5 px at 55%); fill 20% light, 26% dark. The Total hover's total row gets a dot in the circle's own colour. The mode mix's light-map grey (#aaaaaa, T-1450) belongs to the local bubbles and is untouched.
- **The font is Nunito and not a setting; related lines start on** (T-1524, T-1529, my calls 2026-10-26): a stored `font` is dropped on load with the other retired settings and the Zen Maru Gothic fallback for kana and kanji stays. The related lines toggle was never stored (a signal kept while the page is open), so its default simply went to on and no old choice needed carrying over.
- **Clicks and hovers pick lines, stations and trains before demand bubbles** (T-1515, Anita 2026-10-26, reversing T-097's "Clicks: trains and stations first, then a bubble, then the rest of the network", which the code had become bubbles first, and T-486's "a click never acts on something drawn beneath another clickable thing" for these): a small bubble's reach past its disc (`MIN_PICK_PX`, `bubblePad`) hid the lines and bus routes behind it. With the select tool, a rail or bus line, station or train within its own reach now wins, for the click, the bubble tag, the cursor and the track tag alike; a bubble keeps its wider reach where none of those is near. Track and junctions without a line still give way to a bubble.
- **A trip's fare curve extra is shared between the lines it rides by km** (T-1516, my call 2026-10-26): it was all put on the line the trip first boards, so under a tapering curve (Anita's China save: $0.05 a km to 20 km, then $0.031) a feeder bus into a long rail trip took the whole trip's discount and its fares went below zero (Bus 13 in Shenzhen, "-$312k"). Now each line takes the base share of its boarding and the curve's fare a km on its own km, which is never below zero; the network's total is unchanged. Splitting the whole fare by km (the bus would lose most of its base) was the other choice; boarding plus km keeps the rule players already see.
- **A zone's access list reserves slots by track, not by line** (T-1506, my call 2026-10-26): past its 8 best stations a list keeps the best station of each track component none of them is on, up to 10. A slot per unlisted line (the proposal) cost Tokyo 1.28x the market's pass time for +30 longer boardings and added nothing on China, since a line on linked track is reached by changing trains. notes/T-1506.md.
- **Loose-end marks show near the pointer, as accent rings** (T-1485, my call 2026-10-26): Anita's "floating station bubbles" with the track and station tools were the loose-end marks, white with a dark rim like a station, at every loose end in view, many at the ends of faint unserved or switched-off track that does not show at city zoom. Now a hollow accent ring within 160 px of the pointer (all of them on a touch screen), and only on track the network draws. Not reproduced: a loose end with no drawn track at all; the drawn-track rule covers it if it exists.
- **A line's past-24-hour running cost is held at the service its fares were solved for** (T-1492, my call 2026-10-26): both figures are the rate in force times 24 hours, and fares can only move when demand lands, so the running cost now uses the trains and cars the shown demand result solved (`LineDemand.tph`, `cars`; `todayService` in game/today.ts) rather than an estimate of the new fares; a line with no figures yet, or a cached result, uses its trains now.
- **A zone in view without its bubbles says "Loading data…" and asks again** (T-1493, my call 2026-10-26): the gap Anita saw was the bubbles being asked for and built after the zone's figures were in (up to seconds while the workers solve), which no loading state covered; the status tag now covers it, and an ask that ends with nothing is retried from 1 s doubling to 8 s, since a dropped answer (a worker replaced, a zone not yet in the result) was otherwise asked again only at the next result.
- **Station ridership circles are nested discs over the bubbles** (T-1448, T-1449, my calls 2026-10-26): of three looks tried on the real save (nested discs, a pie, a two-colour ring; artifacts/trainworld/t1448/look-*.png), the nested one reads best and stays calmest: a blue disc for all riders with a green inner disc of the transfers' share of the area, translucent, each edge on a thin casing of the land so it holds against the purple bubbles. The ring was loud, the pie's wedge hard to read. Total is entries plus transfers (each rider counted once, where they board), so blue is trips starting there, not starting or ending. The circles moved off the network canvas to a 2D canvas over the bubbles; their hover and clicks come before the bubbles, but the network under them keeps its own. The ridership row sits second at the map's upper left, between the bubbles and the track rows; folding a row keeps its choice in force. One circle per complex always; the old setting is dropped from storage.
- **One rider is drawn from the boarding account's shares, and the explorer link sets the purpose filter** (T-1436, my calls 2026-10-26): no per-rider or per-stop-pair figure exists, so the rider's stops come from the period's boardings and the line's alighting shares, their purpose from the stretch's riders by purpose; the two assumptions are that who is aboard got on in proportion to what boarded and stayed through the shares, and that purpose is independent of the stops. Opening the explorer sets the map's purpose filter to the rider's purpose (the explorer reads that filter, and "all" would show whichever purpose has most trips between the stations) and its period to the rider's; the filter stays set when she comes back to the train.
- **Station ink is #8f8b85 in dark, new bubbles and light-mode grey** (T-1432, T-1441, T-1450, my calls 2026-10-26): the station box rim, terminal end bars, upgrade marks and selection outline use their own `stationInk` (the map's #2b2b2b in light, #8f8b85 in dark): bright enough to read on the dark land and the terminal bars, not the map ink's near white, which made the boxes loud. The mode mix's even-share grey is 170 on the light map (190 in dark), the one theme-dependent bubble colour; the shade is part of the scheme key so built discs repaint on a switch. Starting any new game (the sheet's Start, a world-map start too) sets Destinations on, replacing T-801's Homes for world-map starts; the setting stays per browser, so opening a saved game keeps what was stored. A first visit with no saved or account games opens on the new game sheet, with Back to the one-button menu.
- **The trouble list is a corner button, not a dock tab** (T-1433, my call 2026-10-26): the map's lower right above the attribution button, where nothing else sits and it never competes with the top bar or panels; a tab would hide it from anyone on another tab, and a list some players will not treat as real trouble should cost nothing shut. Its cuts keep the real NYC save at 20 rows, not 96: only the map's orange and red capacity tags (90% and up), one row per kind by a station; riders left behind from 3% of those wanting the line; full trains only at a quarter of trains and with fewer left behind (a line leaving riders behind is one row, its full trains in the hover). Reversals sit second, after broken lines, since they are usually an accident and cheap to fix.
- **Imported names are tidied by shape, in the sim's load** (T-1442, T-330, my call 2026-10-26): a save has no "imported" flag, so `track/names.rs` runs on every `load` and in `realsave`, and changes only a name with the untidy shape: a station name with a part in capitals (two words, or one of five letters), a line name that is a real feed's key, a space and the route, with no symbol. Titles keep initialisms (HBLR, NJT, PATH) and drop a trailing "STATION"; a mixed-case "Seoul Station" or "Battersea Power Station" stays, since cutting those makes a wrong name. A player's own name in capitals ("PARIS") would be re-cased too; accepted. Lines lose the feed word ("subway 6X" is "6X", "lirr Babylon Branch" "Babylon Branch"); a chip with no symbol shows a code of up to five capitals or digits whole, else the first letter.

## 2026-10-20
- **China's zones take the `china` module through their zone rule** (T-1185, my call 2026-10-25): `ZONE_RULES["CHN"]["module"]`, as the US's zones take `usa`, rather than a plan_config fallback by country, so the plan row says where its people come from; rows already built get it from T-1189's replan, which rewrites every Chinese zone row. notes/T-1185.md.
- **China's zones replanned whole, not repaired** (T-1189, my call 2026-10-25): every Chinese zone is rebuilt for the township census anyway, so keeping T-1140's passing rows saves no build; `zones --replan` regroups all of a country's zone rows, a zone with an old row's exact units keeping its id, order and status (80 of 638). 660 zones become 638, 82 under the 1M floor (T-1140's repair design: 608, 57). notes/T-1185.md 3.
- **In China the two-cities rule counts a city from 200,000** (T-1189, my call 2026-10-25): `ZONE_RULES["CHN"]["city_pop"]`, a fifth of China's 1M floor as T-1191's 100,000 is of 0.5M. At 100,000, 821 of China's 2,418 free counties hold one and their shape joins stop: 681 zones with 140 under the floor, mostly county towns; at 200,000, 638 with 82 (without the rule, 629 with 72). notes/T-1185.md 3. (T-033, my call 2026-10-24): our layer draws on its own canvas and context, outside MapLibre's programs, and the prelude's `projectTile` takes tile coordinates; so every vertex shader of ours calls `project()` (`map/gl.ts` `PROJECT_GLSL`), the same sphere, horizon depth and globe-to-flat blend as MapLibre's `_projection_globe.vertex.glsl`, fed from the custom layer's `defaultProjectionData`. On the flat map it is exactly the old `u_matrix * p` (screenshots identical), and from zoom 12 the globe is the flat map, so close-in precision is untouched.
- **Globe close in follows MapLibre: flat from zoom 12, building everywhere** (T-033, my call 2026-10-24): MapLibre's "globe" is a globe to zoom 11 and blends to the flat map by 12; we draw and pick through the same camera, so the tools need no switch to flat and stay on at every zoom (picks and the track tool checked at zoom 5.5-10 off centre). Far side: points behind the horizon clip by MapLibre's depth (a station or train goes whole with its centre), project to NaN for picking and hovers, and DOM marks there hide (`hiddenOnGlobe`).
- **Globe sizes stay the flat map's px at the same zoom** (T-033, my call 2026-10-24): lines, dots, bubbles and trains keep their flat-map size in px (`pxPerUnitAt`), rather than being rescaled by latitude to the globe's true scale; the overview bubbles are px-sized anyway, and a ground-sized bubble far from the view's latitude differs by the Mercator factor as it does on the flat map.
- **The globe setting is Settings > Map, "Flat" or "Globe", flat by default, before Theme** (T-033, my call 2026-10-24); its hover says the globe becomes the flat map zoomed in to a city.
- **The realm shade on the globe is cut into triangles no longer than 1/64 of Mercator's width, with caps over both poles** (T-033, my call 2026-10-24): about 0.3 px from the sphere at world zoom; the caps (the realms never reach a pole) are drawn only on the whole globe, since MapLibre fades its own poles while it blends to flat.
- **Fares never go below $0 at 0 km, so demand and income agree; the free-short-trips curve is no longer possible** (T-1114 sim batch D, Anita's orchestrator call 2026-10-24): with the multi-node fare curve gone from the sim, demand charges a base plus a rate per km on each ride, and a line below $0 at 0 km (say $0 at 5 km, then $0.10 a km) cannot be that. The Money tab holds the dragged or typed node where the line meets $0 at 0 km (`fitOrigin` in `game/fareCurve.ts`; `tidyNodes` raises an older save's first node), and `faresFor` is the same base and rate with no clamp.
- **A map start's "every realm shown" ends at its first station or track** (T-1133, my call 2026-10-24): it is a picking aid, not the player showing each realm, so it does not count as "shown this session"; a map-start game with nothing built shows them all, also after a reload, and with a station shows what any game would, so a reload never changes the look. Kept as a set of every realm (not "everything shows"), so the shade still darkens the countries no zone covers; the cut, 151k triangles, runs in the shade worker (notes/T-1133.md).
- **Uncrowded zoomed out by dot spacing, not by realm size** (T-1133, my call 2026-10-24): Anita asked for small realms to draw no per-city bubbles zoomed out. Crowding comes from how close dots sit, and big realms crowd as much (Germany's 113 zones span 124 px at zoom 2.5, eastern China's 201 about 156 px), while one-zone realms never do; so every overview zoom leaves out an unopened zone's dot within 20 px of a bigger zone's mark (`DOT_GAP`), keeping each area's biggest cities. Opened zones always draw.
- **Current location's start** (T-1132, my call 2026-10-24): the zone whose coarse outline holds the location, else the nearest centre among zones whose box is within 40 km (the view's own reach, `ZONE_REACH_M`), the camera on the location; farther out a map start centred there at zoom 4, so a player in an uncovered country sees what lies near rather than being dropped into a far city. Waits 15 s for an answer; no cached position.
- **Ground track 0.5x, elevated (+1) 1.0x** (T-1068, Anita 2026-10-23): "raise 0 elevation rail cost from 0.3x to 0.5x (to account for the like building knockouts and road crossings that we're not actually computing). also i guess raise +1 elevated rail to 1.0x". `LEVEL_MULT` in `params.rs`; ground is now US$45M a km of double track, +1 US$90M, the same as -1. Only new construction and blueprint quotes change (stations, junctions and crossings priced from the level follow); built track keeps what was paid, and upkeep is per route-km, not by level.
- **"Ready" for the map is the loading card going** (T-1051, my call 2026-10-23): `mapBlank` is the start menu or a game load not yet released (set at the pick and at every whole-game load, cleared where the clock is released, on a failed demand load and on "play without demand"), not the first world snapshot: the realms are known well before the first demand, but Anita wants the end state drawn once. A load can take 15 s or more (Tokyo, the NYC save over a minute), so the basemap alone shows that long; the card is the only thing over it. Dead space (`demandIdle`) is never blank.
- **World view draws no cities and no region circles below zoom 2.5** (T-1046, my call 2026-10-23): `CITY_MARKS_MIN_ZOOM` in `map/intercityView.ts`; at 2.5 a 1,100 px map shows about 140 degrees, a hemisphere, with 10 or so circles, which still reads; the whole world on a 1,400-1,900 px map is zoom 1.6-2.1. Only the unasked overview is hidden: a city the player selected still draws its trips at any zoom.
- **A region circle is an unopened zone's dot, 16 px** (T-1046, my call 2026-10-23): fill from the Unopened zones setting at the bubble opacity and a 1 px map ink outline, as the overview's dots for zones not opened; those are 4-10 px across, opened cities 10 to 70 px, so 16 px is a little bigger than a typical dot without outweighing a real city.
- **The shaded city grey is the outside shade** (T-1048, my call 2026-10-23): the fill over everything outside the focused city (7.5% of #182536 light, 35% black dark); a hidden realm is that plus the `inactive` fill (total 28% and 64%). Half way to it is a total of 18% and 50%, which is just the `inactive` opacity halved (11%, 22.5%).
- **A click opens a hidden realm only when it did nothing else** (T-1049, my call 2026-10-23): select tool, no Shift, not linking stations, inside one of the realm's zone outlines (not sea or dead space, which are shaded too), the world zoom excluded, and the click left no selection (checked after the pick's own handler ran), so a bubble or line drawn over a hidden realm (a selected city's far ends) keeps its click. The shaded land has no hover or pointer cursor of its own: the circle's tag says to click the shaded area.
- **Labels: 130 px between labelled bubbles, 28 x 16 px between label boxes** (T-1050, my call 2026-10-23): larger box gaps alone only moved a label to another side of its bubble (Nagoya's hopped above its bubble), so the cluster stayed; keeping bubbles apart drops the smaller one of a cluster and the next biggest elsewhere takes its slot, still 8 at most.
- **A unit's fixed list is filtered to the registry, not rebuilt** (T-997, my call 2026-10-23): `intercity.solve_units` costs about 6 minutes on China's 1,516 outlines, and a parked pack leaving a group cannot add a neighbour within the cut, so dropping unregistered ids gives the same list.
- **Planned US rows spread blocks from their internal points** (T-1016, my call 2026-10-23): the `usa` module reads only each state's block .dbf (8 MB by range for Georgia), so a block's people go over the cells nearer its internal point than any other block's by GHSL, and its jobs by the jobs proxy, rather than over the block polygon by land as the built packs' spread.py does; the polygons would be about 20 times the download. County totals come out within 0.5% for people and 1.3% for jobs on the test rows.
- **Planned US rows: home end from population, one work-from-home share** (T-1016, my call 2026-10-23): tier 0's home end (population x commuters a resident) and ACS B08301 over the row's counties, where the built packs use LODES RAC and ACS by tract and industry; both would need new tier 0 hooks for a small change (RAC: T-1034).
- **Puerto Rico rows take the `usa` module's outline, people and work from home** (T-1016, my call 2026-10-23): TIGER municipios, 2020 blocks less institutions and ACS B08301 all cover it; jobs (no LODES), the curve and one region stay tier 0's, as for any country.
- **US metros by Europe's rule** (T-1004, my call 2026-10-23): a GHS-FUA commuting area over 1M (54, giving 44 rows beside the built packs), not an urban centre over 1M (25 in the US: low-density cities such as Atlanta, Tampa, St. Louis and Charlotte have centres under 1M and would have been countryside zones).
- **US countryside zones: counties grown within their state to 0.5M, cap 1.5M, no dead space, contiguous** (T-1004, my call 2026-10-23): Anita's floor (China's 1M was for counties already a European zone's size; the median free US county holds 23,000); every county in a zone, as "whole states as zones" asks, with T-1000's cap for the huge western zones. Small states come out one zone each.
- **US rows take a `usa` module, zones included** (T-1004, my call 2026-10-23): census blocks less institutions, LODES jobs and sectors, the US curve 0.8/30 and the 0.23 state factor, TIGER county outlines, on the tier 0 runner. The sources are national, open, already coded for the 13 built US packs they will be solved with, and clearly better than GHSL and the jobs proxy; Europe's zones keep global sources only. Schools, universities and airports stay tier 0 until T-1019.
- **Alaska: one zone of its road and rail belt; the four small territories out** (T-1004, my call 2026-10-23): Anchorage, Mat-Su, Kenai, Fairbanks, Denali, Southeast Fairbanks, Chugach and Copper River (0.66M, the Alaska Railroad's line); the rest of Alaska (0.16M, the Aleutians across the 180th meridian among it) is dead space. Hawaii and Puerto Rico are in; Guam, the Northern Marianas, American Samoa and the US Virgin Islands (each under 0.2M) wait for their own regions.
- **Riverside County is a metro row** (T-1004, my call 2026-10-23): GHSL's Los Angeles area holds San Bernardino but not Riverside, and a 2.4M county cannot be a zone under the 1.5M cap; Imperial County joins it rather than cross into Arizona.
- **US joint commutes by Census division** (T-1004, my call 2026-10-23): eight solve regions, Delaware, Maryland and DC with the Northeast so the 13 built packs solve together; the Northeast leaves its own `intercity.json` solve for the general one (T-1014).
- **The US's metro rows are planned alone and kept by other replans** (T-1015, my call 2026-10-23): `world_plan.py metros --countries US` plans US FUAs against US counties only and writes only US rows (`FUA_COUNTRIES`); a replan of the other metros keeps them, since the plan's `state.pkl` holds no US units and `--fresh` would recompute every country.
- **San Diego and San Francisco are named in the plan** (T-1015, my call 2026-10-23): GHSL's "Tijuana" and "San Jose" are replaced in the plan rows (`METRO_NAMES`) rather than only in zone-names.json, so the pack ids follow the cities (snd, snf), not Tijuana.
- **Across water, a zone joins another region's only under 80% of the floor, as by land** (T-1015, my call 2026-10-23): South Jersey (0.44M) stays a zone of its own instead of joining Delaware's across the bay. It holds for every `contiguous` country's next replan, China's and Taiwan's planned rows included.
- **Geofabrik extracts: a set of states beats a regional group** (T-1015, my call 2026-10-23): `extracts` weighs sibling extracts that group no other sibling against a single covering one, by area, and takes a smaller single extract anywhere in the index that covers the outline: Chicago takes Illinois and Indiana rather than us-midwest, Cincinnati and Louisville no longer the whole US, Puerto Rico its own extract rather than central-america. Recomputed for every other row in the plan, nothing else changes.
- **Puerto Rico's rows take the `usa` module as archetype C** (T-1015, my call 2026-10-23): population and work from home from the module, jobs from tier 0 (no LODES there).
- **Long commutes: cap the job pull at 20x, add back 1%** (T-1000, Anita 2026-10-23): inside a zone a job's pull is at most 20 times what distance alone gives; 1% of each zone's workers commute to jobs 50-150 km away through the between-zone commutes. Built before the US joins; the built countries' joins rerun later in one fix batch.
- **T-1000's constants, as built** (T-1000, my call 2026-10-23): the cap 20 against each pack's own job-weighted geometric mean pull (`kernel::PULL_CAP`), the share 1% (`joint_gravity` `LONG_SHARE`, about Japan's 1.6% crossing a prefecture line), the ring's inner edge 50 km (`LONG_KM`; where a daily commute becomes a trip between towns, and about the 16 km x ln 20 the cap is worth on China's curve), outer 150 km (the existing cut). Each pack's unheld columns share one fill so its net exchange stays nil; the add-back leaves the balanced shares by pair as they were (jobs scaled with homes, not reduced by its arrivals).
- **Ring zones: a planner guard now, regroups later** (T-1005, Anita 2026-10-23): the planner skips a neighbour that would make a zone wrap another (half of B inside A's hull, A holding half of B's zone border); built countryside rings merge under the cap or re-split, in the same later batch; rings round a metro stay. Brugge-Kortrijk and Lausanne-Fribourg-Neuchâtel are not merged (separate cities).
- **One later fix batch** (Anita 2026-10-23): T-1000's joins, T-1005's regroups (Taiwan's Zhubei split, T-1002, among them) and T-903's European merges run together after China and the US.

- **Hidden regions are shaded by the zones' own outlines, not land shapes** (T-1006, orchestrator's call 2026-10-23, on Anita's "without relying on the accuracy of a shapefile"): one shade over the whole world, sea included, with the shown regions' zones cut out along `outlines.json`, so the edge is a zone outline and never a coast; gaps the zones enclose (central Spain, central France) count as shown. The app no longer reads `realms.geojson`; a station's region is the zone whose coarse outline holds it, else the nearest zone centre, and a region's button sits at its zone nearest the mean of its zones' centres (my calls).
- **Xinjiang's joint commutes are solved with no fixed neighbours, and a solve runs up to 300 steps** (T-998, my call 2026-10-23): held at their own factors, any one of the huge packs around it (Golmud alone, or Golmud, Zhangye, Alxa, Shigatse and Lhasa together) kept Xinjiang's column error at 1e-3 to 4e-2 through 500 steps; alone it reaches 9e-9 at step 249 (`intercity.SOLVE_ALONE`). Its commutes with those regions come from their own solves, which hold Xinjiang's packs. The cap went from 150 to 300 since the best step is kept, so a stalled solve only spends time.
- **An OSM relation holding most of another row's unit is not matched to a neighbour** (T-897 wave 4, my call 2026-10-23): `osm_units.foreign` drops it from a unit's several-relation match, since geoBoundaries draws some city districts far smaller than OSM, and the zone built first took the city's centre (Yingkou's Zhanqian into Dashiqiao's zone, Qitaihe's Xinxing into Bin's). The build order no longer decides which zone holds a city.
- **Huge western zones ship with commutes of 50-160 km** (T-897 wave 4, my call 2026-10-23): under the 1M floor Golmud's zone is 680,000 km², Lhasa's 659,000, Wensu's 556,000; the gravity sends commutes between oases far apart (gol 164 km, weu 112, smz 100, aks 91), relaxed per row with reasons as big zones are (Anita, 2026-10-22). Cutting dead space from them waits until performance or play needs it.
- **Leagues and prefectures name zones as autonomous prefectures do; two centres in one zone show the bigger city** (T-897 wave 4, my call 2026-10-23): a league's or prefecture's seat (Xilinhot, Ulanhot, Hotan, Artux, Tacheng) is an alias, never the zone's name; a zone holding two prefecture-level cities' centres shows the bigger with the other an alias (Wuhai with Shizuishan, Tonghua with Baishan, Jixi with Qitaihe, Zhangye with Jiuquan and Jiayuguan, Lhasa with Chamdo, Nyingchi and Shannan), and Karamay, a prefecture-level city, names the zone led by Shihezi.
- **An autonomous prefecture's seat does not rename its zone** (T-897 wave 3, my call 2026-10-23): wave 2's rule (a zone holding a prefecture city's centre shows that city) is for prefecture-level cities; the seats of autonomous prefectures are small county-level cities, so a zone holding one keeps its most populous county's name with the seat as an alias (Dujiangyan with Kangding, Pingwu with Barkam, Tengchong with Mangshi, Lanping with Shangri-La and Lushui, Kaili with Duyun, Panzhou with Xingyi, Wenshan with Mengzi). Which zone holds a centre is read from the built outlines (OSM), not the plan's geoBoundaries units: Yibin's centre is in yib, Guilin's in Lingui's zone, Beihai's and Qinzhou's both in Hepu's (shown Beihai).
- **A sliver neighbour extract is dropped from a Chinese zone's row** (T-897 wave 3, my call 2026-10-23): under about 1% of the outline outside the main extract (jng into Yunnan 0.68%, wao into Shaanxi 0.91%, qib into South-East Asia 1.17% at the Vietnam border), so one zone does not chain whole provinces into one region and one gate; the outline comes from the main extract's OSM admin polygons anyway.
- **Regions are "realm" in code** (T-975, 2026-10-23): realms.json, pipeline/realms.py, packs/realms.geojson and the registry's `realm`, since pipeline/regions.py already exists; players see only the region names. Land: Natural Earth admin 0 cut by geoBoundaries ADM1 (China, Russia) or the Census states (US), parts over 2,000 km from all of a region's zones dropped; the US split at the Census West region, Russia at the Urals by federal district.
- **A clicked region lasts the game in hand** (T-975, 2026-10-23, Anita's answer): it resets at each load unless a station is there; everything shows until the first game's first snapshot, so a loading save never flashes dark. A station's region is the land holding it, else its zone's.
- **Builds run one thread each; other runner steps take all `--jobs`** (T-966, 2026-10-23): a build step uses about 0.91 cores whatever it is given, and a run's builds are idle while its reads, gate or join run.
- **Pack outline boxes cached in `data/work/pack_boxes.json`** (T-963, 2026-10-23), keyed by name, size and mtime, not beside the packs; the coastline read from `data/raw/osmdata/water-polygons-split-4326.fgb`, made from the zip when missing or older, `zip_fid` keeping the zip's order.
- **The browser retry lives in city-browser.mjs** (T-965, 2026-10-23): a timed-out zone gets one try in a fresh page; rows split over the 3 sessions in plan order, in contiguous chunks.
- **A Chinese zone holding a prefecture city's centre takes the city's name** (T-897 wave 2, my call 2026-10-23): when the centre districts sit in a zone led by a county (Hengyang's in Hengshan's zone, Changde's in Hanshou's, Jingmen's in Zhijiang's), the zone shows the city and the county becomes an alias; the county's namesake zone keeps "County" (Hengyang County, Ji'an County). Jiangxi's Fuzhou shows Linchuan, beside Fujian's Fuzhou metro; Taiwan's island zones show Penghu and Kinmen, the names English uses for the islands.
- **Taiwan's national figures from its own statistics** (T-897, my call 2026-10-23): the World Bank has no Taiwan, so `tier0.NATIONAL` holds its employment ratio and share aged 0-14 (DGBAS 2024, the Ministry of the Interior end 2024); hospital beds take the world figure and tertiary students go unscaled, as for any place without a figure.
- **One anita.garden account shared with noritetsu** (T-972, Anita: either is fine, 2026-10-23): sharing needed only a token check and a delete hook on the server; deleting the account from either page deletes both pages' data.
- **No account icon until the server says so** (T-972, 2026-10-23): the game shows sign-in only when `/noritetsu/config` reports `trainworld`, and a localhost page has no account server unless `?acct=local|live`, so smoke and the pre-deploy live page make no failing request.
- **Game ids are per browser slot** (T-972, T-973, 2026-10-23): kept in localStorage, not the save header; a game that turns sandbox sends one notice and the server refuses it for good. Join stays enabled in a sandbox game with "Sandbox games are not listed" under it, since joining is per account.
- **Unopened cities: light grey dots with a dark outline** (T-976, Anita 2026-10-23, replacing plain black): fill #d9d9d9 with a 1 px ink outline, at least 2 px radius so the outline leaves some fill; Black, Maroon and Grey stay as choices. Stored settings below version 8 take the new default once.
- **Which market rows to redo comes from a record of their inputs** (T-929, 2026-10-22): each row's pack hash, place, row hash and centre, plus the market_pack binary and the rule, not the ids a caller passes, so parks, restores and runs of older code are caught; anything unexplained redoes every row.
- **The gate's rates test is built before the first region** (T-931, 2026-10-22): synchronously at the run's start rather than beside the first builds, to stay inside `--jobs`; the first recompile is paid up front.
- **Chinese county names keep "County" when bare would mislead** (T-897, my call 2026-10-22): a one-syllable county shows "Cao County", "He County" (and the pilot's Pei), as English writes them; a county sharing its name with a city or county nearby shows "Puyang County", "Quzhou County" (Hebei's), "Yucheng County" (Henan's). Two real cities of one name both keep it (Suzhou in Jiangsu and Anhui, Taizhou in Jiangsu and Zhejiang, Fuyang in Anhui and Zhejiang); the map tells them apart.
- **A pack with no OSM medical facility spreads its visits by jobs** (T-897, my call 2026-10-22): Qihe and Yi County have none; an empty medical purpose, the last slot, made the kernel drop it and the empty slots before it (3-4 purposes, the gate wants 6). Jobs are tier 0's fallback for missing places, and the draw is relative. Parks: one park off every cell no longer fails a pack (two must, Yongjia had 1 of 17).
- **A loading card that cannot finish gives way to the failure screen** (T-950, my call 2026-10-22): a clock worker failing before its first game and an uncaught error in main.ts's setup fail at once ("The game could not start", Try again alone, since there is no game to play without demand); otherwise a watchdog fails a card that sees nothing move for 3 minutes with no bar, 10 with one (a big pack batch opening on a slow phone sends nothing for minutes). Query answers and the clock's money ticks do not count as movement, and a hidden page counts no quiet. Times in `ui/LoadingScreen.tsx`.
- **No big calibration at the end** (Anita, 2026-10-22): demand is calibrated in parts, task by task, as it has been since T-636; T-551, which waited on a deep calibration, is settled.
- **No incremental join** (T-928, my call on measurements, 2026-10-22): a solve unit holding a new row is still solved whole. Holding far members at their factors, measured on four real cases, was slower on compact units (Jiangsu with xuz new: 378 s against 200 s whole) and, where fast, not the full result (GB with hih new: 155 s against 765 s, zones up to 0.9% off, since the whole solve also repairs drift from earlier solves). Starting from the old factors took as many steps. `joint_gravity --hold-report` stays as a diagnostic.
- **Publishing reads a freeze** (T-928, my call 2026-10-22): hard links into `data/frozen/<stamp>/` of every file a production build copies, plus copies of the four game JSON files, made by the runner after each clean join or by hand when no run is live; about 2 s and no space on NTFS, safe because pipeline writers replace files rather than rewrite them (a size and time manifest catches one that does not). The newest three are kept.
- **One browser session per region gate** (T-928, my call 2026-10-22): all of a region's rows in one sandbox game, each zone's test line removed after its checks, 20 zones a page load; kept per region rather than once a batch, since China's regions are provinces of 30-100 zones and a batch-end check would change when rows park. The fixed waits became waits on the condition, capped at the old times. Same riders and purposes as one zone alone; about 10 s a zone instead of 27-30.
- **The gate's other-packs check compares every pack** (T-928, a bug, 2026-10-22): `other_packs_unchanged` overwrote the registry it filtered by with the first pack's hashes, so it compared one pack; any FAIL it gives now is a real change.
- **Three loading bars at most** (T-939, my call 2026-10-22): the zone in view first, then the loading zones whose centres are nearest the map's centre, re-picked when a move ends. Zones of one load share a fraction, so "most advanced" would tie; nearest the player's eye is what they will look at. Cap `MAX_BARS` in `map/zoneLoading.ts`.
- **A selected bubble: everything solid and 2 px bigger** (T-936, my call 2026-10-22): "selected" is a commuter-bubble or zone-bubble (city) selection; the Opacity slider is ignored for every bubble on screen meanwhile, and the radius gets +2 CSS px (hit tests too, `bubblePad` in `map/hovered.ts`), not +2 px only on the small ones, so sizes keep their order.
- **Driving 10% quicker, owning a car about 30% dearer** (T-938, Anita's ask, my steps 2026-10-22): every local drive speed x 1.1 (16.5 / 44 / 88 km/h, open highway 110) and ownership $2.00 a trip + $0.13 a km (was $1.50 + $0.10). No speed had ever been cut, so +10% rather than undoing a cut; the two pull against each other and ownership wins slightly: ceilings A and B up about a point (Houston A 86.4 to 87.9), real networks +0.4-1.1% rail. notes/T-938.md.
- **Big zones are fine; only small ones merge** (T-903, Anita 2026-10-22): the gate's `area_km2` over 40,000 km² is a warning, no longer a fail (Highlands and Islands is 41,100). Zones too small in people or area are the ones to merge (the people / 0.5M + km² / 10,000 rule).
- **Vestland's drive factor relaxed** (T-903, my call 2026-10-22): `drive_factor_median` 2.08 against the 2.0 cap (fjords make every drive long), as `gate_relax` on its plan row; all its other checks passed.
- **De facto lines are a short explicit list** (T-897, my call 2026-10-22): `world_plan.DE_FACTO` clips geoBoundaries units only along real lines of control and the territories kept apart (China against India, Bhutan, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau; India and Pakistan in Kashmir; Crimea and the Golan with the side holding them, Anita), not against every other Natural Earth row, which dropped Åland and moved border villages where the two sources merely draw the line differently.
- **China's zones are contiguous; islets stay out** (T-897, Anita's no-exclave rule, my numbers 2026-10-22): `zone_groups(contiguous=True)` crosses at most 10 km of water to a neighbour; island zones of one region join within 50 km (Penghu, Kinmen); a zone that joins nothing and holds under 10,000 people is dead space (Matsu at GHSL's 7.8k, Green Island, Orchid Island, Xiaoliuqiu). Units left alone inside one metro join it (Jianghai, Lüshunkou, Sanzhi, Jinshan, Shimen).
- **China solves its joint commutes by province** (T-897, my call 2026-10-22): rows carry `solve_region` (Beijing, Tianjin and Hebei together; Shanghai with Jiangsu), and `intercity.solve_units` treats each as a country, neighbours within 150 km held fixed (T-786's way), since one group of about 730 packs passes MAX_GROUP_ZONES and every wave would re-solve all of it.
- **The Pearl River Delta by prefecture; Macau and Hong Kong alone** (T-897, 2026-10-22, Anita's split): Guangzhou, Shenzhen, Dongguan, Foshan, Zhongshan and Jiangmen rows; the planned zhu row held only Macau's units, so it is now Zhuhai, and Macau (mcu) and Hong Kong (hon) are rows of their own countries. Every Chinese row runs on Beijing time, Xinjiang included.
- **Chinese zone names from Wikidata division codes** (T-897, my call 2026-10-22): a zone shows its most populous county's English name without its kind; a zone led by a city district shows the prefecture city's name, once per prefecture (the oldest district), unless a metro row is that city.
- **A drive to a station counts double** (T-916, my call on Anita's ask, 2026-10-22): `ride_drive_f` 2, so 2.66 perceived minutes a minute against 1.33 for driving the whole way. Of the simple levers it is the one that cuts long drives to a station and keeps short ones; a larger start constant (25-40) did the reverse, and the drop-off and its cap on station parking changed nothing in suburbs.
