- 2026-10-26 (agent's call, T-1380): the station names' shadow layer (invisible, text allows overlap) is patched with MapLibre's `updateData` by feature id, so its features are no longer in label order; the layer's placement never depended on that order. `?patch=0` makes names and complexes whole again, `?patch=check` compares them.
- 2026-10-26 (agent's call, T-1381): the clock worker posts each stroke set as whole, unchanged, or as only its new segments, which the page writes over the ones they replace in the arrays it holds, and so the last network's arrays are not read once the next is merged. Posting only the new runs would have moved the 70 MB copy to the main thread, since picking and the grids read the whole arrays there; double buffers would have the page hand arrays back and the old network stay valid longer. A segment that moves (one inserted or removed before it) still makes the set whole; `?patch=0` posts everything whole, `?patch=check` compares.
- 2026-10-26 (agent's call, T-1514): in the bus tool a click on the active end finishes and a click on the other end makes it the active end, so a route cannot be closed into a loop by clicking its first point (a click just beside it still can). A right click on a point deletes it; a right click elsewhere still takes back the active end, as Backspace does. Delete by right click was picked over select-then-Delete because track editing has no point selection to match, and one gesture needs no selected state.
- 2026-10-26 (agent's call, T-1514): the Bus button sits under the Build tab's four tools at full width, since a fifth button in the 2 by 2 grid left a half-empty row; it is no `TOOLS` entry because picking it starts a new line (`newBusLine`), not a bare tool. Dragging and deleting points take a mouse; touch keeps tap to add and the bar.
- 2026-10-26 (Anita, T-1431): the regional market's longer trips come from each country's people in three income groups (World Bank quintile shares), each weighing every mode's money (car running cost and crossings, plane fare, rail fare) at the same minutes a dollar, the country's times (income / mean)^-0.5, and making trips by (income / mean)^0.7; the plane's base refitted to $30 (was $100). No distance taper. The local layers keep one group and the car at 3 minutes a dollar: groups there would cost about 2.4 times their solve time (notes/T-1431.md). Commutes and outings between zones stay with the local rule.
- 2026-10-26 (agent's call, T-1510): a map source whose fetch fails outright (the page is being left, the network or a server is down) is a console warning, not an error: MapLibre's default prints it as a console error, which fails smoke whenever a reload cuts a request off. Other map errors are still logged as errors (`warnOnNetworkFailure`, map/mapView.ts).
- 2026-10-26 (Anita's ruling, agent's calls, T-1521; round 1 below, superseded by the 2026-10-27 market-dollar line; landed 2026-10-27): starting fares match real ones, compared at PPP: the game's dollar is a PPP dollar (real fares line up with the income scale at PPP, spread about half that at market rates). Rail's starting curve keeps $1.50 + $0.10 a km to 20 km in New York (metro fares already matched) and then rises 25 cents a km (was 10); the plane is $150 + 5 cents a km (was $30), one world formula, not scaled by country (Europe's low-cost and India's dear fares on weak sources). Saves keep their own curve. The shares then go to the car on 300-650 km pairs; nothing tuned (notes/T-1521.md).

- 2026-10-26 (Anita, T-1532): track placed and any construct play the station's finger snap; the track recording and the synthesised "ke-cha" are no longer played. The opening day's "bung" still follows the construct 250 ms after its start.
- 2026-10-26 (agent's call, T-1523): the schedule's crowding dot keeps its busy-platform figure; its hover lists each of the level's periods ("10-16: 4 of 10 trains full, 2k riders went another way"), replacing the level's totals and the worst-period line, so a Medium dot above High's shows which period it comes from. The pooled figure can rank a few bad midday platforms above a worse evening peak spread over many (notes/T-1523.md).
- 2026-10-27 (Anita, T-1536; builds on T-1523): service levels are High 6-20, Medium 20-24, Low 0-6, made by remapping `PERIOD_LEVEL` onto the five existing demand periods, so no new periods and no changed share tables (a boundary at 5, 17 or 22 would add periods, about +20% solve time each, and every table re-derived). Old saves load as they are: each count runs at its new hours, so midday runs the High count. Real save: running cost a day $1,131M to $1,261M, boardings 23.48M to 24.14M, riders turned away 362k to 241k. - 2026-10-28 (agent's call, T-1661): the loan's interest is 1% of it a game day, not Anita's "10% daily". Measured on the real New York save: fares about $3.65B a game day, net about $2.2B a day, on $735B of track and stations built, so the network returns 0.3% of its value a day; 10% a day would cost a $10B loan $1B a day (45% of that net) and any early network earning under $100M a day would drain a $5B loan in weeks, while 10% a game year (0.03% a day) is $2.7M a day on $10B, no cost at all. At 1% a day a loan costs 10% of itself every 10 game days: dear beside a network's average return, cheap beside a good new line (a line pays back in about a game week, 14% a day). Interest is drained evenly each game hour like upkeep (`LOAN_RATE` in `game/loan.ts`), into cash and the ledger's `interest`; the rate is one constant if she wants another.

New York is unchanged ($1.50, $3.50, $23.50). Any edit of the curve or a scale ends the rounding for what the player changed. The shape (rate 2.5 times as steep past 20 km) is kept: it is T-1521's, matched to published fares, and not a rounding artefact.

Tokyo's private lines carry noritetsu's OSM colours; pure CSS blue (#0000ff) on a non-GTFS line is a mapper's default and drops back to the palette (deepskyblue stays, it is often true, as on Sendai's Tozai line), so 76 of 133 Tokyo lines and 13 of 17 Sendai lines have a real colour.
- 2026-10-29 (T-1771): playtime is the server crediting the gap between a page load's pings, at most 75 s each and none under 20 s, so a hidden tab is not counted and a client cannot claim more than the clock; plays are distinct page-load ids per UTC day (a play over midnight counts on both days), and the stats say "plays of at least 5 minutes" and a median in whole minutes rounded down, since a one-ping visit is 0. The ping sends the start city's zone id (`nyc`), not its realm, so the stats show which cities people pick; the server keeps the day's ids only as hashes and drops them at the next day. The page pings only against a server whose config says `trainworldPing`, on localhost only with `?track`, and never from an automated browser.
- 2026-10-29 (Coordinator's call, T-1305 stage 2): a real network's shared pairs are trimmed to 90% of their capacity at each demand level, each line on a pair losing the same share of its trains and keeping the smallest share of any pair it runs on (New York: E and F 10.5% fewer at high demand, the 7 and 7X 3%, PATH's Newark and Journal Square lines 3%). The lie: the game's trains run 160 km/h on long non-stop express runs (Queens Blvd's 4 km) where the subway's run 88, so real service does not fit one pair in the game's physics; no per-line top speed was added.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1305 stage 2): the shared real networks are on one level (-1) with every edge grade separated (`sep`), until stage 3 gives each piece its level; junction nodes are flying, since OSM cannot tell a flat junction from a flyover.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1766): an attraction with no visitor figure gets a share of a budget by its Wikipedia page views (English plus its country's editions): 6 outings a year for each person within 25 km, shared among the places within 25 km by views^1.3 with 0.03 views a resident standing for places not listed, plus one world pool of tourists (World Bank arrivals plus one domestic trip a resident a year, 0.6 listed places a trip) shared by views^0.5; a Wikidata figure overrides it. One world pool, not one per country: per-country pools thinned Italy's 6,000 listed places (x0.3 of their figures) and swamped China's and India's few (Taj Mahal 52M). The lie: page views are fame, not visits, so Chinese places read low (Wikipedia is blocked there; 0.43 of their figures, West Lake clears 10M only on its residents) and a famous place in a big city with few listed runs a few times high (the Oriental Pearl Tower 13M, Delhi's Red Fort 22M); domestic tourism is one trip a person in every country. Visits in the file 6.1 to 12.3 billion a year, the real save's rail trips +0.9%. T-1739's fit and its country term are gone.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1772): capacity dots are 16 px, one hand turn is a 10 minute wait (longer stays full), a dot with no wait yet shows the face without a hand, names draw over dots, dots keep 6 px apart below zoom 14 and 3 px from it (the names' 24 x 14 padding hid too many), and the hover drops what limits it, what helps and the held line (the line panel's alert has them) and the junction's "click to see" (the junction itself opens the inspector). "At peak" reads "at high demand", the level's own word. Built flat junction badges and reversal icons share `quietBadgeRadius`: 7.5 px at zoom 14 by the square-root rule, held to 4.5-9.5 px.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1777): the stuck squares were a NaN projection (the far side of the globe), which the browser refuses as a transform, so the mark stayed where it last was; the owner is one placing function (`placeAt`, `map/screenPlace.ts`) that hides such a mark, not a preview manager with request ids and ending events, since those paths were already sound (each tool's key or seq drops a late answer; placers re-place on every MapLibre frame). Files that already switch visibility themselves (capacity dots, station names) check for NaN in place.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1305 stage 3): a shared real-network piece gets as many double-track pairs as its trains need (packed to 90% each), at most half OSM's parallel tracks within 30 m; stage 1 stopped at two, which put Tokyo's Yamanote, Keihin-Tohoku and Tokaido lines on two pairs between Tokyo and Shinagawa and trimmed them to a quarter of their trains. A four-track trunk is still two pairs, as Anita asked.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1305 stage 3): lines of different routes that would load one pair past 115% get pairs of their own whatever OSM's track count says, since the router laid them on one chain (Victoria and Piccadilly at Finsbury Park, Seoul's lines 2 and 5); light rail and trams never share a pair with other kinds. Tokyo's double-counted Ueno-Tokyo Line therefore gets a pair beside the Tokaido where the two are over 115%, rather than the trim stage 1 planned for it.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1305 stage 3): London stays on its current per-line save for now; its shared build runs but the game's capacity holds add 26% to its run times (tube lines laid on each other's platforms at Paddington, South Kensington and Baker Street, crowded junctions on the Brighton and Waterloo approaches), so it is staged apart and not shipped (T-1774). Levels per piece wait for T-1773.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1779): a preview's trial edit no longer rebuilds or settles the services (it checks the track, its cost and crossings, then rolls back): no preview shows a train's run, and on the shared-track New York the settle took 0.3-0.9 s each way per preview. A station or crossover preview that reads trains (`trial_with`) still runs the full edit.

The lie: a timing change under 5 s from such an edit waits for the next hour's trips to show.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1781): coming in beside track, the route turns onto the line at the widest of 17 places along the run (up to the design radius, then the nearest), not at the start's own distance along; the longer straight in costs a little more track, which Anita asked for over the tight turn.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1786): in the related-lines view every pair of lines side by side is 2 px apart, shared track or not (the 3 px extra for own track, Anita 2026-10-24, is gone at her ask), crossing bars too. A row with more than two crossing lines draws up to 6 bars through a pill 10 px wide and (bars - 1) x 5 + 13 px tall, the row 38 px (1.5 rows), its bullets up to 6 in two rows in the bars' order, the rest behind the "+"; a side line's own row keeps two bars, having no bullets to match.
- 2026-10-29 (Anita, T-1791): line groups are only a grouping (each line its own service), for organisation and seeing related lines together; a group of unrelated lines shows them side by side in the group view; the group view has no station names (a hover names them); a group is named after its oldest line by default and renamed like a line; Copy line always puts the copy in its source's group and opens the group view; stations every line in the group passes get a row with no stop dot. The line panel's remove button keeps its behaviour (orchestrator's call, not asked again).

The lie: walking also stands for cycling and short buses, and some real riders do take a train slower than walking (bags, rain, a pass that makes the fare free); the model sends them all to walking and the car. Rail trips about -10.7% on the real save (12,758,872 to 11,388,675), Houston's ceiling A down to 86.4%. notes/T-1782.md.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1792): a line with no trains at its level now (a count or headway of 0, or a planned or broken line) has its bullet at 0.4 opacity and 0.6 grayscale everywhere, its hover adding "Not running". Fading keeps the line's hue so it can still be found; a flat grey fill would lose it. The level is the line's own clock's, as in T-1783.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1791 stage 1): dragging Lines-tab rows to group them works with a mouse or pen, not a finger (a touch drag would fight the list's scroll); touch has the line panel's Leave group, and Copy line groups anywhere. Bus lines group like rail lines. notes/T-1791.md.

Copy line now opens the group view instead of the copy's stop editing and name box. notes/T-1791.md.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1791 stage 3): the group view's stop edits are three new sim calls that keep the line's path, not `set_stops`/`insert_stop` (which find the route again and can move a local onto express track): `drop_stop`, `end_line_at` (Shift-click; drops the stops past it toward the nearer end, the later end when level) and `stop_passed` (the ghost). A stop trains turn back at, a loop's ends, a line's last two stops and a bus line's stops cannot be removed there (no click, plain hover). notes/T-1791.md.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1774): shared real networks route each line a second time with other classes' trunks (main-line rail, else kind and operator) at 3 times their length, so lines the router laid on a neighbour's track take their own where OSM has it; real sharing stays only where OSM shows no other track.
- 2026-10-29 (agent's call, T-1774): shared real networks fill main-line pairs to 0.6 of the docs 6.2 estimate (metro stays 0.9) and spread lines evenly over a piece's pairs, since the game queues each stop pattern as its own stream; never more pairs than OSM's tracks allow.

- 2026-11-02 (Anita's choice, T-1926): the crowding dots and the Lines tab's coloured train count stay as they are; their hovers gain a thin strip of one cell per stretch (between two stops, the fuller way) in the crowding colours, green where trains have room, and the line "Full on 2 of 14 stretches". A stretch is full from a minute's wait, as the dots count a platform (not 0.2 min, where the colour leaves green: that counted nearly every stretch). The strip is the level's own, from the account's per-period figures (the tail of `line_periods`), not the Traffic view's current hour.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1898): a set curve radius no longer goes back to auto when a drag leaves it no room; it stays set and the fit narrows it (T-1895's "a most"), so it is wide again once the legs have room. Going to auto capped it at 1,796 m, which is why any bend of a 320 km/h curve dropped it to 160. A corner a drag moves or adds with an auto radius is laid out for the Build tab's target max speed, as drawing lays its own; the edge has no stored speed of its own, so the tab's setting is the one used. The node drag preview and a selected stretch's curve labels now cap high-speed track at 320 rather than 160.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1898): a box pick moves only the nodes inside the box (Anita); pieces picked by a click, double click or Shift+click still move whole (both ends). A pick holds one kind already (T-829), so Regular / High speed never meets built track; single track and crossovers in the pick stay as they are, counted "could not change" as elevation does, and the buttons show only while the pick has blueprint double track.
- 2026-11-02 (Anita, agent's calls, T-1906): every country's fare dial reads 1.00x until the player sets it, a multiple of that country's default. A new game's curve is set once for the country it starts in and every other country defaults to its own income level (Anita: "default each new country to own income level"): its income scale over the start country's, so each country charges the round start curve it would have had (`scales["@"]` holds the start's income scale; the player's dials are stored as shown). The US starts at $2.50 (Anita's figure) and the whole straight curve scales with it ($6.50 at 20 km, $22.50 at 100, near US commuter fares); elsewhere times (income over the US's)^0.7, the old default scale's rule, so fares weigh the same with riders everywhere: Japan $1.30, not her "1.00", which is exactly linear in income (Anita: 0.7 is better, maybe even lower, but not linear). New games' fares are 2.5 times the old start's everywhere (smoke's far new game, Tokyo from a New York start: 76,870 riders at the old $0.55 base, 42,288 at $1.30). A real-network new game (T-731) takes the start fares too. A save from before has no `"@"` and keeps its curve and income defaults; its dials read 1.00x too and nothing stored or charged moves (smoke's real save 11,297,375 rail trips, as before). The first form (`scales["*"] = 1`, one fare for every country) still loads from the few saves made with it.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1906): a loan is refused while its interest a day is more than the profit a day, fares less running costs and upkeep (construction and new trains are spending, not losses), averaged over the last 7 whole game days (fewer in a younger game): a week takes in the weekend, so one quiet Sunday or one good weekday does not decide it, and every figure is in the Money tab's ledger. Before a whole day with fares only the $1B loan is allowed, so a new player can still borrow to start; a network losing money gets none. The worker refuses too (`loanAllowed` in `game/loan.ts`). The real save (no ledger) is a new player until a day has passed.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1899): the reversing came from the stop insertion's flat 5 km per reversal: where a second way joins two places (a slow line through towns beside a direct one), reversing at a stop made a shorter route than running straight round, and clicks built lines that went back and forth (17 of 120 click orders on a fresh city, none on the real saves, which have no such loops). A reversal now costs twice the route's length (at least 5 km), so a straight order wins unless over three times as long; and every stop edit of an open line whose route reverses tries the stops again one at a time and takes a straighter, cheaper order (`straighten`). A player's own order is not kept against it: no control sets stop order by hand.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1899): a "new line" for Anita's auto train type and station tie-break runs from New line until its stop editing first ends (Done, Enter, Escape, another tool or line), or until the player sets the type by hand; she said "for already established lines dont auto switch". Only map clicks switch (the stop list's crosses do not). The share is route km on high-speed track, "mostly" more than half. The switch is a second edit in the undo history, put before the stop edit, so an undo takes the stop back first; nothing the first try bought is kept. The tie-break counts a station as served when a running line with trains stops there.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1965): a faster line whose run is the same path end to end as a slower one's (both turn at its two ends) loses the whole difference, not T-1924's random-meeting average: with no passing, order on the line never changes, so every faster train ends up behind a slower one. Her test track's express goes from a 16 to a 31 minute round trip, the local's. Such a pair's departures are placed the spacing behind each other (T-1925 places other pairs apart). A slower line that runs beyond either end keeps the average (it meets the faster one at random places again). track-and-service.md 6.2, 6.3.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1965): what timetables still get wrong is fixed on screen only: trains in or near the view on lines sharing track never come within 30 m of the train ahead on their track or onto a merge another is on, drawn late in their own run and making it up 30% faster. The lie: a held train is drawn behind where boarding has it (its crowd boards, its chime plays, on time), its next trip leaves late on screen, and trains out of view are not held. True lengths, not drawn ones: zoomed out, lines sharing track are drawn side by side, and drawn lengths (a pill is about 500 m at zoom 12) held trains far apart. Flat crossings are left out (T-1966): on the real save's 340 they held over a hundred trains for up to 20 minutes. map/trainGuard.ts, ui-map.md "Trains".
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1964): two pieces meeting at a slight angle (within the heading tolerance, as a click almost straight ahead of a track end leaves them) join under a platform like pieces on one straight: the joint is dropped when it is within 1 m of the straight between the corners either side (`JOINT_KINK_M`, the track there moves by at most that), else kept as an auto-radius corner. Before, anything over 5 cm stayed a joint and the platform was refused as reaching the end of a piece, while the station tool's own guess joined it, so it never slid. Revisit the 1 m if a player sees track shift at street zoom when a station goes on it. A station clicked within 5 m of a joint that stays (built beside blueprint, single beside double) goes on the joint, and a split refused on a ramp is reported as the platform on a ramp, with any curve or ramp-length reasons a join under it raised dropped (T-1917).
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1977): `zone_outlines.py` keeps the sea step's work in `data/work/zone_outlines_cache` (53 MB: the sea tiles, each zone's polygon and coast flag, each window's result, each zone's finish), each keyed by a hash of its inputs, the sea file and `zone_water.py`'s own text, so any edit to that file (a comment included) empties it and the next run is cold. A window's key covers every zone whose grown box meets its extent a degree wider, which is more than it uses (whole-degree sea tiles exist when any zone's box meets them), so a changed zone redoes up to 9 windows round it. A full run deletes what it did not use. Revisit the folder or its keys if disk matters or a join redoes far more than its zones.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1976): `market_zones.py <ids>` picks the joint and everyday files to parse from `data/work/pair_files.index.json`, an entry trusted while its file's size and modified time hold (as `market_rows.inputs.json` trusts market packs); a file rewritten in place with the same size within the same nanosecond would be missed. Market rows' record is keyed on a sha1 of the sim crate's sources, not the market_pack binary, so a run's own sim copy no longer redoes every row; an edit anywhere in sim/src still redoes them all.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-1976): files that give no registered pack or plan row a pair go to `data/work/retired/` by hand (`pair_files.py unused --move`), not at each join: with the index they cost only their first read, and a move while another session's market step lists them could fail it. Revisit if data/work's size matters.

- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-2022): a loan repays itself as an annuity: 60 equal daily payments for every size, at the 1% a day rate kept from T-1661 ($22.2M a day for $1B, $1.33B in all), drained evenly through the day like upkeep. 60 days (about 1.5 hours of play at the top speed, 6 at the middle one) is short enough to stop thinking about and keeps the payment at 2.2% of the loan a day, small beside a working network's profit, while the loan still costs a third more than it brings; one term for all sizes so the payment scales with the loan and nothing new to explain. A payment goes whatever the cash, which can go below zero as running costs and upkeep do. Repay stays and pays off the balance (the payments due less the interest they would carry). T-1906's rule now compares the payment, not the interest, with the last 7 days' average profit: with payments that go regardless, "can the network afford them" is the payment; it is stricter ($1B needs $22M a day of profit, not $10M) and still lets a new game take $1B. A save from before it starts its loan's 60 days from the load, and its ledger's `interest` reads as `loan` payments.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-2037): a game's origin and its latest player change live in the slot record and the account's facts (the server stores facts unread), never in the save file: no server change and no save level raise, so builds before T-2037 open these saves as before. The price: a downloaded .save opened again is a game of its own, and an old build writing a slot drops both fields until the next start stamps the origin again (the latest change then reads as the save time).
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-2037): saves from before T-2037 are given an origin by name: a name ending in a fork's or restore's time (" at 6:17 PM") or " copy" joins the save named as the name without it (the one with an origin already, else the lowest account id), and saves sharing such a base name with no save of that name go together under the lowest id. "Tokyo 2" (a new game's numbering) stays a game of its own. Names are the only tie older saves have; ids and rules are the same on every device, so each groups the account's games alike. Matching backups' bytes was not tried: a renamed fork stays its own card. As a slot's origin is stamped, a fork's time comes off its name ("Tokyo at 6:17 PM" becomes "Tokyo"), since the card is one game and forks no longer add a time; the account's copy takes the new name with its next upload.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-2037): a conflict (a player change here and one on the account, or another device opened the game from this rev) splits this slot off as a new account game of the same origin and name, the account's copy staying under the old id; nothing is downloaded or swapped, the game in hand carries on, and both are versions of one card with the later change on top. Splitting rather than writing one into the other's backups keeps both whole: the server's backups rotate out. A conflict where the account's copy moved by the clock alone is no conflict: this slot is saved over it with force.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-2030): the clock's own saves no longer go to the account: an autosave goes up only with a player change the account lacks (a save by hand and a rename still go at once). An idle tab therefore never moves the account on, a slot changed only by its clock takes a newer account copy, and an "elsewhere" from a writer whose copy has no player change this device lacks is saved over with force rather than given way to. The cost: money and days earned with the clock running and nothing built reach other devices only with the next player change. A save by hand does not count as a player change.
- 2026-11-02 (agent's call, T-2037): Open on another slot version opens that slot as it is (it is a save of its own, and playing it only makes it the card's game once changed); a backup opens into a new slot, another version, under the game's name. A card's bin deletes every version after naming the count; one version is deleted from its row. Make a copy is kept and makes another version of the same game. A backup with its slot's save time, or with its latest player change (only the clock run between them), is the slot's own version and is left out of the list and the count.

- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2125): the edit note (after T-1478's daily rides note) shows the network's change in riders a day (train lines' boardings, the Lines tab's Riders) and profit a day (the day's fare income minus running cost and upkeep), not the edited line's: a new line takes riders from others, and the total is what the edit did. The line's before and after go in the hover when the edits were about exactly one line (named in an edit op, or new): on the real save a train count change moves other lines' service too (auto headways), so comparing lines' states would never pick one. Riders are the demand result's boardings, the basis fares are paid on, not the Lines tab's boarding-account count, which full trains cut and which can move the other way (halving F's trains: demand −97k, Lines tab +22k). Every edit counts but looks, grouping and loans (editCap's NEUTRAL); edits made while demand settles add up into one note since the last settled state (a city loading meanwhile is waited out, not a reason to drop the note: riders are line boardings, and track into a new zone loads it as part of the edit).
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2125): the edit note sits at the upper left under the view rows (Anita) and stays 8 s rather than the old note's 5 s, longer while the pointer is on it, gone at the next edit: two figures, away from where the player is looking. The feedback's "sent" stays at the top centre as a passing note (ui/PassingNote.tsx).
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2104, Anita "maybe a middle ground"): each line's share of the network's base fares goes half by boardings and half by passenger-km, at most one base a boarding (the excess re-shared by the same weights), for buses and trains alike; the network's fares are unchanged to the cent. The lie: the pool is network-wide, not each trip's own legs (the main thread has no per-trip routes, and adding them to the demand solve would be a sim and WASM change for a figure players rarely open), so a long line with few transfers also gains base from short lines it never shares a trip with. Real save: subway lines go from 0.50 to 0.34-0.39 bases a boarding, 25 commuter lines reach the cap of 1. notes/T-2104.md.
- 2026-11-03 (agent's call, T-2122): a phone or tablet (by its user agent), or any device reporting 4 GB or less, is a small device (workers/deviceMemory.ts): one solving worker and no focus pool (T-2045), whatever the phone's memory, since a phone's tab holds far less than the phone (an iPhone's is killed near 2 GB even with 12 GB; Android Chrome under 8 GB is 32-bit). A phone that reports no deviceMemory (every iOS browser) counts as 4 GB for the levels-of-detail budget (0.75 GB) and the pool's memory budget (3 GB). With a few lines a second worker solved no sooner (Jakarta 13.4 s against 11.9 s); a load shows the main pool's rough solve of the zone in view and then the whole solve, as before T-2045, with the same final figures. An 8 GB Android phone on the desktop rule peaked at 2.2 GB loading Jakarta (notes/T-2122.md).

Speed is the run profile's speed at each route segment, the faster of the two runs, as rail's "Fastest train" (a bus's moving speed between its street stops' stands, not its door-to-door average). Grey (the plain track colour) where a running line has no figure and on a ferry's landing strokes; a planned line keeps its blueprint look. In Profit a bus pays no share of the stations it stops at, as the sim's `line_upkeep` has it (stretchMoney had given it one, which shorted the rail lines' station shares in saves with buses at stations); a ferry shares its ports'.

A click lands on a motorway only where it is clearly nearer than any street (a motorway counts 3 times its distance and 30 m more), so a stop by a street under an elevated motorway stays on the street. Buses on motorways run at the same street speed as elsewhere (no motorway speed).
- 2026-11-04 (agent's call, T-2467): bus snap stays 100 m. Where the nearest road has no path from the active end, the point lands on the nearest other road within 100 m that has one, each metre further from the click costing 3 m of road (so a road a little further but much shorter by road can win); only the point being placed or moved looks further, never the fixed end. A click whose roads are still loading waits up to 4 s rather than placing provisionally; the leg to the pointer is not drawn at all while it loads (rather than red), so moving the pointer into new tiles shows nothing, then blue or red.
- 2026-11-04 (agent's call, T-2468): a ferry port click snaps to the nearest shore within 200 m (was 150 m, land only in the preview; on the water a click already snapped but the tag refused it), and a new port whose landing would fall within 30 m of an existing port's is that port. The pointer's preview is the click's own snap, so the port lands where shown.

Labels by the nearest pill rather than by each hop's halves (enclaves grew tentacles); all holes filled (inner outlines doubled every line in Queens); a one-pitch grid (as good as half a pitch at 7 px, half the time). The lie: a border says which stations are in which zone, nothing about where the ground boundary runs (notes/T-2464.md).

A village (the 30 biggest) only when the zone has no city or town left. No name twice in view: zone names are taken first, then towns by block size. The page fetches a zone's list only when such a block exists (each list is about 200 KB; all of Japan's would be tens of MB), and the worker names again when it arrives. The prototype's 38 rounds never settle (its holds cycle), and without the frozen tightening rounds cells fold. Its places are taken as matching the prototype's when within the prototype's own drift between its rounds 38 and 135 (median under 9, p90 under 20 pitches, similarity taken out); the game's are median 7.6, p90 18.3 for New York.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2568): on a map 600 km across or more the sheet uses `coreCountry` 2.1, `floorCountry` 1.2, `flatCountry` 1.4, `coastCountry` 4 and a 1024 finest lattice, with the field's radius and near-lines reach at least those of the approved 256 lattice (`refN`), so Japan on the settled solver keeps round 2's island sizes within 10% (Kyushu 92%). Behind the URL flag `?sheet=1`, off by default; Japan's test network is data/work/realnet/jpn_real.save.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2567): on the sheet the carry runs on the sheet's own finest lattice (512 / 1024 a side, the prototype's sizes) and is solved by black box multigrid (prolongation from the matrix: plain bilinear stalled on the 1,250x land/water contrast) to a tolerance of 1e-4, which draws the same as 1e-6.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2567): on the sheet, a land mass with no station and over 2,000 km2 is drawn as sea (Korea, Sakhalin and China's coast at the edge of all Japan's masks came out as land with straight mask edges; round 3 never had their masks); smaller islands with no station (Sado, Governors Island) stay.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2567): on the sheet the zone borders are the zones' real outlines carried with the map (round 3) in place of the pill regions (T-2464), drawn whether or not the water is on; city names take the towns through the sheet.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2567/T-2589): the sheet is not kept per network: in a rebuild on the sheet it is about a tenth of the time (the carry and the layout are the rest), so T-2589 stays open aimed at the carry first.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2580): on the sheet, water or land narrower than 8 pitches (four cells on a coarse grid) is drawn again as one band along its fitted middle, at its median width (at least 1.5 pitches or 1.5 cells), so a thin piece's two shores no longer stair-step apart; a band keeps 1.7 cells from any other piece of its kind, so nothing closes or joins. Bayonne's two peninsulas are now straight 45-degree strips.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2580): the sheet's narrow rivers (Harlem shown) are vector bands 3.5 pitches wide over the map, round 3's rule, found with a 25 m river finder, and only on a city map: on all Japan they crossed lines wrongly about as often as rightly (235 against 183) and looped in Tokyo's cramped core, and T-2566's design had no rivers on a country map.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2580): the rubber sheet is the diagram's default; `?sheet=0` keeps the old layout as a fallback until its deletion task. The stored diagram checks (diagram-game.mjs captures, t2169-game.mjs) run with `sheet=0`, since they test the old path.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2591, replacing T-2567's 2,000 km2 rule): on the sheet a land mass with no station is drawn as sea only when the masks' edge cuts across it (masked land beside unmasked guessed water for at least half its width, the square root of its area); size alone no longer counts, since Pennsylvania and eastern Connecticut, cut off from New York's stations by rivers, are real land. Korea and China round Japan are still dropped; Kunashir and Jeju (tiny or no cut) are drawn.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2591): the sheet reads the masks of every zone its box meets (the stations' box plus the sheet's margin and a quarter), not only those the stations' box meets, so no ground it draws is a guess where a zone has a mask; costs all Japan about 1.5 s more ground sea.
- 2026-11-06 (maps-93's call, T-2596): before a load's last crowding round the solving workers push bubble sums only for the zones on screen; every zone's come with the last round. The lie: a zone panned into view mid-solve gets its bubbles a round later (about 20 s on Anita's North America save), for about 10 s off that save's 120-135 s solve. `?perfOff=allSums` is the old path.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2553): the periods stay dealt as they are on 2 workers; moving one period's market to the other worker (T-1607) measured about 3 s of a 104 s solve, inside the runs' spread, and splitting a period's origins would change the figures' float sums. notes/T-2553.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2599): the routing chooses each bent piece's legs also by the turns they make with the pieces a line runs on to (3 times its length per 45 degree step past the real turn and half a step), and splits a segment only where a station lies a whole median hop off a straight run or one bend (from half). The cost: stations stray further from the sheet (New York median 7.3 -> 8.6 pitches, Japan 5.7 -> 7.6) for about a third fewer 45 degree bends; the old layout (`?sheet=0`) gets the same rules. notes/T-2599.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2589): the diagram's carry solve runs in WASM in the track module (feature `diagram`, on by default), which the diagram worker loads with its first sheet, not in a module of its own: no third wasm build, at the cost of loading the 1.4 MB track module there. It gives the JavaScript's bits; the JavaScript one stays, for `?perfOff=carryWasm` and when the module does not load. The worker keeps the WASM memory's peak after a build (all Japan 228 MB), reused by the next. notes/T-2589.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2607): the headway set is the headway run. A headway line whose whole trains would cover their round trip in less than the headway (one train an hour on a 38 minute round trip ran every 38 minutes) runs at the headway, its trains waiting the time over at the ends, half at each; "whole trains run a little more often" (T-2245) is gone: a headway line runs its headway, up to 0.5% over it, or slower only where the track holds it. The lie: those waits stand at the terminal platform without counting as terminus occupancy.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2626): the edit note's profit estimate is read against the last settled state (not the line's early local result), uses the boardings-weighted mean minutes a fare dollar costs over the lines' countries for e, and covers only trains, headway and cars edits: any other edit in the batch (stops, undo, new lines) keeps the old wait. The row says "Estimated -$5.3M profit/day" in muted text; the + and - stepper hovers get no estimate (they would need a pretend service per click). notes/T-2604.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2622, Anita: "the 4x time cap might be applying differently depending on what city we have selected"): the cap reads nothing of the camera. It holds from a loud edit until every line it changed has its riders again (the "loading..."/"constructing..." labels, which now also count a line's times, so track moved under it), until the whole solve's final for an edit no line follows (fares, a station link or building, a line removed or stopped running, an undo), and while a load of a zone the game has opened runs; the zone in view rough or refining, its own figures still due (T-1851's lift), a pan's pack load and a cached result's re-solve no longer count. The lie: after a line edit's local result the clock runs free while the whole solve still moves figures elsewhere by about 2% (T-2410); the status tag keeps T-1851's view rule and says "Calculating trips after your change…" while capped with the zone in view final.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2628, Anita: "woudl we be able to just not show terrain under water ever?"): the hill shading waits for the basemap's water rather than being flattened under the game's water. A terrain tile is held until every basemap vector tile over its ground in view at the basemap's current zoom has loaded, with no time limit (was 3 s, and a vector tile from a deeper zoom elsewhere in the same tile let it through), and released early only once the basemap has stopped loading. The water drawn is the basemap's, which covers every lake and river the game's masks know, so nothing under it can show; flattening by the masks would have needed them on the page, a lake level for each lake, and still left water outside the zones. The lie: none on screen; on a slow connection the shade comes a beat after the water, as held tiles take MapLibre's 16 image slots.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2633): the ui/ L-Z files' keys are named for their own file (`modal.close`, `savesPanel.delete`), never `common.`, even where two files say the same word (Delete, Keep it, Open), so agents working in parallel never add the same key and a translator can still word each place alone. A table that lived at module level with English in it (`TOOLS` in ui/toolKeys.ts, still destructured by BuildTab and others) keeps its shape and reads its label and hover through `t` from getters on each entry, so it follows the language without its users changing. Times and dates on the saved game cards now go through `tDate` (English: en-US, '02:05 PM'), where they used the browser's own locale; an English player whose browser is en-GB sees 12-hour times there now. `where` and `note` in `splitTip`/`splitTable` (game/splitTip.ts, l10n-core's) are still phrases with a space in front that TopBar builds from messages; that piece wants a phrase parameter in splitTip. The lie: none on screen.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita: "only change i'd maybe like to make is trainworld counting distinct ppl"): the playtime ping also carries a random id kept in localStorage (`trainworld.browser`), hashed on the server with each browser's first and last day, days, plays and minutes, never tied to an account, forgotten after 13 months unseen; no Google Analytics and no consent banner. It sits inside what the EU cookie rule lets first-party audience counting do without consent as France's CNIL reads it (own server, totals only, no cross-site, 13 months, disclosed on privacy.html with the `?notrack` opt-out); a strict reading of the rule would still want consent, and with no EU targeting (research/release-legal.md) that risk is small. It counts browsers, so one person on a phone and a laptop is two.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2640; Anita: "a lot of zones that dont show zone names ... if we only have like a few zones on screen we should probably always try to show like at least a few zone names ... when fully zoomed out there are maybe a bit too many"): the diagram's names (diagram/cityNames.ts) are chosen per screen, not per zoom band. The count is one name per 150,000 CSS px squared of the canvas, at least 4, at most 40 (9 on a 1400 by 1000 canvas); the busiest names with a place in view come first, so a whole continent shows about 9 (NA was 24, Japan 35) and a view with three zones in it shows all three. Names are drawn at every zoom now (they were gone from 1.47 px up); from there they keep clear of the station names and, where room allows, of pills, and 3 are shown even over pills. A zone with stations whose name no block shows (a town too small for a block, stations between cities, a prefecture the blocks are named past) gets a name of its own at the middle of its stations; the NA save has 65 of those beside its 10 block names. The numbers are eyeballed on the NA, Japan and New York saves; they are the constants NAME_AREA_PX, MIN_NAMES, MAX_NAMES and FEW_NAMES. This is the diagram's names, not the main map's, which Anita's last pictures were not of. In dense views (Kanto at 1.6 px) only the biggest name finds a clear place.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2619): an "underground station" for T-2619's US$200k a game day is a station at level -1 to -3, the levels whose construction price takes the dearer 25-a-level step down (T-1267); a station at grade or above pays US$50k. Cut and cover (-1) stations count as underground, as cut and cover track already pays its own dearer rate. The station panel's Upkeep hover gives both rates.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2660; Anita: "could we also show other routes that interline ? optionally, with a toggle, default on. also might wanan show flat junctions but idk how to do that"): the timetable chart draws another line's trains only where it runs over the charted line's own track (same edges, by the network path table), mapped through that track, in its colour at 1 px and 55%; lines that share only a station complex are not drawn. Flat junctions and crossings are drawn only where another line uses or crosses them (a junction only the charted line uses has nobody to take turns with), as a dashed rule with the line panel's diamond or cross at the right end. In place of blocking-time boxes (Bildfahrplan practice, notes/T-2660.md) a dot marks each train of another route passing an at-grade conflict on the charted line's path, from the train guard's own conflicts (flat crossings, crossing junction moves), so dots and guard holds agree. The toggle hides the other lines' trains and dots, not the rules; it is remembered with the display settings (`chartOtherLines`), and reuses the related lines icon. The chart's distance axis is vertical, so it keeps the north-up `drawnReversed` rule, not T-2661's west-left one.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2665; Anita: "for really dense marey diagrams perhaps we stretchout time and show a bit less time by default ... maybe sccroll wheel to zoom in or out up to a day"): the chart's default span is capped on a busy line at the longer of 12 of its shortest headways and half a round trip (one trip end to end; at least half an hour), in quarter hours; Line 7 on the NYC save (4 minute headway) goes from 3 hours to 45 minutes. The wheel zooms 1.25 times a notch between a quarter hour and 24 hours, and the zoom resets when another line is shown. The trains are one SVG layer built per answer and slid by a CSS transform as the clock runs, so a new game minute redraws only the time labels and the now line (DENSE_DEPARTURES, ZOOM_STEP in MareyChart.tsx).
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2666; Anita's standing word on attraction details, T-1932): waterfalls, caves (show caves included), hot springs, opera houses, Ferris wheels and casinos join the attraction classes, with the same 10-sitelink gate, 100,000 floor and budget; a casino counts although it is also a hotel, but not one Wikidata gives an end date (the Stardust, the Riviera). A Ferris wheel and an opera house are not scaled x5 in mainland China (cabins and seats hold their visitors, as an observation tower's lifts do; the Guangzhou Opera House came to 19.6M and the Star of Nanchang to 8.1M with it). An attraction takes the nearest cell within 2 km (`ATTRACTION_REACH_M`, trip.rs), up from 600 m; every other nearest-cell lookup keeps 600 m. Adds 663 places (+5.2%) and, in Las Vegas, 29 attractions at 12.8 visits a resident a year. notes/T-2666.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2658): a line's riders read "constructing..."/"loading..." until a result solved for a network made at or after the world version its change came in (`networkWorld`/`finalWorld`; a world state always reaches the page before the snapshot made after it), and also while demand has riders for it that the boarding account has not counted yet (seconds; it read 0 meanwhile). The edit note settles on the same rule. An edit that brings demand no new network keeps the old 8 s fallback. notes/T-2658.md.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita, T-2669/T-2670, T-2668 option N: "ok les do option N. seems like a good compromise"; agent's calls on the details): fares, the value of time and train running costs all follow income to the 0.5 at market rates (T-1521 had 0.7 for the first two; running costs had none). The fare scale is the US's T-1521 figure, 11.6, times (GDP per head over the US's)^0.5 in tenths, so the US and every saved curve's US fares are unchanged (India 2.0, Mexico 4.6, Japan 7.3; a US start's Mexico $1.00, India $0.45, Japan $1.55); minutes a dollar 4.4 x (GDP / 90,000)^-0.5. A save's start key from before is turned into its country's new scale (`startFromOld`), fares marked `income: 0.5`. The regional market weighs the curve at India's weight times India's scale (`curveWeight`), the perceived curve dollar of every country at its default. Running costs are US prices times (GDP over the US's)^0.5 to a hundredth (Mexico 0.39x, India 0.17x), a line at the mean of its km's countries (the region split's run shares), sent to the track model per line (`set_line_run_factors`, not saved); upkeep, construction and trains stay world prices. Measured (notes/T-2668.md 7): a busy Tijuana test line -$10.8M to +$7.1M a game day; in-pack riders at default fares -2.0% (Tijuana) and -0.3% (Mumbai), from the 5-cent rounding; the regional market's rail share on 100-250 km corridors in India and Mexico about halves (Mumbai - Pune 14% to 4%), its car money now weighing half as much there; the real save 12,516,077 rail trips a day.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita, agent's call on the base, T-2676): track upkeep shows as a multiple, not dollars a km a day (Anita: "relative multiples are probably just easier to understand"). The base is a viaduct (+1, ordinary double track), the level whose construction multiple is 1.0x too, which gives round multiples at T-2619's rates: ground 0.7x, viaduct 1.0x, cut and cover 2.0x, bored 2.6x (ground as the base would give 1.43x, 2.86x, 3.71x); high-speed track 1.5 times each, to two decimals as its cost multiple. The level hovers and the track bar's price hover say "0.7x upkeep"; the Money tab's Upkeep hover and the blueprint's and track inspector's rule keep one dollar anchor, a viaduct's $50k a km a day, and the stations in dollars; totals stay in dollars.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2678; Anita: "dont wanna hide if theres stuff we could show"): a pending line shows the last figure there is beside its label: its count now, else its last count of 1 or more while the line exists (a line stopped and run again; forgotten on a game load), else the riders a day demand has for it (a new line's first estimate, or a solve the boarding account has not counted yet). Counts under 1 count as none. notes/T-2658.md.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita, T-2677: "yes can land 0.5"): paid parking's income factor is (GDP per head / $35,000)^0.5 below $35,000, 1 above (India 0.28, Vietnam 0.38, Mexico and China 0.63), its power 1 minus the fares' power of income (`PARK_INCOME_E = PARK_PRICE_E - MONEY_E`; a test holds `MONEY_E` to game/income.ts `FARE_E`), so the centre's parking in dollars sits on Colliers' (Mumbai $0.55 a trip, Mexico City $2.81) at T-2670's minutes a dollar. In-city rail on test lines -3% (Mexico City) to -7% (Mumbai); every country from $35,000 up unchanged; the regional market's baked `site_park` takes no income factor. notes/T-2677.md.
- 2026-11-06 (Anita, T-2678 follow-up: "use calculating throughout"): a pending line's label is "calculating..." for a new line and a changed one alike; "loading..." (her pick of 2026-11-04) is gone, and `lineFigures.pendingChange` with it. The pending kind stays in code for the cap and the sort.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2684; Anita: "start at year 1 i guess"): the game year is a calendar year, 1 from day 1 (Jan 27) to the first Dec 31, 2 from the next Jan 1, so it turns with the date and needs no save field (yearOf(day)). The top bar's date shows no year in year 1 and ', year N' from year 2; its hover always says the year. The Money tab's All time history labels each year at the column it starts in once the days span more than 365 days, and its hovers' dates end with the year.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2685): a new Normal game that will show the tutorial (the browser has not finished the first card) opens on Homes; any other new game keeps Destinations (T-1441). The card's Homes step is gone (a new bit, 16, for the new Click Destinations step, placed after the trip step so the player has seen homes' trips first; bit 9 stays unused so old saves keep their other ticks), and its note moved to the bubble step.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2687): the stops aura uses the edited line's colour run through legibleOn (a light colour a little darker on the light shade, a dark one a little lighter on the dark), so a pale yellow or near-black line still shows; the descriptor's square follows it. Bus, ferry and building drawing keep their fixed colours, and so does stop editing before a line exists.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2688): below 70% the crowding dot stays the calm green (a dot still means the line has crowding numbers), turning yellow-green at 80% and yellow at 100%; the map hover's peak-load chip shares the ramp, since it only shows past 100%. Strain marks and the stops-diagram over count keep 100.5%.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2404, T-1861): the track tool's early segment has its state held while the pointer moves: the clock worker sends it once no ghost has been asked for 220 ms, 5 s after the first segment held, or when a message other than a preview, a line query, demand's results, the clock or the next early segment comes; the next segment joins it (one state). Until then the main thread's world lacks the held segments: each is drawn as a stand-in (its ghost, or its edges from the answer when no ghost of it was answered), the blueprint total and undo button catch up at the rest, and the snap cannot see them, so the tool asks for the state at once when the pointer comes within 24 px of a held segment away from the start (a click there waits for it, at most 3 s), and when the run ends. A segment that paid (a junction off built track) is not held. notes/T-2404.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-2404): while a held follow-up waits, the hourly trips are sent without it (a blueprint changes no line's run), and the boarding account's steps wait while ghosts are asked, at most 3 s a round, then step to the end; a round's riders on the map can be up to 3 s later while drawing. notes/T-2404.md.
- 2026-11-06 (agent's call, T-1861): a pick or snap grid of 4,096 items or more is built when first wanted, not in idle time: on a big save a scan per pointer move costs about what the build does and left no idle time for it (every move on all of Japan scanned 750k segments for seconds). The first hover after a network is set can take one build, 50-150 ms on all of Japan, ~10 ms on New York. notes/T-2404.md.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2703, T-2705): a slot read or slot list that fails four times (100/400/1600 ms apart) throws a `StorageReadError`, so no caller takes it for an empty slot; at startup the game stops on the failure screen holding no slot. Storage the page may not use at all (no IndexedDB, a `SecurityError`) still reads as empty, since nothing can have been saved there.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2704): a demand worker erroring with no message before it posted anything is taken for a failed download and replaced through the panic path (reopening what it should hold) after 1 s and 4 s, counting as no panic. The clock worker (T-2710) instead holds every message until its worker's first message, `booted`, so a restart needs nothing rebuilt; the cost is one message's round trip before init.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2682): the edit note's like-with-like rule (T-2180) compares levels of detail only for zones with train trips on both sides, so a line into a zone new to the network is never dropped; a network with nothing running is a base at any moment, the game's load included; when a level did change mid-settle, a T-2626 estimate showing stays as the note (8 s) instead of nothing; edits wait up to 60 s for the zone in view's fine figures when `refiningZone` says they are coming.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2588): the save's network key leaves out the eras tail (trains changing over after an edit, drawn only, pruned as the clock runs), so a save with eras keys the same on every load; a cached result shown ahead is still checked against the network that arrives.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2702): units the plan state had lost go to the row holding their longest border within their first-level unit (islands with none to the row holding their ferry town: Zhoushan's Daishan and Shengsi to nnh, Changdao to lnk, Phu Quy to pha); units under 10,000 people join only rows rebuilt anyway, the rest left (Fernando de Noronha, Con Dao, Lakshadweep, five Korean islets). Chukotka (35,000) stays out: one zone of it would be under `ZONE_ALONE_MIN`. notes/T-2702.md.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2712): Colombia's rows take DANE's 2025 projections by municipio (COD-PS COL) rather than the 2018 census itself: the same base, seven years nearer GHSL's 2025, and on HDX beside COD-AB's polygons with the same codes. Only the four blob rows are rebuilt now; the rest stay on GHSL until rebuilt (T-2725).
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2585): the language picker stays the Settings row right under the Controls and account buttons, read as Anita's "top right under the first row"; no second picker. Japanese and Chinese ship with every key reviewed once by a separate agent and no native read.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2743): the crossings chip's red-brown means a bored tunnel is the cheapest build, since cut and cover costs the same as a viaduct (1.0x each, terrain added to both); below the break-even the tan thins (lower opacity) by how far the crossings fall short of it, rather than mixing toward white, so it fades into the tag in the dark theme too instead of turning bright grey. It judges the ghost's ground-level stretch with the whole route's mean terrain surcharge.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2729): an edit's solve shows progress in units of like work (a period of the local solve; a round of a period of the whole solve), the local solve's while its result is awaited and the whole one's after, and the unit in hand moves by its time against the stage's mean unit (to 90% of a unit). Weighing single population passes by their last time was tried and read 8-15% at the end: the weights the load left planned 94 ms for a local solve that took 40 s. A first unit with no measured mean slows toward 90% of itself (63% of the way in 4 s) rather than stopping. Work outside the units (setup, the results) is not counted; the bar stops at 97%.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2738): a deleted game's account copies are recorded as owed before anything is deleted locally and the browser hides owed games from the account's list, so a delete the server refuses still leaves the browser at once (with a note) and goes from the account at the next load, rather than keeping the slots until the server answers.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2734): the line cross section draws a level as 5 m (Anita's figure; the sim's `LEVEL_H` stays 8 m), stretches the whole line's heights to fill its strip (true scale would be under a pixel; the factor is in the hover), and its close-up has one fixed scale, 2 km across and 1 m a pixel up. Ground comes from the hillshade's Terrain Tiles with the sea floor read as sea level; other lines' track meeting the line at under about 12 degrees is not a crossing (the real saves' parallel lines weave over each other: 768 dots on the Tohoku Shinkansen before).
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2734, after Anita's review): the cross section drops its "heights drawn N times taller" lines and marks sea level with a "0 m" rule instead; water is drawn a nominal 4 m deep from its surface (the tiles give no river or sea bed, and the track's levels count from the surface, so a tunnel at -1 reads as under the water), from the zones' water masks every 20 m, a stretch's surface its lowest tile height, and where no mask covers, the tiles below 0 m read as sea.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2746): a bubble selection's longer trips are asked of the workers only after its far end's `flows` question is posted (a 1 s cap while a solve runs, 5 s otherwise), and not when the selection is gone by then: the far end the map draws goes first in each worker's queue, the panel's longer trips come a few ms later. notes/T-2746.md.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2638): the load bars are front-loaded by one curve on their shown fraction (power 1.7) rather than by refitting each stage's model: on measured loads the model ran ahead early in every stage mix, and one curve fixed both the North America and New York imports to within 10 points of the clock from 30% on. A short load (a new Tokyo game, 4.5 s) starts lower and jumps more; refitting the solve model (it underestimates a first load) is the deeper fix if the bar still drifts.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2756): a zone come back to shows its bubbles from its last visit's final legs (the last 6 zones left) while the result shown's are asked for, also when those are of an earlier result; the lie is a zone's bubbles one solve old for the seconds until the new ones come. Chosen because after a memory restart (T-1205) the pool answers nothing until the whole game is solved again, which left the zone bare for minutes. notes/T-2756.md.
- 2026-11-07 (agent's call, T-2773): while a line is pending ("calculating…"), the Lines tab shows its last settled riders, fares and profit, and the ledger is paid its last settled fare income (clockClient.ts `settledIncome`, game/today.ts `settled`); the line's own new answer replaces them when it lands. The lie is a line's figures one edit old for the seconds until then. Chosen over showing the half-done answer, which reads a long line near 0 (Anita's screenshot). Not reproduced on the NYC save (a headway edit there starts no solve); the Japan save has not been tried.
- 2026-11-08 (agent's call, T-2799): minimum curve radius 50 m, from 100 m. 50 m is about the tightest regular metro curves (New York subway 53 m, Central line 61 m, Washington 68.6 m; Chicago's elevated reaches 27 m and streetcars 10-15 m, too tight for this game's trains; figures from Wikipedia, Minimum railway curve radius). The speed rule is unchanged, so a 50 m curve runs at 27 km/h and tight curves stay slow. Routes snapped to a real line keep the old 100 m (`TRACE_MIN_RADIUS`), so a jagged source does not slow them and `realsave` rebuilds the same. A search for other games' limits (NIMBY Rails, Transport Fever, Subway Builder) found nothing usable.
- 2026-11-08 (agent's call, T-2797): a station's short end piece (station to bare end, at most 11 m past the platform) cannot be removed on its own, because a removal would only lay it back (T-1699 keeps track under a station). The delete tool takes the station there; the inspector and the Select tool's Remove are refused with a reason (`StationTrack`), replacing a "Removed 110 m of track" that changed nothing. Removing the station still leaves its track, one more click (T-2412). With no tool on, Delete or Backspace removes the selected track or station, and Delete with nothing selected picks the delete tool.
- 2026-11-08 (Anita, T-2787/T-2790/T-2810): this candidate keeps currencies, the update/changelog UI, tighter curves and bug fixes; line money history and trip-purpose graphs are held for later, preserved in c18d2fa1 and b167196a. Deployment waits for her playtest; the earlier publish-after-round permission is superseded.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2793): a solve of one zone alone (the focus pool, a zone refined aside) never replaces a line's figures once the line has any, its stops changed or not: the old figures stand, carried onto the new stops, until a result of the whole network. A line edited with stops changed therefore shows its old riders a little longer on the map and in the panels, instead of that zone's share of them (a 1,068 km Hikari read 2% of its fares). A new line with no figures still takes the zone's. Also a cluster's free-flow round estimates only that cluster's lines. notes/T-2793.md.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2873): the bus road graph joins roads by shape, since the tiles do not say which roads connect: a vertex within 2 tile units (about 1 m) of another road at its level, or two ground-level roads crossing. The lie: about 1% of the joins added have no OSM junction, so a bus could turn at a crossing that has none; 98-99% of real junctions are now in the graph, up from 51-72%. A motorway takes only another motorway's end, so a street end beside one stays off it. Stored routes keep their shape until a point beside them is moved. notes/T-2873.md.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2882): a bus may drive a plain service road (no driveway, alley or parking aisle tag, no access limit, ground level) at most 60 m long with each end on a street, and a click never lands on one. Service roads as a class stay out, so buses do not cut through car parks. Road ends are not bridged across footways by distance: College and Oxford St in Sydney stays unconnected, since a street closed to cars looks the same to such a rule. notes/T-2882.md.
2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2886): bubbles coloured by purpose use the mode colours' blue, red and green (work, errands and leisure, everything else), blended by share like the mode mix but with no pull to grey, since nearly every bubble holds all three (New York's local trips: work 20%, errands and leisure 70%, the rest 10%, so most bubbles read pink-red). A bubble gets one blended colour, not a split. The purposes' legs load only while the setting is on, and bubbles keep the mix or ramp chosen before until they are in. No-route trips are not greyed under it, and a selection's own and far-end bubbles stay in the mode colours.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2889): Portuguese is one Brazilian Portuguese under the plain code `pt` (Intl's `pt` is Brazilian; `pt-PT` browsers also get it), as Spanish is plain `es`. Buttons use the infinitive, sentences the imperative with você; boats are "balsa" everywhere (ferry term); glossary in notes/T-2889.md.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2890): terrain cells show as hexes, and track is charged by those hexes: a point takes the class of the old 1/960 degree cell under its hex's centre (no terrain data changed, `cost::hex_centre` and the tint share the lattice), so a hex that looks like hills is charged as hills, exact and not a border approximation (the display-only alternative, hexes over a square price, was wrong at every class border). Constructed track is re-derived at load like everything else, so a save's Built figure on the Money tab moves slightly (New York's real save: 769,563 to 769,549 US$M, -0.002%, 179 edges up and 178 down); no cash is paid or refunded for it. Keeping built track on the square grid was rejected: charges come from the change in the built total, so two samplers would charge phantom money on edits to track built since the change. The lie: a hex's class is one sample at its centre, so a thin feature between hex centres is not seen; the 3 x 3 majority filter upstream already drops those.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2889): Turkish (`tr`) addresses the player as `sen` in the imperative, boats are "vapur" and a ferry stop "iskele", and a placeholder never takes a case ending (labels, postpositions and compounds instead). The code lower-cases some translated words, so those Turkish words avoid I and İ rather than the code mapping by locale; `checks/i18n.mjs` folds the dot a lower-cased İ leaves. "In a straight line" keys are excepted from the "line" glossary check and say "kuş uçuşu".
- 2026-11-09 (agent busspeed, T-2904): a bus hop takes the car's time over its road at 90% of the car's speed, never under 30 km/h (a bus lane, `BUS_LANE_KMH`; so every dense centre has Seoul's median-lane speed, a choice Anita may turn down), plus 28 s a stop; replaces 70% of the car capped at 18 km/h. A stretch on a trunk road counts the car at 88 km/h whatever the density and hop (`CAR_TRUNK_KMH`, not the car's open highway 110); route points carry a saved `K_TRUNK` bit, old routes lack it until redrawn. The lie: the stop cost covers acceleration, so the drawn buses still run at constant speed between stands.
- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2889): Russian (`ru`) addresses the player as `вы` in the imperative, buttons use the infinitive, and a placeholder never takes a case or follows a case-taking preposition (labels, colons and a noun before the value instead); every count plural has one, few, many and other. Trips are "поездка", rides "проезд", ferries "паром", a station complex "узел". Nunito's Cyrillic subset is self-hosted (`public/fonts/Nunito-cyrillic.woff2`); glossary in notes/T-2889.md.

- 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2889): German (`de`) addresses the player as `du` in the imperative (casual rail and builder games do; `Sie` reads like a form), buttons use the infinitive, and a placeholder never takes an article, case ending or agreeing adjective. Trips are "Reisen", rides "Fahrten", riders "Fahrgäste", stations "Station". Code that lower-cases a translated word inside a sentence now goes through `lowerWord` (`i18n/index.ts`), which leaves German as written because its nouns keep their capitals; the demand level is dative ("bei hoher Nachfrage"). - 2026-11-09 (agent's call, T-2889): French (`fr`) addresses the player as `vous` in the imperative (French software and games of this kind do; `tu` reads as a children's game), buttons use the infinitive, and a placeholder never follows `de`, an article or an agreeing adjective (a label and colon or a fixed noun first, so elision and gender never depend on a name). - 2026-11-09 (Anita, agent's calls, T-2901): built track on land that had no terrain data reprices at its next load from the world's terrain blocks, no save level (as T-2890; the Built figure rises, no cash moves). A game opening waits at most 15 s for the blocks under it (a slow connection must not stop a game opening), the fetch set coming from what the track model priced (`take_terrain_wanted`) rather than a walk of the save before loading, so the load derives once and only reprices terrain. The terrain blocks are published once under a fixed R2 prefix (`terrain-world/v1/`, `deploy.py --terrain-world`), held in a build as hard links, and a release is refused while that prefix is not complete, since a page without them could build nothing anywhere. `index.bin` missing or failing counts as a failure like a block's, never as "no terrain anywhere".
- 2026-11-09 (Anita picked it, T-2936): Simplified Chinese gets Hanzi Round, which is Chill Round M (Zen Maru Gothic completed to GB2312, SIL OFL) cut into 12 woff2 slices, 1.5 MB in all; it replaces the system face and supersedes the 2026-11-06 line (T-2636) saying no rounded Simplified face exists to host. The font's Reserved Font Name forces a new family name in the files and CSS. Regular only, so zh-Hans has no bold hanzi and 16px headings, like zh-TW (T-2680). Resource Han Rounded CN (real bold, Simplified shapes, 1.4 MB per weight) is the backup; notes/T-2936.md.

This reverses T-2891's "By hand starts empty every time": the sim keeps a line's hand stops for the session (`BusRoute::hand_kept`, not saved) and choosing Manual again gives them back, those within 200 m of the route if it was redrawn between; the spacing before Manual is not remembered, Auto returns to 0.

A flat junction's crossing still weighs against the direction leaving the trunk (up to about 1.4:1 at 6 minutes); count lines without spare time keep one phase for both runs.

- 2026-11-10 (agent's calls, T-2952): a bus crossing is decided alike from both lines. Where one station is a stop of both within 200 m of the crossing along each, they change there and neither stops at the crossing; otherwise both stop at it, a line with its own stop within 50 m of it (a station's from the station's place) stopping there only. 50 m is half demand's 100 m station merge, so the two lines' stops are always one place; the old 200 m skip, per line, left one line at the crossing and the other at its station 212 m off. Two crossings of the same two lines within 200 m along both are one (the lower-numbered line's first), which keeps two routes sharing a street from a stop every bend. A line can now have a crossing stop 50-200 m from a station stop. A line with its own spacing or by hand keeps its stops and a line crossing it still stops at the crossing, so a hand stop placed there is shared; a spaced line may pass it.

- 2026-11-10 (agent's calls, T-2992): bus-to-bus transfers at a distance needed no demand change: demand already makes stops within 100 m (`station_merge_m`) one station, whoever placed them, and walks a change up to 300 m (`xfer_walk_m`); on a new game a Manual stop 23, 46 and 68 m from the other line's crossing stop and 106 m off all kept about the same transfers there (31-33 a day). What was missing was seeing and placing it, so the distance is demand's own 100 m (`BUS_XFER_M = DEMAND_MERGE_M`) rather than a new 60-80 m that would leave 80-100 m pairs joined but undrawn: such a pair gets the dashed transfer line (none between two stops on one spot), and Manual editing snaps a stop to where the route passes another line's stop within 100 m of it, the tag naming that line (stations first). A dashed line between a stop and a crossing stop on its own route runs along the route and barely shows; the snap avoids that case.

The look is the hover's (stations' hover tint; bus lines lighter and as wide as a selected one, up to 16 each), plus the built line's dashed transfer line from each station stop to its station, since a station's hover tint alone barely shows on a box; no new text.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's calls, T-3005): Color blind, the fourth Colors choice, is the 2-color ramp with fixed ends, Okabe-Ito blue #0072b2 at 100% train and a yellow #f2c200 at 0% (Wong 2011, "Points of view: Color blindness", Nature Methods; the set most used for colour-blind-safe figures). The set's own yellow #f0e442 had about 1.3:1 contrast on the light basemap, so it is deepened to #f2c200 (about 1.6:1 on the light land, 9.5:1 on the dark; blue is 4.9:1 on light, 3.1:1 on dark; luminance ratios against #fbf7f0 and #262120). The two differ in lightness as well as hue, so they stay apart under deuteranopia and protanopia (checked in a simulated deuteranopia view) and, by lightness, under tritanopia, which blurs blue against green. The mid shares come out a grey-olive, as in any blue-yellow ramp. It leaves the player's own 2-color ends alone (the stored rampFrom/rampTo), and the mode bars and swatches follow it (train blue, car yellow; walking stays green).

T-1894 had left those out of the count, so a line sharing a section with another could not add the shared stations in one go. Nothing is left out on purpose: the sim's passed list is already only stations on the line's own path that it does not stop at.
