A refusal still replaces it at once with the red straight and its reason, and the next spot keeps that red line until its own answer. The tag's figures are the old route's for that moment (well under a second); a spinner joins them past 300 ms.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's calls, T-2997): tram track's smallest curve is 18 m on the centre line (`TRAM_MIN_RADIUS`, was 25): every common modern low-floor tram takes it (Citadis and Skoda Astra 18 m, Combino 15, Brussels Flexity 14.5), while old street networks run 11-13 (Philadelphia 10.8, Toronto 11, Boston and San Francisco 12.8; Graebner, Jackson and Lovejoy 2007). Not lower: on double track the inner track is about 1.5 m tighter, and new systems build 18-25. The refusals she saw came from carrying on from a placed segment's end, not from the street fit: the lead-in along the end's heading was 0.3 of the way to the first street corner, too short for any legal curve. Now a street tram route from or to track with a heading has a lead-in long enough for an 18 m curve onto the street and drops up to 3 street corners by that end where the curve has no room before them, fewest first (the track there leaves the street and is priced off-street), and the street search carrying on from the route placed last never starts back the way it came (her U-turn). Measured: "curve too tight" on chained street segments 24 of 45 before, 0 of 45 after, fresh routes 0 both (notes/T-2921.md "Round 3").
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-2998): the streets a tram may follow are tinted green (#3f9a1c light, #86d65a dark, the bus hint's opacities) while the track tool has Tram with Snap to streets on; it is the bus hint's layer with motorways left out of its filter (`busRoadFilter(false)`), as the street search leaves them out. Ctrl held does not hide it.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3000): a street's bridge or tunnel end joins a ground street within the junction gap (about 1 m), as its end is on the ground; before, ends joined only roads at their own level, so a tunnel or bridge meeting a cross street at a T was cut off from it (two in central Pittsburgh). A motorway's ends keep their old rule. Middle vertices still join only roads at their level.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3001): the station tool's guide rings are 0.5 and 1 km (or miles) round a tram stop, 1 and 2 elsewhere.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3004): tram track, street and off-street, pays a quarter of the hills and mountains surcharge (`TRAM_TERRAIN` 0.25); water stays half on streets and whole off them. The kind table's one `surcharge_mult` became `terrain_mult` and `water_mult`.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3006): tram wires are white (#ffffff) in light and stay near white (#e6e4e0) in dark, a colour of their own (`tramWire`) rather than the map's ink. No dark edge: on the yellow and light blue lines they are faint but there, which reads as wires.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3028): the bus fare multiple takes the same 0 to 5x range as the high-speed and ferry ones, 0.05 steps, default 1.00. It scales only the part of the fare above the base, so 0x is a flat base fare; 0 is allowed since a long bus loses money well before it and the player sees it. Shown once a bus line exists.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3016, T-3035): the street snap searches on the next tick after each pointer move (`REST_MS` 60 to 0; the ghost update went from about 78 ms to about 22 ms median, notes/T-3016.md), and a street turn of 135 to 165 degrees is allowed at 200 m rising to 400 m, refused only past 165, so a tram takes a slow sharp corner rather than a block loop.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3034): the road graph joins a road end, and a short service line's end, to a street within 3 m (`JUNCTION_GAP_M`, in metres and converted per tile), where it was a fixed 2 units (0.6-1.2 m). Over 12 cities' 108 tiles that joined 206 more ends (roadgaps predicted about 90 counting spur tips; dense Tokyo tiles carry most) and kept 28 more links (predicted 24). Not higher: at 5 m about 7 in 10 extra joins tie a dead end to a road that OSM does not join.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3025): a turned line's row shows the outbound pass's time only (the stem is passed twice; the return times are the same stations later in the lap), and its boardings are summed over both passes. Rows do not drag: a drag cannot say which pass it moves. Related lines' side columns are not drawn for a turned line (its toggle is hidden); their bullets still show at each row.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3037): a region's rail share for the national and Europe achievements is train rides a day in its built zones over 3 rides a person times its zone populations (the world share's fixed-denominator rule), not the top bar's per-zone percent, which only covers zones the player has built. Thresholds are about twice what one strong metro reaches in the real saves (US 2, Korea 8, and so on); Japan 10 and Europe 5 are Anita's. Trans-Siberian's eastern end is Russia at or east of 104E (Irkutsk), Europe's name is Grand Tour. Notes: notes/T-3037.md.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3038): achievement counts are an anonymous count per achievement and a differently salted hash of the existing browser id, once per browser per achievement, no account or IP, forgotten after 395 days; the game shows "n% of players" in hovers only once 20 players are known. Notes: notes/T-3038.md.
- 2026-11-10 (Anita, T-3043): the real-rail layer is real track, not real lines: it draws every rail a line runs over, plus track carrying the name of a rail line (20 km or more) that no section covers, unless tagged freight. The second is a tile flag `x` from noritetsu's build_tiles (its own map still draws that track grey) instead of tail sections in cn_register, because a section needs a station at each end and the track has none.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3061): The device-memory half of T-1197's check no longer refuses; it warns and the start menu offers Load anyway (a slot or a picked file), since `navigator.deviceMemory` rounds down and stops at 8, so an 8 GB laptop reads as 4. The warning no longer states the device's memory, only what the save needs. The 3.5 GB worker cap still refuses with no override (past 4 GB the solve fails). In-game switches and backup restores skip the device check. Messages no longer say the game works best on a computer.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3023, T-3024): manual mode is its own saved flag on a line (`LineData::manual`, `LMN1` block, save level 14), not "hand ordered, one way and closed" read as manual, so older hand-ordered or turned lines keep the 12-an-hour reversal limiter and their U-turn icon. Switching back is refused unless the list reads the same both ways; a list with a skipped visit stays manual. The "+" is a button on each row (not a bar between rows, which doubled a 59-row list) opening a "Pick a station on the map" row; the next station click on the map puts the stop in after that row through `orderStops`. A manual line has no "stop looping" or "open the loop" marker and its first stop's marker is a plain stop; the loop marker (turn) stays as a shortcut. The train panel's "Towards" names the stop where the train next turns, not the list's last stop (always the first stop).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3072): the pool size a worker's running out of memory leaves is remembered for the tab's session (`sessionStorage`), not for the save or the browser: free memory depends on what else is open that day, so a stored low count would slow the game after the cause had gone, while a reload in the same tab (as "Play without intercity trips" does) would otherwise run out again first. No notice is shown for the fall (the figures are the same, only slower). No flat memory budget at load, so a reported 8 keeps its workers (Anita).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3079): switching Exact edits off always works. A list that reads the same both ways folds to its open line; any other list stays as it is, a normal line with those stops (a turned line keeps its loop). A manual line's route search turns back at a listed stop at no cost and takes the quickest closing hop, so a leg never runs round a loop to turn. Exact edits shows a plain numbered list in running order with no related-lines side drawing.
- 2026-11-10 (Anita, T-3083): picking a build tool (track, station, delete, select-blueprints, a new bus or ferry line by key or button) closes an open line, line group, bus stop, station, track, junction or train view; the city, world, trip, commuter and achievement views stay, since the demand views stay under build tools and the tutorial's bubble and trip steps read them. Kept: New line (selects the new line), Edit stops, Edit route (bus and ferry) and the station building tool, which are editing the thing that is open; the Select tool; the Escape and tool-toggled-off paths. The rule lives in `pickTool` and `newBusLine`/`newFerryLine` through `closeNetworkView` (game/ui.ts).
- 2026-11-10 (Anita, T-3082; the high-speed call is mine): rail track can be drawn down to 25 m radius (`MIN_RADIUS`, was 50), so a 25 m curve runs at 19 km/h by the existing `sqrt(1.1 R)` rule and the Chicago Loop's 27 m corners are buildable. High-speed track keeps 50 m (`HSR_MIN_RADIUS`, enforced in `geom::fit_min`, `trackKinds.ts`): a real line takes nothing like 25 m, and no save loses track it had. It is a one-constant change if she wants it lower or higher; the speed rule already makes a tight curve cost a train most of its speed. Snapped real lines stay at 100 m, tram at 18 m. Quad track needs a drawn curve of 33 m or more (the express pair is 8 m inside). Check: `checks/t3082-tight-curve.mjs`.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3089, T-3080): in Exact edits the row's cross, a click on the stop's dot on the map and a click on its map number are one edit, `dropStop` for one visit. A click on a stop with two visits takes out the first (a second click the next), so one direction can go from the map; before, it took out both. Dropping the first or last row removes the cycle's first stop, so the next stop becomes the first; dropping a turning visit shortens the line. An empty circle sits on the right of each pass of the route over a station it has no stop at, and a click puts the stop in after the stop before that pass (the route already runs there); a direction the route never passes gets no circle. Numbers show from zoom 12.5; left-hand running will flip the side (`side` in `placeVisits`). Check: `checks/t3023-manual.mjs`.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3094): one-way single track is only useful if a line's return run can take a different way, so `Network::back_path_of` routes the stops in reverse order whenever the line's path run backward would go against a one-way piece (`build_run` builds run 1 from it; a loop that runs one way has no return run and keeps its path as the only way). The design note had every line's return run as its path reversed, which would have made a one-way piece unusable by any line that goes both ways. The return way is derived, never stored, not listed in `lines_on_edge`, so `Network::back_lines` has those lines rechecked and rebuilt after every edit (a scan of the paths; empty while no track is one way). Known gap: the edge panels and removal prompts do not list a line over track only its return run uses (T-3101).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3094): setting a piece one way moves only the lines that must move: a line whose forward path ran against it takes `route_hops_turn` through the same stops, and one whose return run must change takes its way back automatically; a line already on a legal way keeps it, even where the direct way has become legal again after a Reverse (Ring B stayed on the long way in the check). The flag is not set on double, high-speed, tram or crossover track and is cleared when a piece stops being single, so Double up on single needs no refusal. Splits keep the flag on both halves; a join takes only pieces with the same way along the joined edge. The app reads the flags through `oneway_edges()` (pairs) rather than a new column in `edges_info`, so the stride-10 readers in `clock.worker.ts` stay as they are. The edit is refused naming the line (`OneWayNoWay:<line>`) when a line has no way, so the player never meets a half-applied change; the confirmation lists the lines whose way changes ("{line} takes another way", via the `oneWay` query).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3106): every `StreetTram` edge may sit at ground level over water, not only the stretches where the street is a bridge, since the sim holds the street as a plain line with no bridge flags; the half water surcharge (T-2928) stays, as it prices the bridge the street already has. Off-street tram, hand-shaped street track and rail keep the rule.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3092): a real flyover's crossing leg runs straight on along J3's tangent back toward J1, crosses the main ahead of J1 and comes back in to J2 behind it, in place of notes/T-3085.md's leg 12 m beside the main crossing behind J1: a leg starting between the plain leg and the main cannot get behind J1 without crossing the plain leg, and a ramp holds no corner. A 30-degree branch gets J3 550 m out and a flyover about 1.1 km along the main and 100 m off it; notes/T-3085.md "As built: T-3103 and T-3092".
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3092): J3, where a flyover's double branch splits into its two one-way legs, is priced as a junction (US$9M at grade), as every node with three track ends is; one rule over a special case for a split with no real switch.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3092): with the ramp switch on, a junction with no room for a flyover is built flat with "No room for a flyover here" in the drawing tag; no note where the route is refused anyway, or where the route or the track it leaves is not double rail (single, quad, high-speed, tram stay flat silently, as the first cut leaves them out).
- 2026-11-10 (Anita's ask, T-3112): `SINGLE_TRACK_MARGIN_S` goes from 60 to 240 s, so two-way single track holds each train for its run time plus 4 minutes, both directions together (about 10 minutes headway each way over 1 km, 12 over 2 km, 14 over 5 km, 20 over 10 km). Her words: "10/h is 6 minute headways. thats like really high. for a double track without any terminals, we're already capped at 4 minute headways, so i think a single track should be at least 10m headways. maybe 15m. it just feels unrealistic cuz in the real world there aren't really single track metros ... it would be immersion breaking for people to just be building their small city metros as mostly single track. maybe we could just decrease the tolerance." One-way single track keeps double track's rule; the constant is used only for that section hold.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3109): while stops are edited a right click on a station with no loop to close, open or turn round (a stop with no offer, or no stop) changes nothing and the tag says "Can't loop back here" under the name for 4 s. A click on a stop takes out every visit of it; a loop losing its first stop closes again on the next. Exact edits and manual mode are gone, so the free turn at a listed stop went with them (T-3100 moot); saves' `LMN1` lines fold on load when they read the same both ways (notes/T-3109.md).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3109, T-3063): in the bus route tool a click on a point deletes it and a right click loops, as for trains; two exceptions kept: a click on the active end (or where a loop closes) still finishes, since a double click to finish would otherwise eat the last point, and a right click on the active end deletes it, having no loop meaning. Clicking the other end of a two-point route no longer switches the active end. A bus loops back from its active end to the point right clicked; the sim keeps it as a loop with its way out run backward at the end (`bus::loop_back_at`) rather than a new route field, so service, demand and saves needed no change beyond stops on both passes. Ferries keep their left click to close a loop (not asked).
- 2026-11-10: Country leaderboards (`country_<cc>`) sum a game's per-city points over the country and rank each person by their best game; the server gets city to country from an embedded table (`trainworld_countries.json`, written by `tools/country_table.mjs`) because ids carry no country, so the client sends nothing new. A city missing from the table counts for no country (T-3116).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3118): a right click on any stop, point or port of a loop opens it there. A circle (train, tram, bus ring or ferry ring) that calls at each place once ends at the one clicked: the stretch leaving it goes, so in a route tool the clicked point is the active end to draw on from. A train line turned round a loop, and a bus or ferry route that loops back, cannot end at an arbitrary point of the loop without dropping some of it, so any of their points stops the looping instead (the way back, or the closing leg, goes). On touch a Loop button makes the next tap a right click; a ferry port has no hold (taps place ports at once).
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3117): a flyover's crossing leg leaves J3 along the route for 40 m and eases 12 m toward the main by 160 m out, so the legs never share a line; on the map a one-way single end meeting double track at a junction is drawn 2 m to its own track's side, easing back over 60 m (drawing only, trains still run the centre line, at most 2 m off).
- 2026-11-10 (T-3125, from T-3119): Anita chose three margins. Trams hold a section 60 s + v / 0.6 (was 40 s, about 41 an hour at 60 km/h), a platform dwell + 60 s (was 30 s, 48 an hour), a crossing or junction 45 s (was 30 s). - 2026-11-10 (T-3125): trains' junction move and flat crossing hold 120 s instead of 90 s (`CONFLICT_S`, 30 an hour per conflicting pair instead of 40), landing with flyovers. - 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3123): a bus loop with stops by hand keeps at least one stop between its ends. The first stop placed makes the stop it had at half its length a placed one (it stays, never jumps away), and the last stop between cannot be taken out (no tag, no click). The other choice, letting the half-way stop come back when the last placed one goes, moved a stop the player never placed.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3130): a street route's curve cut out as tram track (it leaves the street, T-2927) is street track where it lies at ground level over water, since it rounds the corner of a street on its bridge (T-3106); as tram track it was refused there (GroundOverWater), so a street tram onto or off a bridge with a sharp corner could not be built (Bilbao's Nervión). The station and delete tools, and the track tool before its first click, still clear.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3132): Community's region filter uses the game's realms (game/realms.json), the only grouping above a country or within one that players see named, leaving out a realm that is exactly one country. Share again from Community on a game not being played opens that game first, then the Share dialog, rather than laying out a game that is not running.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3122): a loop line stops at every pass of a station it stops at, except a pass straight before or after a stop at the same station (Anita's rule, "stations between" read as stops between: a terminal whose loop has only stations the line passes still stops once). - 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3122): a click that puts a station at a line's end turns every end whose stop stands partway round a loop of track (the loop rejoins the path before that stop) round the loop, one way, as the right click would; an end before a loop with no stop of the line stays the right click's, an end already on a loop before the click is left open (one the player opened), hand-ordered lines never turn by themselves, and a ring (loop rejoining at the first stop) stays open until closed. notes/T-3122.md.
- 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3122): which way a line serves a stop comes from the sim alone (`line_stop_ways`, by which side of the station its run passes it), and the map's half dots, the stop list's half dots and the hover chevrons all read it. - 2026-11-10 (agent's call, T-3137 C1): the move table holds, per arriving edge end, a bit per end a train may leave by, not lane pairs: a path entry fixes its lane (double track's direction is its lane), so every lane move is one end pair, and C2's joins clear bits. Turning back at a bare track end stays outside the table, as turning at stations does.

- 2026-11-11 (agent ubc, T-3216, T-3226): tier 0 universities: a campus item with no Wikidata count takes its counted institution's (P361, P749, P466 occupant, P137 operator; the smallest), whole; sites named as open or distance universities or research centres are left out; a count is cut to 14x the counted campuses' curve (1,872 x ha^0.31) and 100,000 and caps its sites after the national scaling, an area-sized site at 5.4x the curve and 50,000; students no site can take study near home. - 2026-11-11 (agent meet, T-3202): on screen, trains of opposite directions take two-way single track in turn (one stands at its stop until the other is in, about a run time late), and at a station on single track each is drawn on its own side as on double track, a line's end there having two places; drawing only, the timetable still sends both at once (the real fix is T-3219). The lie: until T-3212 draws the track split too, trains at a single-track station stand half beside the one drawn track.
- 2026-11-11 (Anita, T-3240): demand factors need not be worldwide any more ("now that the game is actually like having a fair bit of traffic, we can loosen our rule that demand factors should be universal"); a local factor is fine where published figures show a gap, and the US intercity, which fits, can stay as it is. Big cities (and small capitals) get more pull for long trips; the GDP-per-head travel exponent (0.7, never fitted) gets a crude fit. Prepared now, applied with the next world rebuild.
- 2026-11-11 (Anita, T-3241): a level rebuild that ends at a station takes the station to the new level and puts the ramp on the track beyond it, outside the pick ("we should move the stations and ramp the adjacent segments"), charged as built new over the stretch that changes.
- 2026-11-11 (agent rebuildstn, T-3241, notes/T-3241.md): details of that rule. Every station on the picked track moves, picked or not (before, a station left out of the pick refused the pieces at it). The ramp starts 2 m past the platform, held by two new no-turn vertices on a straight (high-speed track's at its design radius, so no curve limit) or the corners up to one, whichever changes less; too short a next piece moves whole and the ramp runs on through plain joints, up to 8 pieces. Track on to another moving station or back into the pick moves whole (no ramps down and up between two moved stations); a short stub to a loose end moves whole; a dead-end station just moves. No room (a junction or a staying station too close) keeps the station, and the picked piece ramps inside itself as before or is refused with "Not enough room to change level". Not with a change of family. The stretch beyond is drawn in the pick's band and counted in the hover's length and the price.

Checked: wasm and market rows HIT, a .bin Range read answers 206 on MISS and HIT.

EOX's 2018+ mosaics are CC BY-NC-SA (we are free, no ads); an email to EOX about volume is still open.
- 2026-11-11 (agent pull, T-3240, notes/T-3240.md 6): the long-trip pull as built: Natural Earth 10m populated-places scalerank against a rank-for-people line (20.80 - 1.08 ln people) is the measure; remote centres trimmed by a rule (the cap of 3 shrinks on a log scale from 5M people within 300 km to none at 0.5M) plus Leh by hand; across a border gamma 0.25 (FR24 airport pairs: the slope of air passengers over the model on excess matches the domestic pairs' at 0.2-0.3), so Warsaw draws about 8.7x at home and 2x from abroad; China's gamma 0.6 of 0.8, read as her "20% down" on China's 300 km+ trips with the pull (0.0105 to 0.0084 a person a weekday); resort towns from Wikidata and Wikipedia's seaside-resort categories give their place an excess of at least 1.5 x their people's share over 0.1. The US, Japan, the UK and Korea stay exempt as origins; their places still pull visitors from abroad.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3267): over satellite the Base map slider is the imagery's opacity over the theme's background ("like satellite map being 10% opacity over a light/whiteish background"), not a dimming. The game's marks use the dark theme's colours while the imagery shows at 50% or more and the panels' theme below that (judged at 10%, 40%, 100% in both themes: light ink on a faded light image washes out, dark ink on strong imagery reads best); the flip is `SAT_INK_FLIP` 0.5 in game/ui.ts. Labels and roads flip with it, labels never fade with the slider.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3254): demand is fitted at or a bit under published figures, never over ("err on the side of undershooting ... overshooting then correcting afterward would feel bad for most people"); where sources disagree and it is unclear which is right, the lower one.
- 2026-11-12 (agent pull2, T-3254, notes/T-3240.md 7): the long-trip pull refitted under that rule: gamma 0.6 (was 0.8), faded in over the tail's 300-700 km (was 50-150), so pairs under 300 km keep their no-pull trips; no damping by the origin's own pull (fitted to 0: Berlin - Hamburg and Berlin - Munich have the same two pulled ends and only distance separates them); Russia's and Turkey's residents exempt (300 km+ already over the published without it); China's factor stays 0.6 (her "half the change", 0.8, puts Beijing - Shanghai 24% over its 50k); 0.25 across a border kept (the lower of FR24's 0.25-0.34); Magong set to 0 by hand, Geneva kept. Binding: France's 300 km+ (EMP 2019) and Madrid - Barcelona (CNMC 2024).
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3254; agent pull2's value): China at 0.6 confirmed; a small Poland-only pull ("maybe do a very slight poland-only pull, but keep it pretty small. we dont need to match exactly"), built as `near_gamma` PL 0.2: within Poland a destination's attraction is also times 1 + expm1(0.2 x excess) at every distance, unfaded, so Warsaw - Kraków goes 3.6k -> 5.1k a weekday both ways (under the 7.2k by rail alone, H1 2019) and Poland's 300 km+ stays at 0.0025 (ceiling 0.003). notes/T-3240.md 7.8.
- 2026-11-12 (agent hexterrain, Anita's ask, T-3266, reverses T-2890): terrain is drawn and charged by square grid cells again (1/960 degree), not hexes. The class data only changes every 4 cells (1/240 degree, about 460 m, squares), so 110 m hexes showed blocks of same-class hexes over visible squares and promised detail the data hasn't. Pricing went back with the drawing so what is shown is what is charged (it keeps one sampler, and track built since T-2890 reprices at the next load like then, no cash moves); T-2890's reason against the square grid for built track was two samplers at once, which doesn't arise. Quote change on 179 seeded routes in three areas: see notes/T-3266.md.
- 2026-11-12 (agent commview, Anita's TODO, T-3257, reverses T-3115's open-as-copy): Community's Open a copy is now View. A viewed network is a game in no slot: nothing autosaves it, it is not on the account and counts nowhere (it is still a copy); it plays and edits as any game. The saves panel shows it as its own card with only Download and Save; Save (also Ctrl+S and the top bar notice) makes it a saved game named "<title> - <name>", and its play time counts from then. Anything that replaces it (another game, a file, a backup, New game, another View, the main menu) asks first, Leave without saving or Stay; closing or reloading the tab asks only once something was changed, as for any unsaved change, since an untouched view is still on Community.
- 2026-11-12 (agent commview, Anita's TODO, T-3258): Community's Most liked orders equal likes at random by the list's seed (shonei-server), the one the Random tab uses: the panel keeps one seed while open, so a refresh after a like keeps the order and reopening deals again.
- 2026-11-12 (agent commview, Anita's TODO, T-3259): the 60 minutes played to share, like or report are waived for accounts listed in shonei.env's SHONEI_COMMUNITY_NO_WAIT (ids or logins, like the old testers list), so the list changes with no code change; Anita's own account is the only one on it.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3243): a Cloudflare cache rule "noritetsu data" (data.anita.garden/noritetsu/data/*, edge TTL 2 h ignoring the files' no-cache, after "data bypass") moves noritetsu's pmtiles reads (most of R2's ~800k GetObject an hour) off R2. Noritetsu's file names don't change between builds, so a rebuild can show its old map for up to 2 h; pmtiles' etag check refetches on a change. Added via the ops token's API; Range reads answer 206 on MISS and HIT.
- 2026-11-12 (agent airports, T-2896 (1)-(2), under Anita's undershoot rule of 2026-11-12): an airport's passengers are a national authority's published count where `pipeline/airport_passengers.csv` has one (China: CAAC's 2025 bulletin, all 266 airports with traffic), else Wikidata's patronage taking whole years only (no month or part-of-traffic statements: Hong Kong had a December figure), the newest year outside 2020-22 from 2016 on, else the newest; the newer of the two wins, one year in both takes the lower. Shanghai's own 2024 press figures gave way to the table. ACI is paid; Eurostat's avia_paoa is the next national-style source (EU), not used yet.
- 2026-11-12 (agent airports, T-2896 (3)): airport terminals snap to a cell within 3 km (was 1 km; Shijiazhuang's is 1,423 m off), a terminal that does not place gives its share to the others, and an airport placing nothing is a WARNING in the build log.
- 2026-11-12 (agent airports, T-2896 (4)): attractions leave out subclasses of railway station, metro station and airport too, unless the item is a museum (Hong Kong Railway Museum stays). Subclasses of every NOT class were tried and dropped real places by the hundred (national monuments under protected area, ancient cities under human settlement), so only those three inherit. Moscow Kiyevsky and Niamtougou airport leave the file.
- 2026-11-12 (agent airports, T-2896 (5), Anita 2026-11-11 "sure we can do airport trips cross zone lines"): the pack keeps its GHSL share of an airport's outings; the rest goes to neighbouring packs through cross_everyday, each cell weighed as the everyday layer weighs an airport's ends x exp(-d / 20 km), scaled by the GHSL weight of the people outside the pack, so unbuilt land's share is dropped rather than handed to built neighbours (never over the published figure). The lie: the outside people's weight is GHSL population only while the cells' weight mixes jobs and leisure in, so a job-heavy neighbour can take a little more than its people's share, capped at the outside total.
- 2026-11-12 (agent airports, orchestrator's ask, T-2896, undershoot rule): tier 0 airports' O&D share is a city's config, else a hub table of 1 minus the published transfer share rounded down (Istanbul 0.5, Frankfurt 0.5, Munich 0.55, Amsterdam 0.6, Doha 0.25, Dubai 0.5, Singapore 0.7 by judgement), else DB1B for US airports as the hand-built US packs have it (Atlanta 0.45), else 1. Istanbul takes 0.5, not the suggested 0.7, because about half its passengers connect.
- 2026-11-12 (agent dbltrack, T-3296, Anita's TODO "add ability to rebuild single into double track"): single track is rebuilt as double through the Select tool's bar (Double track, then Rebuild for built; at once and free for blueprint), at the full double price with nothing back for the single, as every rebuild; undo only for blueprint (a paid edit is final). Stations on it become double-track stations and are charged as such. Single track that runs one way, and high-speed single (flyover legs), stay single. The doubled stretch meets single track in a plain joint, no join given. No double-to-single rebuild: it would lose paid-for capacity, and passing tracks, quad pairs and joins hang on double track.
- 2026-11-12 (agent jpattract, T-3280, Anita "for japan tourism, i think A makes sense. lets do it"): 79 Japanese resort towns' published visitors a year (pipeline/market_tourism.csv, each cited) join the long-trip ground attraction of their sites, 4 people for each visit a day, fitted under Hakone's and Atami's rail counts (Hakone 1,090 -> 9,144 a weekday each way, Atami 1,746 -> 3,498); a table of its own beside market_pull.json since it is a count at 6 km zone level at every distance, not a place multiplier. The lie: trips from abroad see the towns but their zone files are not rebuilt, so their departures run up to 0.06% over (Jeju) until their next rebuild.
- 2026-11-12 (agent transfer, T-3295, player report via Anita): after a walk to another stop of the line they left (within 300 m, or one complex through a link or passage), riders may board that line again away from where they rode, either way; barred are its departures whose next stop is the stop they walked from or behind it along their ride (a circle's the nearer way round). Before, a walk barred the whole line (T-1268 kept T-575's guard whole for walks), so a line bending back near itself could not be changed onto across the bend, passage or not. Walking to the next stop and riding on is allowed (a long bend with no stop on it); walking to the next or last stop to ride back stays barred.
- 2026-11-12 (agent dbl2, T-3321, Anita on T-3296: "i guess maybe we shuold also allow rebuilding double track to single track, maybe that direction can be free. the buttons to do double track or single track should be same as for placing new track, just the little 1 ilne or 2 lines"): replaces T-3296's "no double-to-single rebuild". Double regular track rebuilds as single from the Select tool's bar, through the track tool's own 1-line / 2-line icons (they replace T-3296's "Double track" button): free for built track (nothing charged, nothing refunded, final as every built rebuild), at once, free and undoable for blueprint; with a level or type change in the same rebuild that change is paid at the single track's price. Stations on it become single-track stations, uncharged. What needs two tracks is refused with its reason, never removed: high-speed track (unless made Regular in the same rebuild), tram track, quad track, a crossover at either end, a flying junction, a one-way single piece joined to one of its two tracks (a flyover's leg), a station with passing tracks left with single track only. Safe cases taken: a two-way single piece joined to it keeps its stored join (ignored while the track is single, counting again if it is made double again); a crossover between a double piece's own two tracks cannot exist in the track model, so there is nothing to remove. Running trains: no new rule, the rebuild's own (lines keep running over it, the timetable takes the shared section at once). A piece refused for its track count now keeps its level and type too, in both directions.
- 2026-11-12 (agent names, T-3318, feedback): a multi-prefecture metro takes the region's name in ja and zh, not its biggest city's (Keihanshin is 関西, not 大阪: Hyogo's players read 大阪 as another prefecture); zones get Wikidata labels like cities, China's and Taiwan's zh labels are kept only when their pinyin reads as the shown name (a wrong Chinese name is worse than the Latin one; T-3324).
- 2026-11-12 (agent dbl3, T-3339, Anita on T-3321's refusals: "quad tracck is just 2 double tracks right? so making quad single shoul d just make it 2 singles? ... i think we should allow it" and "why do passing tracks for single tracks nto make sense?"; orchestrator's calls): T-3321's refusal list loses quad and passing tracks. Quad: each picked pair goes single on its own (whole quad becomes two singles side by side; one pair leaves single beside double; a crossover between the pairs still refuses). Passing tracks: the station stays a double-track passing place, never refused: each picked piece at it keeps double track over the platform's reach plus 20 m (`PASSING_RUN_OUT`, as a junction keeps room past a platform), split there in the same free edit, and goes single beyond; a piece too short for that, one that will not split there (a ramp), or one whose level or type changes in the same rebuild stays double whole. The refusals left: high-speed (unless made Regular too), tram, a crossover, a flying junction, a one-way single piece joined to one of its two tracks.
- 2026-11-12 (agent t3345, T-3345, Anita: "ok lets do the city-turn schematic fix"): on a country map (600 km across or more) the diagram's rubber sheet holds each city's turn (weight 16 on land near lines, the sheet's own turn measured from the hops); city maps keep the sheet exactly as before, so their look does not move. All Japan changes shape (Tohoku more upright, Kanto no longer turned 8 degrees), which Anita liked.
- 2026-11-12 (agent hsr, T-3352, Anita's TODO "should allow single track for high speed (needed for manual flyovers!)" and "should probably also allow single track for tram"; the orchestrator's calls on the scope's six questions): single track goes with every track type. Single high-speed and single tram are 0.6x the kind's price and upkeep (single high-speed 0.9x double rail), which also fixes the auto flyover legs, priced as regular single until now against T-3170's decision; single street tram is allowed (one track down the street); two-way single high-speed is allowed; the Select bar retypes single track between regular, high-speed and tram, and doubles and singles them keeping the type; a tram stop on single track is one platform with no passing loop (drawn and run); One way is allowed on single tram and single high-speed. Still refused: a crossover of either kind. The old refusals ("High-speed track can't be single track.", "Tram track can't be single track.") were only the first slices' simplicity (T-817, T-2921), so they go with their strings. No save change.
- 2026-11-12 (Anita, T-3337): high speed stays 320 km/h ("keep to 320. dont wanna change this"). It was built at 350 as an orchestrator's call on a player's ask and taken out before landing; a game change a player asks for waits for her word (CLAUDE.md).
- 2026-11-12 (Anita's ask, agent's method, T-3372, reverses T-3266's squares): terrain classes follow the ground at the 115 m cells, and are drawn and charged by hex again (T-2890's lattice, 1/768 degree). The tower cap's coarse slope (1/240 degree, unchanged thresholds) is taken bilinearly at each cell's centre instead of copied onto 4 x 4 blocks, so its borders are no longer 460 m squares; the slope is interpolated rather than the elevation, whose bilinear gradient would jump at coarse cell edges. Tokyo, Nagoya and Shanghai centres stay flat (99.3 / 95.5 / 100%), the zone mix moves under a point a class, quotes rise 0.7-1.9% in the Tyrol, Garda and Chongqing and 7.5% on the Ligurian coast, where flat squares the old cap left on steep valley sides go (notes/T-3372.md). The blocks are v2 (data/terrain-world-v2, R2 terrain-world/v2/); built track reprices at load, no cash moves. The lie: the cap is still a 920 m slope, so a steep feature narrower than that inside gentle ground is charged at most the gentle ground's class, now with a smooth border.

Jurong Island (3.65 km2 built) and Sentosa (0.65 km2) have no residents, so sin's outline left them dark though both were in its units; with the rule they come in, and so do Pulau Bukom's refinery islands, while Singapore's empty islets stay out (Southern Group 0.02 km2, Changi Bay's reclaimed strips 0.11).
